<a id="t1_truth_inference"></a>
## Truth inference, label aggregation and latent-class models

- **[balasubramanian2026dependence]** Krishnakumar Balasubramanian, Aleksandr Podkopaev, Shiva Prasad Kasiviswanathan (2026). *Dependence-Aware Label Aggregation for LLM-as-a-Judge via Ising Models*. arXiv (preprint). [link](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.22336)
  - Relevance **high**; role: component prior art; components: family effects/correlated errors, anchored cell model, identifiability
  - Studies aggregation of binary judgments from K dependent LLM judges with a hierarchy of Ising and latent-factor models. Independence-based methods (Dawid-Skene, weighted majority) can flip the Bayes label and stay strictly suboptimal as K grows. The dependence-aware method improves on three real datasets.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* It formally shows the risk SpecDx's cross-family independence assumption carries, and offers a latent-factor correction that could serve as a family-effect extension of the anchored cell model.
- **[he2026experts]** Zeyu He, Zhuqian Zhou, Kirk Vanacore et al. (2026). *Experts Rise Where LLMs Disagree: Using Cross-Model Disagreement to Target Expert Effort in LLM Codebook Revision for Large-Scale Annotation*. arXiv (preprint). [link](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.26926)
  - Relevance **high**; role: direct competitor; components: baseline B4, defect taxonomy, active synthesis
  - Applies an early annotation codebook with several LLMs, surfaces strong cross-LLM disagreement, and compares three ways of eliciting expert feedback on these cases (verifying LLM-generated codebook revisions, answering questions, labelling with rationales) on tutoring transcripts. Rationale labelling gives the best LLM labelling accuracy and cuts codebook revision from months to days.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* It uses disagreement across an LLM population to locate defects in a natural-language specification (the codebook), which partly overlaps SpecDx's claim. It relies on human experts, has no rater model, defect typing, executable checks or witnesses, whereas SpecDx is fully automatic and logic-backed.
- **[ron2026version]** Javier Ron, Benoit Baudry, Martin Monperrus (2026). *N-Version Programming with Coding Agents*. arXiv (preprint). [link](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.20158)
  - Relevance **high**; role: component prior art; components: family effects/correlated errors, defect taxonomy
  - Repeats the Knight-Leveson experiment with 48 implementations from AI coding agents of the Launch Interceptor specification, tested on a million random inputs. It finds substantial common-mode failure, much of it traceable to where the specification is hard or ambiguous, yet majority-voted triples still reduce failures sharply.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* It is direct evidence that coincident failures of an LLM-agent population localise specification difficulty or ambiguity, which is SpecDx's core signal. It reports the phenomenon but offers no method to separate spec defects from agent errors or to type them.
- **[nakamura2026observational]** Kentaro Nakamura, Jing Ling Tan, George Yean (2026). *Observational Equivalence of LLM and Human Annotation*. arXiv (preprint). [link](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.22133)
  - Relevance **high**; role: direct competitor; components: baseline B4, defect taxonomy, set-valued truth
  - Replicates 14 political-science text-classification studies with ten LLMs, three experts and 165 crowd workers. LLMs are observationally equivalent to experts, and the equivalence is driven by ambiguity in texts and coding rules: where LLMs disagree with experts, experts also disagree, and clarifying rules reduces both. Proposes using cross-LLM disagreement to find difficult cases and refine codebooks, plus ambiguity-aware bounds.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* It is empirical support for SpecDx's premise that cross-model disagreement concentrates on ambiguous rules, and it proposes refining the codebook (spec) from that disagreement. It is not a typed or localised diagnosis and has no anchor model or logic, so it overlaps SpecDx's ambiguity component only.
- **[zhang2025quantifying]** Yichi Zhang, Ignacio Martinez (2025). *Quantifying and Correcting Measurement Error in LLM-Generated Classifications: A Beta-Binomial Latent Class Model for Correlated Repeated Judgments*. arXiv (preprint). [link](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.23874)
  - Relevance **high**; role: component prior art; components: family effects/correlated errors, identifiability, anchored cell model
  - Using 52,500 judgments from seven open-weight LLMs, shows that repeated samples of the same model on the same item are strongly correlated (ICC 0.50 to 0.93), so binomial latent class models are miscalibrated and biased. Proposes a beta-binomial latent class model with one correlation parameter and proves its identifiability.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* SpecDx draws several runs per model per cell. This paper shows that such runs are not independent readings and gives the beta-binomial fix, which SpecDx's likelihood should adopt or test against.
- **[guerdan2025validating]** Luke Guerdan, Solon Barocas, Kenneth Holstein et al. (2025). *Validating LLM-as-a-Judge Systems under Rating Indeterminacy*. arXiv (preprint). [link](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.05965)
  - Relevance **high**; role: component prior art; components: set-valued truth, defect taxonomy
  - Introduces 'rating indeterminacy', where rating criteria admit several valid interpretations so a rater may deem multiple ratings correct. Gives a framework for validating LLM judges under it and shows that forced-choice elicitation and aggregation can heavily bias judge validation (NeurIPS 2025 per the arXiv comment).
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Rating indeterminacy is SpecDx's ambiguity defect (A_c = {x, y}) viewed from the evaluation side. It supports eliciting or modelling set-valued truth rather than forcing one label.
- **[ustalov2024learning]** Dmitry Ustalov, Nikita Pavlichenko, Boris Tseitlin (2024). *Learning from Crowds with Crowd-Kit*. JOSS (peer-reviewed). [link](https://doi.org/10.21105/joss.06227) · [code](https://github.com/Toloka/crowd-kit) ★255 · **baseline candidate**
  - Relevance **high**; role: evaluation/benchmark; components: baseline B2, baseline B3, anchored cell model
  - Software paper for Crowd-Kit, a Python library implementing common aggregation methods for crowdsourced categorical, multi-label, pairwise, textual and segmentation responses, plus quality metrics and dataset loaders (from the repository README and JOSS metadata).
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* It is the most practical offline implementation of the classic truth-inference baselines SpecDx must beat in simulation and on real matrices.
- **[jeong2023recovering]** Hyeonsu Jeong, Hye Won Chung (2023). *Recovering Top-Two Answers and Confusion Probability in Multi-Choice Crowdsourcing*. ICML (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://proceedings.mlr.press/v202/jeong23a.html) · **baseline candidate**
  - Relevance **high**; role: component prior art; components: set-valued truth, item difficulty/IRT
  - Models multi-choice crowdsourcing tasks as having two plausible answers, with task difficulty as the confusion probability between them and worker reliability as the probability of answering within the top two. A two-stage algorithm recovers the truth, the most confusing answer and the confusion probability at a minimax-optimal rate.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* It is the closest crowdsourcing model to SpecDx's two-valued entailment set {x, y}: both posit items with two plausible answers. Jeong and Chung still assume one is the ground truth, whereas SpecDx treats both as entailed (ambiguity), adds the empty set and an anchor, and maps outcomes to spec-defect types.
- **[yu2022learning]** Peilin Yu, Tiffany Ding, Stephen H. Bach (2022). *Learning from Multiple Noisy Partial Labelers*. AISTATS (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A). [link](https://proceedings.mlr.press/v151/yu22c.html) · [code](https://github.com/BatsResearch/nplm) ★16
  - Relevance **high**; role: component prior art; components: set-valued truth, identifiability
  - Extends programmatic weak supervision to partial labelers that output subsets of classes, with a generative model that estimates their accuracies without ground truth, a scalable learning procedure, and a proof of generic identifiability up to label swapping.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* It handles set-valued rater outputs with identifiability, the mirror image of SpecDx's set-valued truth (sets on the latent side, not the observation side). It is useful for identifiability arguments.
- **[fu2020fast]** Daniel Fu, Mayee Chen, Frederic Sala et al. (2020). *Fast and Three-rious: Speeding Up Weak Supervision with Triplet Methods*. ICML (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://proceedings.mlr.press/v119/fu20a.html) · [code](https://github.com/HazyResearch/flyingsquid) ★315 · **baseline candidate**
  - Relevance **high**; role: component prior art; components: identifiability, anchored cell model
  - Shows that for a class of weak-supervision latent variable models the parameters have closed-form solutions via triplets of conditionally independent sources, giving FlyingSquid, which is far faster than SGD-based label models, has generalisation bounds without exact model specification, and matches or exceeds their quality.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Triplet identifiability (three conditionally independent sources) contrasts with SpecDx's two-model-plus-anchor identification. FlyingSquid is a fast binary-label baseline.
- **[li2019exploiting]** Yuan Li, Benjamin Rubinstein, Trevor Cohn (2019). *Exploiting Worker Correlation for Label Aggregation in Crowdsourcing*. ICML (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://proceedings.mlr.press/v97/li19i.html) · **baseline candidate**
  - Relevance **high**; role: component prior art; components: family effects/correlated errors, anchored cell model
  - Proposes Enhanced Bayesian Classifier Combination (EBCC), which induces inter-worker correlation through a mixture of intra-class reliabilities linked to tensor decomposition and item clustering, fitted by mean-field VI. It achieved the highest mean accuracy among ten methods on 17 real datasets.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* It is the strongest classic aggregator that models correlated raters, the crowd analogue of SpecDx's family effects. It is a natural simulation baseline to show what modelling correlation without anchors or set truth buys.
- **[paun2018comparing]** Silviu Paun, Bob Carpenter, Jon Chamberlain et al. (2018). *Comparing Bayesian Models of Annotation*. TACL (peer-reviewed). [link](https://doi.org/10.1162/tacl_a_00040)
  - Relevance **high**; role: survey; components: anchored cell model, item difficulty/IRT
  - Compares six Bayesian annotation models, which differ in how they treat annotator ability, item difficulty and parameter pooling, on four datasets with spammy annotators. It evaluates gold-label recovery, predictive accuracy, annotator characterisation and item difficulty, and gives model-selection guidelines.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* It provides the design space (pooling, difficulty, annotator ability) in which SpecDx's anchored cell model sits. It is a good source for the simulation baselines (DS, MACE, hierarchical models). None of the compared models has set-valued truth or an anchor.
- **[lin2018domain]** Xueling Lin, Lei Chen (2018). *Domain-aware multi-truth discovery from conflicting sources*. PVLDB (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A* (as VLDB)). [link](https://doi.org/10.1145/3187009.3177739)
  - Relevance **high**; role: component prior art; components: set-valued truth, item difficulty/IRT
  - Models domain-specific source expertise (via data richness and influence) in a Bayesian framework that jointly infers expertise and value-set confidences, recovering multiple truths per object without supervision (from metadata and a search-result abstract snippet).
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Domain-dependent expertise is analogous to SpecDx's model ability varying with cell group difficulty (alpha_m - beta_g). Like LTM, it supports set-valued truth but has no anchor and no defect typing.
- **[platanios2017estimating]** Emmanouil Platanios, Hoifung Poon, Tom M. Mitchell et al. (2017). *Estimating Accuracy from Unlabeled Data: A Probabilistic Logic Approach*. NeurIPS (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://papers.nips.cc/paper_files/paper/2017/hash/95f8d9901ca8878e291552f001f67692-Abstract.html)
  - Relevance **high**; role: component prior art; components: anchored cell model, MUS/MCS
  - Estimates classifier accuracies from unlabelled data across multiple related classification problems tied by logical constraints (e.g. mutual exclusion), using probabilistic logic. Agreement suggests correctness, and constraint violations imply at least one classifier errs.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* It is one of the few works combining statistics over a population of fallible predictors with logical constraints, close in spirit to SpecDx's statistics-plus-logic design. However, its constraints are assumed correct and the goal is to grade the classifiers, whereas SpecDx treats the constraints (the spec's checks) as the suspect and returns typed, witnessed defect diagnoses.
- **[bach2017learning]** Stephen H. Bach, Bryan He, Alexander Ratner et al. (2017). *Learning the Structure of Generative Models without Labeled Data*. ICML (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://proceedings.mlr.press/v70/bach17a.html)
  - Relevance **high**; role: component prior art; components: family effects/correlated errors, identifiability
  - Learns the dependency structure among weak-supervision sources without labelled data by maximising l1-regularised marginal pseudolikelihood, with sample complexity sublinear in the number of possible dependencies, and is much faster and more precise than maximum likelihood.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Learning which raters are dependent from unlabelled outputs could validate SpecDx's assumed family structure, i.e. whether errors are correlated within families and independent across them.
- **[ratner2017snorkel]** Alexander Ratner, Stephen H. Bach, Henry Ehrenberg et al. (2017). *Snorkel: rapid training data creation with weak supervision*. PVLDB (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A* (as VLDB)). [link](https://doi.org/10.14778/3157794.3157797) · [code](https://github.com/snorkel-team/snorkel) ★6024 · **baseline candidate**
  - Relevance **high**; role: component prior art; components: anchored cell model, family effects/correlated errors
  - Presents Snorkel, an end-to-end system for data programming. Users write labelling functions with unknown accuracies and correlations, Snorkel denoises them without ground truth, and models train without hand labels (2.8x faster model building and +45.5% performance in a user study).
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Snorkel's label model is the standard tool for aggregating correlated programmatic raters. SpecDx's checks C are labelling-function-like, but SpecDx asks whether C itself is wrong.
- **[zheng2017truth]** Yudian Zheng, Guoliang Li, Yuanbing Li et al. (2017). *Truth inference in crowdsourcing: is the problem solved?*. PVLDB (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A* (as VLDB)). [link](https://doi.org/10.14778/3055540.3055547)
  - Relevance **high**; role: survey; components: anchored cell model, baseline B2
  - Surveys 17 crowdsourcing truth-inference algorithms from the database and data-mining communities and evaluates them under one framework on five real datasets. No algorithm wins consistently, so the authors conclude the problem is not solved and release code and datasets.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* It is the main empirical survey of classic truth inference and the obvious source of simulation baselines. Its finding of instability across datasets motivates SpecDx's pre-registered simulation study instead of one benchmark.
- **[ratner2016data]** Alexander J Ratner, Christopher M De Sa, Sen Wu et al. (2016). *Data Programming: Creating Large Training Sets, Quickly*. NeurIPS (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://papers.nips.cc/paper_files/paper/2016/hash/6709e8d64a5f47269ed5cea9f625f7ab-Abstract.html) · [code](https://github.com/snorkel-team/snorkel) ★6024 · **baseline candidate**
  - Relevance **high**; role: foundation; components: anchored cell model, identifiability
  - Introduces data programming: users write noisy, conflicting labelling functions viewed as a generative noise model whose parameters are recovered without labels (with recovery guarantees in some settings), then a noise-aware discriminative loss trains the end model.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Labelling functions are fallible programmatic raters, like SpecDx's executable clauses C. SpecDx inverts the setting: it uses LLM interpreters to audit the programmatic checks rather than using checks to label data.
- **[zhang2016spectral]** Yuchen Zhang, Xi Chen, Dengyong Zhou et al. (2016). *Spectral Methods Meet EM: A Provably Optimal Algorithm for Crowdsourcing*. JMLR (peer-reviewed, CORE2020 A*). [link](https://jmlr.org/papers/v17/14-511.html)
  - Relevance **high**; role: foundation; components: identifiability, anchored cell model
  - Gives a two-stage algorithm for multi-class Dawid-Skene: a spectral (tensor) method initialises the confusion matrices and EM then refines them. It achieves the optimal convergence rate up to a log factor, and is comparable to the best empirical methods (journal version of NeurIPS 2014).
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* It is the reference for provable DS estimation via third-order moments, which needs three or more conditionally independent raters. SpecDx's identification route (accuracy gradient plus anchor) and its finite-sample Hoeffding bounds should be compared against this rate.
- **[jaffe2016unsupervised]** Ariel Jaffe, Ethan Fetaya, Boaz Nadler et al. (2016). *Unsupervised Ensemble Learning with Dependent Classifiers*. AISTATS (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A). [link](https://proceedings.mlr.press/v51/jaffe16.html)
  - Relevance **high**; role: component prior art; components: family effects/correlated errors, identifiability
  - Introduces a statistical model that allows groups of dependent classifiers in unsupervised ensemble learning, with methods to detect strong violations of conditional independence without labels, estimate accuracies better and build an improved meta-learner.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* It is the closest unsupervised-ensemble treatment of SpecDx's family effects: dependent groups of raters detected without labels. SpecDx knows family membership a priori and should test within-family dependence as this paper does.
- **[wang2015integrated]** Xianzhi Wang, Quan Z. Sheng, Xiu Susie Fang et al. (2015). *An Integrated Bayesian Approach for Effective Multi-Truth Discovery*. CIKM (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A). [link](https://doi.org/10.1145/2806416.2806443)
  - Relevance **high**; role: component prior art; components: set-valued truth
  - Proposes an integrated Bayesian approach to multi-truth discovery that accounts for claims made of value sets, the differing implications of mutual exclusion between values, and richer source profiles (from the S2 TLDR).
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* It is direct prior art for set-valued truth inference. SpecDx's entailment sets are a restricted, interpretable case ({x}, {x,y}, empty) tied to defect types, with an anchor rater that multi-truth discovery lacks.
- **[jaffe2015estimating]** Ariel Jaffe, Boaz Nadler, Yuval Kluger (2015). *Estimating the accuracies of multiple classifiers without labeled data*. AISTATS (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A). [link](https://proceedings.mlr.press/v38/jaffe15.html) · **baseline candidate**
  - Relevance **high**; role: component prior art; components: identifiability, baseline B2
  - Shows that under conditional independence the accuracies of many binary classifiers can be estimated consistently from unlabelled predictions alone, because the off-diagonal covariance matrix and third-order tensor are rank-one. Gives efficient spectral algorithms and a better unsupervised ensemble.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* It is the canonical evidence that accuracy is identifiable from agreement structure alone. SpecDx's accuracy-gradient signatures play a similar role but separate spec defects (consistent deviations) from interpreter errors.
- **[zhi2015modeling]** Shi Zhi, Bo Zhao, Wenzhu Tong et al. (2015). *Modeling Truth Existence in Truth Discovery*. KDD (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.1145/2783258.2783339)
  - Relevance **high**; role: component prior art; components: set-valued truth, defect taxonomy
  - Addresses no-truth questions, whose true answer is absent from every source's candidate answers. It proposes a graphical model with a source quality that covers silence, which jointly infers truth existence, truths and source quality without ground truth, initialised by a truth-existence score.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* It is the truth-discovery counterpart of SpecDx's empty entailment set (A_c = empty): an item where no offered value is true. SpecDx ties this case to contradiction and hidden-requirement defects and adds the spec anchor.
- **[aroyo2015truth]** Lora Aroyo, Chris Welty (2015). *Truth Is a Lie: Crowd Truth and the Seven Myths of Human Annotation*. AI Mag. (peer-reviewed, CORE2020 C). [link](https://doi.org/10.1609/aimag.v36i1.2564)
  - Relevance **high**; role: foundation; components: set-valued truth, defect taxonomy
  - Argues that annotation practice rests on the outdated ideal of a single correct truth, dispels seven myths that follow from it, and proposes 'crowd truth': measuring annotations across a crowd captures subjectivity and the range of reasonable interpretations.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* It is the philosophical basis for treating disagreement as information about the item, which is SpecDx's core move (spatially concentrated disagreement means a spec defect). SpecDx formalises 'the range of reasonable interpretations' as the entailment set A_c.
- **[venanzi2014community]** Matteo Venanzi, John Guiver, Gabriella Kazai et al. (2014). *Community-based bayesian aggregation models for crowdsourcing*. WWW (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.1145/2566486.2567989)
  - Relevance **high**; role: component prior art; components: anchored cell model, family effects/correlated errors
  - Proposes CommunityBCC, a Bayesian label-aggregation model in which crowd workers belong to a few latent communities that share similar confusion matrices, and reports that it consistently beats prior aggregation methods (from the S2 TLDR; the abstract was not shown).
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Communities of raters with shared confusion matrices are the crowd analogue of SpecDx's model families. CBCC infers the communities, whereas SpecDx knows the families, and it uses them to model correlated errors, not to pool accuracy.
- **[pochampally2014fusing]** Ravali Pochampally, Anish Das Sarma, Xin Luna Dong et al. (2014). *Fusing data with correlations*. SIGMOD (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.1145/2588555.2593674)
  - Relevance **high**; role: component prior art; components: family effects/correlated errors
  - Generalises source dependence beyond copying to arbitrary positive and negative correlations among sources and extractors (e.g. complementary domains, shared extraction errors), and gives fusion algorithms that account for them, outperforming prior methods on real and synthetic data.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Correlated LLM families (shared training data or architecture) are precisely the correlated sources studied here. SpecDx assumes cross-family independence. This paper is the template for modelling or testing that assumption.
- **[parisi2014ranking]** Fabio Parisi, Francesco Strino, Boaz Nadler et al. (2014). *Ranking and combining multiple predictors without labeled data*. PNAS (peer-reviewed). [link](https://doi.org/10.1073/pnas.1219097111) · **baseline candidate**
  - Relevance **high**; role: component prior art; components: identifiability, family effects/correlated errors, baseline B2
  - Shows that for conditionally independent binary advisers the off-diagonal covariance is rank-one, which lets one rank advisers and combine their predictions without ground truth (the spectral meta-learner). The method is robust to small cartels of malicious advisers.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* It is the founding spectral result on unsupervised rater ranking. 'Cartels' are correlated subgroups, similar to SpecDx's model families. It can rank interpreters for B2 without gold.
- **[hovy2013learning]** Dirk Hovy, Taylor Berg-Kirkpatrick, Ashish Vaswani et al. (2013). *Learning Whom to Trust with MACE*. NAACL (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A). [link](https://aclanthology.org/N13-1132/) · [code](https://github.com/dirkhovy/MACE) ★143 · **baseline candidate**
  - Relevance **high**; role: component prior art; components: anchored cell model, baseline B2
  - MACE (Multi-Annotator Competence Estimation) learns without supervision which annotators are trustworthy and predicts the underlying labels, improving both label accuracy and trustworthiness estimates over standard baselines (from the S2 TLDR and metadata; the abstract was not available).
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* It is the closest classic analogue of SpecDx's competent-reading-or-noise mixture for each rater. SpecDx adds item difficulty, set-valued truth and an anchor, and restricts errors to values outside the entailment set.
- **[zhao2012bayesian]** Bo Zhao, Benjamin I. P. Rubinstein, Jim Gemmell et al. (2012). *A Bayesian approach to discovering truth from conflicting sources for data integration*. PVLDB (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A* (as VLDB)). [link](https://doi.org/10.14778/2168651.2168656) · **baseline candidate**
  - Relevance **high**; role: component prior art; components: set-valued truth, anchored cell model
  - Proposes the Latent Truth Model, an unsupervised Bayesian graphical model that infers true records and source quality for data integration. It models two kinds of source error (false positives and false negatives), so it is the first truth-finding method able to merge multi-valued attributes, and it scales via sampling-based inference.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* LTM is the closest data-integration prior art for SpecDx's set-valued truth: each fact (value) is independently true or false, so an entity can have several true values. SpecDx restricts the entailment set to {x}, {x,y} or empty, and adds an anchor source plus item difficulty. LTM has no notion of the spec as a source whose disagreement is diagnostic.
- **[bachrach2012grade]** Yoram Bachrach, Thore Graepel, Tom Minka et al. (2012). *How To Grade a Test Without Knowing the Answers — A Bayesian Graphical Model for Adaptive Crowdsourcing and Aptitude Testing*. ICML (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://icml.cc/2012/papers.1.html#paper-597) · **baseline candidate**
  - Relevance **high**; role: component prior art; components: item difficulty/IRT, anchored cell model, active synthesis
  - Proposes a Bayesian graphical model that jointly infers question difficulties, participant abilities and the correct answers in aptitude tests and crowdsourcing, plus an adaptive testing scheme that picks the next question by greedy expected-entropy minimisation, which needs fewer questions.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* It is the closest classic model to SpecDx's anchored cell model: IRT-style ability-minus-difficulty with an unknown answer key, and active item selection like SpecDx's Thompson-sampling scenario synthesis. SpecDx adds the declared key as a fallible anchor and allows two-valued or empty keys.
- **[yin2011semi]** Xiaoxin Yin, Wenzhao Tan (2011). *Semi-supervised truth discovery*. WWW (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.1145/1963405.1963439)
  - Relevance **high**; role: component prior art; components: anchored cell model
  - Proposes semi-supervised truth discovery that uses a small amount of ground-truth data to guide the estimation of true values, with an optimal solution and a convergent iterative algorithm (from the S2 TLDR).
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* It is the closest truth-discovery analogue of SpecDx's anchors. Here gold facts are trusted, whereas SpecDx's anchor (the declared spec value) is itself fallible and is what is being audited.
- **[allman2009identifiability]** Elizabeth S. Allman, Catherine Matias, John A. Rhodes (2009). *Identifiability of parameters in latent structure models with many observed variables*. Ann. Statist. (peer-reviewed). [link](https://doi.org/10.1214/09-aos689)
  - Relevance **high**; role: foundation; components: identifiability
  - Develops an algebraic approach, built on Kruskal's theorem for three-way arrays, to prove generic identifiability of many latent-structure models (finite mixtures of product distributions, HMMs, random graph mixtures). It shows that classical identifiability is too strong and generic identifiability (non-identifiable set of measure zero) is the right notion.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* It is the standard identifiability reference for conditional-independence latent class models with three or more raters. SpecDx's identification arguments (two models with distinct accuracy, or one model plus a known error pool, plus the anchor) are an alternative route for a structured model with set-valued truth, and should be contrasted with the Kruskal/Allman conditions.
- **[dong2009integrating]** Xin Luna Dong, Laure Berti-Equille, Divesh Srivastava (2009). *Integrating conflicting data: the role of source dependence*. PVLDB (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A* (as VLDB)). [link](https://doi.org/10.14778/1687627.1687690)
  - Relevance **high**; role: component prior art; components: family effects/correlated errors
  - Shows that copying between sources spreads false values and defeats majority-based truth discovery. Proposes Bayesian detection of dependence, where sources sharing many rare (especially false) values likely copy, inside an iterative algorithm that jointly discovers truth and dependence, extended with source accuracy and value similarity.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* It is the canonical treatment of correlated raters in truth discovery. Shared rare errors between LLMs of one family are exactly 'copying' signals. SpecDx assumes independence across families and could borrow Dong et al.'s dependence test to validate that assumption on its reading matrix.
- **[whitehill2009whose]** Jacob Whitehill, Ting-fan Wu, Jacob Bergsma et al. (2009). *Whose Vote Should Count More: Optimal Integration of Labels from Labelers of Unknown Expertise*. NeurIPS (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://papers.nips.cc/paper_files/paper/2009/hash/f899139df5e1059396431415e770c6dd-Abstract.html) · [code](https://github.com/Toloka/crowd-kit) ★255 · **baseline candidate**
  - Relevance **high**; role: foundation; components: anchored cell model, item difficulty/IRT, baseline B3
  - Proposes GLAD, a probabilistic model that jointly infers each image's label, each labeler's expertise and each image's difficulty from multiple noisy binary labels, and shows it beats majority vote and is robust to adversarial and noisy labelers.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* GLAD's accuracy term (labeler ability times inverse item difficulty inside a logistic) is the direct ancestor of SpecDx's sigma(alpha_m - beta_g) faithful-reading probability. GLAD assumes one true label per item and has no anchor rater or empty or two-valued truth.
- **[albert2004cautionary]** Paul S. Albert, Lori E. Dodd (2004). *A Cautionary Note on the Robustness of Latent Class Models for Estimating Diagnostic Error without a Gold Standard*. Biometrics (peer-reviewed). [link](https://doi.org/10.1111/j.0006-341x.2004.00187.x)
  - Relevance **high**; role: component prior art; components: family effects/correlated errors, identifiability
  - Shows that misspecifying the conditional dependence between tests in latent class models biases estimates of sensitivity, specificity and prevalence. With few tests, likelihood comparisons cannot distinguish dependence structures. Gives asymptotics, simulations and practitioner guidelines.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* It is a direct warning for SpecDx: if same-family LLM errors are correlated and the model assumes independence, the inferred truth (and so the defect type) can be biased in ways fit statistics cannot detect. This motivates SpecDx's family-level design and its simulation stress tests.
- **[qu1996random]** Yinsheng Qu, Ming Tan, Michael H. Kutner (1996). *Random Effects Models in Latent Class Analysis for Evaluating Accuracy of Diagnostic Tests*. Biometrics (peer-reviewed). [link](https://doi.org/10.2307/2533043)
  - Relevance **high**; role: component prior art; components: family effects/correlated errors, identifiability
  - Extends two-class latent class models for diagnostic accuracy without an error-free reference by adding random effects that capture conditional dependence among tests or readers, and gives a graphical check for when that dependence matters.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* It is the standard way to relax the conditional-independence assumption SpecDx makes across model families. A random family effect is a natural extension, or sensitivity analysis, for SpecDx's family-independence assumption.
- **[littlewood1989conceptual]** B. Littlewood, D.R. Miller (1989). *Conceptual modeling of coincident failures in multiversion software*. TSE (peer-reviewed, CORE2020 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.1109/32.58771)
  - Relevance **high**; role: foundation; also in t6_llm_disagreement_ambiguity; components: family effects/correlated errors, identifiability
  - Extends the Eckhardt-Lee model with forced (methodological) diversity: formalising methodology as a sequence of decisions, it shows that diverse decisions imply likely diverse behaviour of versions developed under forced diversity (from the S2 TLDR).
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* It justifies SpecDx's use of diverse model families (different 'methodologies') to decorrelate interpreter errors. | Justifies SpecDx's use of several model families and Tell variants as forced diversity: diverse 'methodologies' reduce coincident errors, so a consensus deviation that survives diversification is more attributable to the specification.
- **[batchelder1988test]** William H. Batchelder, A. Kimball Romney (1988). *Test Theory without an Answer Key*. Psychometrika (peer-reviewed). [link](https://doi.org/10.1007/bf02294195)
  - Relevance **high**; role: foundation; components: anchored cell model, item difficulty/IRT
  - Presents a model for dichotomous items when the answer key is unknown, structurally a two-class latent model with the roles of respondents and items swapped. It develops estimation methods and shows by Monte Carlo that the key can be reconstructed accurately from small groups of respondents.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Cultural consensus theory, i.e. recovering the answer key from competent respondents, is exactly SpecDx's setting with LLMs as respondents. SpecDx differs in starting from a declared key (the spec) that may be wrong, and in allowing set-valued keys.
- **[knight1986experimental]** John C. Knight, Nancy G. Leveson (1986). *An experimental evaluation of the assumption of independence in multiversion programming*. TSE (peer-reviewed, CORE2020 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.1109/tse.1986.6312924)
  - Relevance **high**; role: foundation; also in t6_llm_disagreement_ambiguity; components: family effects/correlated errors
  - An experiment in which 27 programmers independently implemented one specification (the Launch Interceptor Program), evaluated on a million test inputs, rejected the hypothesis of independent failures: coincident failures were far more frequent than independence predicts (paraphrased from a search-result abstract summary).
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* It is the classic evidence that independently built executors of the same spec fail together, often where the spec is hard. SpecDx's committee of LLM interpreters is an N-version system, and SpecDx exploits exactly such coincident deviations as evidence of spec defects while guarding against family-level correlation. | The classic evidence that independently produced interpreters of one specification fail together; SpecDx's assumption of independent errors across model families is exactly what this paper warns against, so SpecDx must model shared difficulty and family effects and test the assumption empirically (e.g. by within- vs across-family error correlation).
- **[eckhardt1985theoretical]** D.E. Eckhardt, L.D. Lee (1985). *A Theoretical Basis for the Analysis of Multiversion Software Subject to Coincident Errors*. TSE (peer-reviewed, CORE2020 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.1109/tse.1985.231895)
  - Relevance **high**; role: foundation; also in t6_llm_disagreement_ambiguity; components: family effects/correlated errors, item difficulty/IRT, identifiability
  - Provides a probabilistic framework for multiversion software subject to coincident errors, centred on an 'intensity of coincident errors' function over the input space that describes the propensity of versions to fail together. It analyses how system failure probability depends on the number of versions N (paraphrased from a search-result abstract summary).
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* The EL model is the software-engineering ancestor of SpecDx's cell difficulty: input-dependent difficulty induces correlated failures even among independent developers. SpecDx's beta_g plays the role of the intensity function, and a spec defect is the limiting case where every competent version fails at the same input. | The Eckhardt-Lee model explains correlated failures by input difficulty varying over the input space, which is precisely SpecDx's item-difficulty term beta_g: conditional on a cell's difficulty, interpreters can be independent while marginal errors are correlated. SpecDx must distinguish such difficulty-induced coincident errors from specification defects, which is what its accuracy-gradient identifiability argument addresses.
- **[hui1980estimating]** S. L. Hui, S. D. Walter (1980). *Estimating the Error Rates of Diagnostic Tests*. Biometrics (peer-reviewed). [link](https://doi.org/10.2307/2530508)
  - Relevance **high**; role: foundation; components: identifiability, anchored cell model
  - Shows that when two tests are applied simultaneously to individuals from two populations with different disease prevalences, and assuming conditional independence of test errors, maximum likelihood estimates both tests' error rates and both prevalences. It generalises to several tests and populations.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* It is the classic proof that fallible raters can be calibrated without gold when an exogenous contrast (different prevalences) exists. SpecDx's 'identification from two models with distinct accuracy' uses a contrast across raters instead of across populations, and the spec anchor plays the role of the imperfect reference test.
- **[dawid1979maximum]** A. P. Dawid, A. M. Skene (1979). *Maximum Likelihood Estimation of Observer Error-Rates Using the EM Algorithm*. JRSS-C (peer-reviewed). [link](https://doi.org/10.2307/2346806) · [code](https://github.com/Toloka/crowd-kit) ★255 · **baseline candidate**
  - Relevance **high**; role: foundation; components: anchored cell model, baseline B2
  - Introduces the latent-class model in which each observer has an unknown class-confusion matrix and the true class of each item is latent, and shows that EM yields maximum-likelihood estimates of observer error rates and true classes without gold labels (motivated by compiling patient records from several clinicians).
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* SpecDx's anchored cell model is a Dawid-Skene descendant: models are observers, cells are items. SpecDx differs in three ways: the truth is a set (A_c in {{x},{x,y},empty}), the specification's declared value is treated as one more fallible rater (the anchor), and per-model accuracy is parameterised by ability minus cell difficulty instead of a free confusion matrix per rater.
- **[stinson2025nonparametric]** Patrick Stinson, Nikolaus Kriegeskorte (2025). *Nonparametric Bayesian inference of item-level features in classifier combination*. UAI (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A). [link](https://proceedings.mlr.press/v286/stinson25a.html)
  - Relevance **medium**; role: component prior art; components: item difficulty/IRT, anchored cell model
  - Develops a Bayesian classifier-combination model that infers latent item features via an Indian Buffet Process, capturing item heterogeneity that affects classifiers, and compares it with IRT and BCC on crowdsourcing and with instance-dependent neural models.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* It is a nonparametric generalisation of per-item difficulty. SpecDx's cell-group difficulty beta_g is a parametric special case.
- **[li2024comparative]** Jiyi Li (2024). *A Comparative Study on Annotation Quality of Crowdsourcing and LLm Via Label Aggregation*. ICASSP (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 B). [link](https://doi.org/10.1109/icassp48485.2024.10447803)
  - Relevance **medium**; role: component prior art; components: anchored cell model
  - Builds a benchmark from existing crowdsourcing datasets, compares individual crowd and LLM labels and their aggregates, and proposes a Crowd-LLM hybrid aggregation. Adding labels from good LLMs to crowd data improves the aggregated labels beyond the LLM labels alone.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* It treats LLMs as workers inside classic truth-inference models, as SpecDx does. It assumes a correct gold standard for evaluation, whereas SpecDx audits the gold.
- **[gao2024bayesian]** Yicheng Gao, Gonghan Xu, Zhe Wang et al. (2024). *Bayesian Calibration of Win Rate Estimation with LLM Evaluators*. EMNLP (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.18653/v1/2024.emnlp-main.273)
  - Relevance **medium**; role: component prior art; components: anchored cell model
  - Proposes Bayesian Win-Rate Sampling and a Bayesian Dawid-Skene model to correct the inaccuracy and bias of LLM evaluators when estimating win rates between generative models, validated on six datasets covering story generation, summarisation and instruction following.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* It applies a Dawid-Skene latent-class model to LLM raters, the same move as SpecDx. Its target is the systems' win rate, not defects in the rubric or specification.
- **[verga2024replacing]** Pat Verga, Sebastian Hofstatter, Sophia Althammer et al. (2024). *Replacing Judges with Juries: Evaluating LLM Generations with a Panel of Diverse Models*. arXiv (preprint). [link](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.18796)
  - Relevance **medium**; role: context; components: family effects/correlated errors, baseline B4
  - Proposes evaluating LLM outputs with a Panel of LLM evaluators (PoLL) drawn from disjoint model families instead of a single large judge. Across three judge settings and six datasets the panel correlates better with humans, shows less intra-model bias and costs over seven times less.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* It motivates SpecDx's multi-family committee. PoLL aggregates by simple voting and assumes the rubric is correct, whereas SpecDx models raters and audits the rubric (spec).
- **[schoenegger2024wisdom]** Philipp Schoenegger, Indre Tuminauskaite, Peter S. Park et al. (2024). *Wisdom of the silicon crowd: LLM ensemble prediction capabilities rival human crowd accuracy*. Sci. Adv. (peer-reviewed). [link](https://doi.org/10.1126/sciadv.adp1528)
  - Relevance **medium**; role: context; also in t6_llm_disagreement_ambiguity; components: family effects/correlated errors
  - Aggregates probabilistic forecasts from 12 LLMs on 31 binary questions and finds that the LLM crowd beats a no-information benchmark and is statistically indistinguishable from a 925-person human crowd. LLMs show human-like biases, and they improve when shown the human median.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* It is empirical support that a diverse population of LLMs behaves like a crowd of fallible raters, the premise of SpecDx's committee. | Evidence that aggregating heterogeneous LLMs yields crowd-like accuracy, supporting SpecDx's population view, while shared biases illustrate correlated errors across families.
- **[liu2023identifiability]** Yang Liu, Hao Cheng, Kun Zhang (2023). *Identifiability of Label Noise Transition Matrix*. ICML (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://proceedings.mlr.press/v202/liu23g.html) · [code](https://github.com/UCSC-REAL/Identifiability) ★4
  - Relevance **medium**; role: component prior art; components: identifiability

  - *Relation to SpecDx:* It is a modern Kruskal-based identifiability analysis for fallible labels. SpecDx needs a parallel statement for its anchored, set-valued model.
- **[bommasani2022picking]** Rishi Bommasani, Kathleen A. Creel, Ananya Kumar et al. (2022). *Picking on the Same Person: Does Algorithmic Monoculture Lead to Outcome Homogenization?*. NeurIPS (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.52202/068431-0265)
  - Relevance **medium**; role: context; also in t6_llm_disagreement_ambiguity; components: family effects/correlated errors
  - Formalises outcome homogenization under algorithmic monoculture and proposes the component-sharing hypothesis: systems sharing training data or models produce more homogeneous outcomes. Shared training data reliably increases homogenization, with mixed results for shared foundation models.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* It provides the theory for why LLMs sharing data or architecture (same family) have correlated errors, the risk SpecDx's family-level independence assumption must address. | The ML version of the N-version independence problem: models that share pretraining data or bases fail on the same items, which threatens SpecDx's cross-family independence assumption and motivates measuring family effects.
- **[shah2021permutation]** Nihar B. Shah, Sivaraman Balakrishnan, Martin J. Wainwright (2021). *A Permutation-Based Model for Crowd Labeling: Optimal Estimation and Robustness*. IEEE TIT (peer-reviewed, CORE2020 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.1109/tit.2020.3045613)
  - Relevance **medium**; role: foundation; components: identifiability, item difficulty/IRT
  - Proposes a permutation-based crowd-labelling model that generalises Dawid-Skene (workers and questions ordered by ability and difficulty without parametric form), derives sharp global minimax rates, and gives efficient WAN and OBI-WAN estimators with non-asymptotic guarantees.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* It is a nonparametric relaxation of the ability-difficulty structure that SpecDx's sigma(alpha_m - beta_g) imposes parametrically. It is useful for robustness of the identifiability claims.
- **[ibrahim2021crowdsourcing]** Shahana Ibrahim, Xiao Fu (2021). *Crowdsourcing via Annotator Co-occurrence Imputation and Provable Symmetric Nonnegative Matrix Factorization*. ICML (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://proceedings.mlr.press/v139/ibrahim21a.html)
  - Relevance **medium**; role: component prior art; components: identifiability
  - Recasts Dawid-Skene learning from pairwise annotator co-occurrences as symmetric NMF, which has stronger identifiability than coupled NMF. It adds co-occurrence imputation for sparse co-labelling and gives a provable low-complexity algorithm.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* It offers a second-order-moment identifiability route for DS, relevant to SpecDx's claim that two models of distinct accuracy suffice under its structured model.
- **[uma2021learning]** Alexandra N. Uma, Tommaso Fornaciari, Dirk Hovy et al. (2021). *Learning from Disagreement: A Survey*. JAIR (peer-reviewed, CORE2020 A). [link](https://doi.org/10.1613/jair.1.12752)
  - Relevance **medium**; role: survey; components: set-valued truth
  - Surveys evidence of human disagreement across NLP and vision tasks and the methods for training from multiple, possibly conflicting judgments, comparing them systematically under a common evaluation.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* It is the survey anchor for 'disagreement is not noise'. SpecDx uses disagreement diagnostically, to type defects in the spec, rather than as a training signal.
- **[mazzetto2021semi]** Alessio Mazzetto, Dylan Sam, Andrew Park et al. (2021). *Semi-Supervised Aggregation of Dependent Weak Supervision Sources With Performance Guarantees*. AISTATS (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A). [link](https://proceedings.mlr.press/v130/mazzetto21a.html)

  - Gives performance-guaranteed aggregation of weak labelers without assuming independent errors: a small labelled set is used to select subsets of labelers whose majority vote has provably low error, beating semi-supervised Dawid-Skene by about 15 points on hard tasks.

- **[pavlick2019inherent]** Ellie Pavlick, Tom Kwiatkowski (2019). *Inherent Disagreements in Human Textual Inferences*. TACL (peer-reviewed). [link](https://doi.org/10.1162/tacl_a_00293)
  - Relevance **medium**; role: context; components: set-valued truth, defect taxonomy
  - Shows that human disagreement about natural language inferences persists with more raters and more context, so it is not just noise, and that current NLI models' uncertainty does not reflect it. Argues for evaluating against the full distribution of plausible judgments.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* It supports SpecDx's position that some cells are genuinely two-valued (ambiguity) and should not be forced to a single truth.
- **[varma2019learning]** Paroma Varma, Frederic Sala, Ann He et al. (2019). *Learning Dependency Structures for Weak Supervision Models*. ICML (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://proceedings.mlr.press/v97/varma19a.html)

  - Learns weak-supervision source dependencies with a robust-PCA method, with improved recovery rates (sample complexity can be sublinear or logarithmic in the number of sources) and an information-theoretic lower bound, beating independence-assuming label models.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* It is an alternative tool for discovering latent family structure among LLM interpreters from the reading matrix.
- **[ratner2019training]** Alexander Ratner, Braden Hancock, Jared Dunnmon et al. (2019). *Training Complex Models with Multi-Task Weak Supervision*. AAAI (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.1609/aaai.v33i01.33014763) · [code](https://github.com/HazyResearch/metal) ★431

  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Its identifiability via the inverse covariance with known dependency structure is the analogue of SpecDx's known family structure. Multi-task granularity is like clause-level vs cell-level readings.
- **[li2019truth]** Yuan Li, Benjamin I. P. Rubinstein, Trevor Cohn (2019). *Truth Inference at Scale: A Bayesian Model for Adjudicating Highly Redundant Crowd Annotations*. WWW (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.1145/3308558.3313459)
  - Relevance **medium**; role: component prior art; components: anchored cell model
  - Shows that in highly redundant crowdsourcing (five or more labels per item, mostly good workers) majority vote beats most truth-inference models, and proposes a conjugate Bayesian model with simple EM that is competitive across redundancy regimes.

- **[imamura2018analysis]** Hideaki Imamura, Issei Sato, Masashi Sugiyama (2018). *Analysis of Minimax Error Rate for Crowdsourcing and Its Application to Worker Clustering Model*. ICML (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://proceedings.mlr.press/v80/imamura18a.html)

- **[dumitrache2018crowdsourcing]** Anca Dumitrache, Lora Aroyo, Chris Welty (2018). *Crowdsourcing Ground Truth for Medical Relation Extraction*. TiiS (peer-reviewed). [link](https://doi.org/10.1145/3152889) · [code](https://github.com/CrowdTruth/CrowdTruth-core) ★64 · **baseline candidate**

- **[daniel2018quality]** Florian Daniel, Pavel Kucherbaev, Cinzia Cappiello et al. (2018). *Quality Control in Crowdsourcing: A Survey of Quality Attributes, Assessment Techniques, and Assurance Actions*. CSUR (peer-reviewed, CORE2020 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.1145/3148148)
  - Relevance **medium**; role: survey; components: anchored cell model

- **[prelec2017solution]** Dražen Prelec, H. Sebastian Seung, John McCoy (2017). *A solution to the single-question crowd wisdom problem*. Nature (peer-reviewed). [link](https://doi.org/10.1038/nature21054)

It proposes selecting the answer that is more popular than people predict (the 'surprisingly popular' principle), which recovers the best answer under reasonable assumptions where majority or confidence rules fail, even for a single question.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* It is a reminder that majority agreement need not be truth. SpecDx uses accuracy-gradient signatures (how answers shift with interpreter competence), a related signal for when the majority reading differs from the competent reading.
- **[li2016survey]** Yaliang Li, Jing Gao, Chuishi Meng et al. (2016). *A Survey on Truth Discovery*. SIGKDD Explor. (peer-reviewed). [link](https://doi.org/10.1145/2897350.2897352)
  - Relevance **medium**; role: survey; components: anchored cell model

  - *Relation to SpecDx:* It is a survey anchor for the truth-discovery side of SpecDx's anchored cell model.
- **[wang2016empowering]** Xianzhi Wang, Quan Z. Sheng, Lina Yao et al. (2016). *Empowering Truth Discovery with Multi-Truth Prediction*. CIKM (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A). [link](https://doi.org/10.1145/2983323.2983767)

  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Predicting the cardinality of the truth set is SpecDx's {x} vs {x,y} vs empty decision. SpecDx does it with an explicit generative model and anchor, not a wrapper.
- **[platanios2016estimating]** Emmanouil Antonios Platanios, Avinava Dubey, Tom Mitchell (2016). *Estimating Accuracy from Unlabeled Data: A Bayesian Approach*. ICML (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://proceedings.mlr.press/v48/platanios16.html)

  - *Relation to SpecDx:* It shares the goal of estimating interpreter accuracy without labels. SpecDx adds the spec anchor and set-valued truth, and its object of interest is the spec, not the classifiers.
- **[gao2016exact]** Chao Gao, Yu Lu, Dengyong Zhou (2016). *Exact Exponent in Optimal Rates for Crowdsourcing*. ICML (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://proceedings.mlr.press/v48/gaoa16.html)

  - *Relation to SpecDx:* It gives the information-theoretic limit for how many models are needed per cell. SpecDx's committee size and escalation thresholds can be justified against this exponent.
- **[dong2015knowledge]** Xin Luna Dong, Evgeniy Gabrilovich, Kevin Murphy et al. (2015). *Knowledge-based trust: estimating the trustworthiness of web sources*. PVLDB (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A* (as VLDB)). [link](https://doi.org/10.14778/2777598.2777603)

  - Proposes Knowledge-Based Trust: estimate web-source trustworthiness from the correctness of facts it provides, using a multi-layer probabilistic model that separates errors made by the extractors from factual errors in the sources, applied to 2.8B extracted facts.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Its two-layer separation (extractor error vs source error) is analogous to SpecDx's separation of interpreter error from specification defect.
- **[karger2014budget]** David R. Karger, Sewoong Oh, Devavrat Shah (2014). *Budget-Optimal Task Allocation for Reliable Crowdsourcing Systems*. Oper. Res. (peer-reviewed, CORE2020 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.1287/opre.2013.1235)

  - Journal version of the KOS work: it poses the problem of minimising total task assignments for a target reliability and gives assignment and inference algorithms proven asymptotically optimal, outperforming majority voting.
