- **[li2014resolving]** Qi Li, Yaliang Li, Jing Gao et al. (2014). *Resolving conflicts in heterogeneous data by truth discovery and source reliability estimation*. SIGMOD (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.1145/2588555.2610509)
  - Relevance **medium**; role: component prior art; components: anchored cell model
  - Proposes CRH, an optimisation framework that treats truths and source reliabilities as unknowns and minimises the reliability-weighted deviation between truths and multi-source observations, handling heterogeneous data types (categorical and continuous) jointly.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Database cells are heterogeneous (categorical, numeric), like CRH's objects. CRH gives a loss-based alternative to SpecDx's likelihood model but has single-valued truth and no anchor.
- **[anandkumar2014tensor]** Animashree Anandkumar, Rong Ge, Daniel Hsu et al. (2014). *Tensor Decompositions for Learning Latent Variable Models*. JMLR (peer-reviewed, CORE2020 A*). [link](https://jmlr.org/papers/v15/anandkumar14b.html)
  - Relevance **medium**; role: foundation; components: identifiability
  - Gives a computationally and statistically efficient moment-based estimator for latent variable models (GMMs, HMMs, LDA) by reducing estimation to an orthogonal decomposition of a symmetric tensor built from low-order moments, with a robust tensor power method and perturbation analysis.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* It is the general method-of-moments machinery behind spectral Dawid-Skene. SpecDx's identification arguments sit beside this third-order-moment route.
- **[passonneau2014benefits]** Rebecca J. Passonneau, Bob Carpenter (2014). *The Benefits of a Model of Annotation*. TACL (peer-reviewed). [link](https://doi.org/10.1162/tacl_a_00185)
  - Relevance **medium**; role: context; components: anchored cell model
  - Argues that inter-annotator agreement coefficients are neither necessary nor sufficient for corpus quality. A probabilistic annotation model applied to crowdsourced word-sense labels gives a certainty for each gold label at less than half the cost of trained annotators.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* It supports SpecDx's choice of a generative rater model over agreement statistics, e.g. B4 entropy. SpecDx extends such a model with an anchor and a set-valued truth.
- **[liu2013scoring]** Qiang Liu, Alex Ihler, Mark Steyvers (2013). *Scoring Workers in Crowdsourcing: How Many Control Questions are Enough?*. NeurIPS (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://papers.nips.cc/paper_files/paper/2013/hash/cc1aa436277138f61cda703991069eaf-Abstract.html)
  - Relevance **medium**; role: component prior art; components: anchored cell model
  - Analyses how many control items with known answers to give each crowd worker when budgets are limited. More control items score workers better but leave fewer target items, and the paper derives the optimal number for consensus estimation of continuous quantities.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* It is the gold-item theory behind anchors. In SpecDx the anchor (d_c) is not known to be correct, so anchor-based contrasts use a robust median (breakdown 1/2) rather than trusted control items.
- **[kim2012bayesian]** Hyun-Chul Kim, Zoubin Ghahramani (2012). *Bayesian Classifier Combination*. AISTATS (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A). [link](https://proceedings.mlr.press/v22/kim12.html)
  - Relevance **medium**; role: foundation; components: anchored cell model
  - Develops Bayesian classifier combination: a framework that models each base classifier's or human assessor's output given the unknown true label, starting from Dawid-Skene and adding richer structure via MCMC. It needs neither probabilistic nor independent base models, nor a correct one, and gives interpretable diagnostics.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* It is the Bayesian Dawid-Skene (IBCC) that SpecDx's MAP/EM fit with Dirichlet priors resembles. IBCC has no anchor rater, no item difficulty and no set-valued truth.
- **[zhou2012learning]** Dengyong Zhou, Sumit Basu, Yi Mao et al. (2012). *Learning from the Wisdom of Crowds by Minimax Entropy*. NeurIPS (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://papers.nips.cc/paper_files/paper/2012/hash/46489c17893dfdcf028883202cefd6d1-Abstract.html)
  - Relevance **medium**; role: component prior art; components: item difficulty/IRT
  - Proposes a minimax entropy principle: maximise entropy over a worker-item-label distribution to infer item confusability and worker expertise, then minimise it to infer ground truth (equivalently minimising KL to the unknown truth), optimised by coordinate descent.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Item confusability is a richer version of SpecDx's cell difficulty, and item-specific confusion is how ambiguity looks under single-truth models. SpecDx instead makes ambiguity an explicit two-valued truth.
- **[li2012truth]** Xian Li, Xin Luna Dong, Kenneth Lyons et al. (2012). *Truth finding on the deep web: is the problem solved?*. PVLDB (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A* (as VLDB)). [link](https://doi.org/10.14778/2535568.2448943)
  - Relevance **medium**; role: evaluation/benchmark
  - Measures the truthfulness of Deep Web data in the Stock and Flight domains, finds large inconsistency and some low-accuracy sources, and evaluates state-of-the-art fusion methods, analysing their strengths and limitations.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* It provides empirical evidence that 'trusted' sources are often inconsistent, and an evaluation template (fusion methods on real conflicting data) that SpecDx mirrors with LLM interpreters on specifications.
- **[karger2011iterative]** David R. Karger, Sewoong Oh, Devavrat Shah (2011). *Iterative Learning for Reliable Crowdsourcing Systems*. NeurIPS (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://papers.nips.cc/paper_files/paper/2011/hash/c667d53acd899a97a85de0c201ba99be-Abstract.html)
  - Relevance **medium**; role: foundation; components: active synthesis
  - Studies minimising the number of task assignments needed for a target reliability, and gives a task-assignment scheme and an iterative inference algorithm that beat majority voting and are asymptotically optimal against an oracle that knows worker reliabilities.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* The KOS algorithm is a standard simulation baseline (it is in crowd-kit). Budget-optimal assignment parallels SpecDx's pilot-then-committee escalation.
- **[raykar2010learning]** Vikas C. Raykar, Shipeng Yu, Linda H. Zhao et al. (2010). *Learning From Crowds*. JMLR (peer-reviewed, CORE2020 A*). [link](https://jmlr.org/papers/v11/raykar10a.html)
  - Relevance **medium**; role: foundation; components: anchored cell model
  - Gives a probabilistic EM approach for supervised learning from several annotators' noisy labels without a gold standard, jointly estimating annotator quality, the hidden true labels and a classifier, and outperforming majority voting.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* It is the canonical learning-from-crowds model. SpecDx shares the joint estimation of rater quality and latent truth, but it has no features or classifier, because the items are specification cells and the truth may be a set.
- **[ipeirotis2010quality]** Panagiotis G. Ipeirotis, Foster Provost, Jing Wang (2010). *Quality management on Amazon Mechanical Turk*. HCOMP (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 B). [link](https://doi.org/10.1145/1837885.1837906)
  - Relevance **medium**; role: component prior art; components: anchored cell model
  - Improves Dawid-Skene-style worker quality estimation by separating systematic bias (recoverable by relabelling) from error, incorporating cost-sensitive errors, and integrating unsupervised estimation with supervised gold data (from the S2 TLDR).
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Separating correctable bias from noise parallels SpecDx's distinction between consistent deviation (spec defect) and scattered error (interpreter error). It also mixes gold and unsupervised data like SpecDx's anchors.
- **[welinder2010multidimensional]** Peter Welinder, Steve Branson, Pietro Perona et al. (2010). *The Multidimensional Wisdom of Crowds*. NeurIPS (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://papers.nips.cc/paper_files/paper/2010/hash/0f9cafd014db7a619ddb4276af0d692c-Abstract.html)
  - Relevance **medium**; role: component prior art; components: family effects/correlated errors, item difficulty/IRT
  - Models images in an abstract Euclidean space and annotators as multidimensional entities with competence, expertise and bias, recovering groups of annotators with different skills ('schools of thought') and groups of images that differ qualitatively.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* 'Schools of thought' among annotators is the crowd analogue of several reasonable readings of a requirement (ambiguity) and of family-specific reading styles. SpecDx encodes ambiguity explicitly as A_c = {x, y}.
- **[reitsma2009review]** Johannes B. Reitsma, Anne W.S. Rutjes, Khalid S. Khan et al. (2009). *A review of solutions for diagnostic accuracy studies with an imperfect or missing reference standard*. J Clin Epidemiol (peer-reviewed). [link](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.jclinepi.2009.02.005)
  - Relevance **medium**; role: survey; components: anchored cell model
  - Systematically reviews solutions for diagnostic-accuracy studies whose reference standard is imperfect or missing, grouped into imputation or adjustment for missing references, correction for an imperfect reference, constructing a reference from multiple tests (rule, consensus or latent class), and validation against clinical outcomes.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* The specification is an imperfect reference standard. This review maps the classical options and shows that SpecDx belongs to the 'construct the reference by statistical modelling' group, with the imperfect reference kept as an anchor.
- **[sheng2008get]** Victor S. Sheng, Foster Provost, Panagiotis G. Ipeirotis (2008). *Get another label? improving data quality and data mining using multiple, noisy labelers*. KDD (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.1145/1401890.1401965)
  - Relevance **medium**; role: context; foundation; also in t7_active_testing_design; components: active synthesis
  - Studies repeated labeling with multiple noisy labelers and shows that, when labels are imperfect, selectively acquiring more labels for chosen items is a valuable strategy (from the S2 TLDR).
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* It is early evidence that redundant fallible labels plus selective acquisition pay off. SpecDx's sequential escalation (pilot, then full committee) is a targeted version of 'get another label'. | Justifies SpecDx's sequential escalation: run one model per family as a pilot and spend the full committee only on cells whose pilot readings are uncertain. SpecDx adds an anchor (the declared value) and seeks specification defects rather than training labels.
- **[yin2008truth]** Xiaoxin Yin, Jiawei Han, P.S. Yu (2008). *Truth Discovery with Multiple Conflicting Information Providers on the Web*. TKDE (peer-reviewed, CORE2020 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.1109/tkde.2007.190745)
  - Relevance **medium**; role: foundation; components: anchored cell model
  - Defines the Veracity problem, finding true facts among conflicting information that many websites provide, and proposes TruthFinder, which exploits mutual reinforcement: a site is trustworthy if it provides many true facts, and a fact is likely true if many trustworthy sites provide it. It finds true facts and trustworthy sites better than popular search engines (journal version of the KDD 2007 paper, DOI 10.1145/1281192.1281309).
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* It is the founding paper of truth discovery in data integration: sources are like LLM interpreters and facts are like cell values. SpecDx adds an anchor source (the spec) whose disagreements are themselves the diagnostic target.
- **[kruskal1977three]** Joseph B. Kruskal (1977). *Three-way arrays: rank and uniqueness of trilinear decompositions, with application to arithmetic complexity and statistics*. LAA (peer-reviewed, ERA2010 A). [link](https://doi.org/10.1016/0024-3795(77)90069-6)
  - Relevance **medium**; role: foundation; components: identifiability
  - Defines rank for three-way arrays and proves lower bounds on it and conditions under which a trilinear (triad) decomposition is unique, with applications to arithmetic complexity and statistics.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Kruskal uniqueness underlies identifiability of Dawid-Skene-type models with three conditionally independent raters. SpecDx cites it as the classical identifiability path that its accuracy-gradient and anchor-based arguments replace.
- **[tu2025ranked]** Weijie Tu, Weijian Deng, Dylan Campbell et al. (2025). *Ranked from Within: Ranking Large Multimodal Models Without Labels*. ICML (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://proceedings.mlr.press/v267/tu25a.html)
  - Relevance **low**; role: context; components: baseline B2
  - Studies whether large multimodal models can be ranked without labels, evaluating 47 models on 9 VQA benchmarks, and finds softmax-based uncertainty scores give robust unsupervised rankings.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* It is label-free model assessment, relevant to choosing SpecDx's 'best single interpreter' (B2) without gold.
- **[ok2016optimality]** Jungseul Ok, Sewoong Oh, Jinwoo Shin et al. (2016). *Optimality of Belief Propagation for Crowdsourced Classification*. ICML (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://proceedings.mlr.press/v48/ok16.html)
  - Relevance **low**; role: foundation; components: identifiability
  - Proves that belief propagation exactly matches a tightened fundamental limit for Dawid-Skene label recovery when each worker gets at most two tasks, and dominates other provable algorithms in general.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* It is theory context for optimal inference under the DS model.
- **[liu2012variational]** Qiang Liu, Jian Peng, Alex Ihler (2012). *Variational Inference for Crowdsourcing*. NeurIPS (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://papers.nips.cc/paper_files/paper/2012/hash/cd00692c3bfe59267d5ecfac5310286c-Abstract.html)
  - Relevance **low**; role: component prior art; components: anchored cell model
  - Casts crowd label aggregation as graphical-model inference and applies BP and mean field. BP generalises majority vote and KOS, and MF relates to EM. Performance hinges on the prior over worker reliability.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* It shows the prior matters, as do SpecDx's Dirichlet error-pool prior and MAP fitting. It is a possible alternative inference engine.
- **[cour2011learning]** Timothee Cour, Ben Sapp, Ben Taskar (2011). *Learning from Partial Labels*. JMLR (peer-reviewed, CORE2020 A*). [link](https://jmlr.org/papers/v12/cour11a.html)
  - Relevance **low**; role: context; components: set-valued truth
  - Studies multiclass learning when each instance has a candidate set of labels of which only one is correct, characterises when learning is possible via a data-distribution property, and gives a convex learning formulation.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Set-valued supervision with one hidden true label is related to, but opposite from, SpecDx's set-valued truth, where every element of A_c is genuinely entailed.
- **[snow2008cheap]** Rion Snow, Brendan O'Connor, Daniel Jurafsky et al. (2008). *Cheap and fast---but is it good?: evaluating non-expert annotations for natural language tasks*. EMNLP (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.3115/1613715.1613751)
  - Relevance **low**; role: context
  - Shows on five NLP tasks that Mechanical Turk non-expert annotations agree well with expert gold labels, and proposes a bias-correction technique that improves aggregate annotation quality.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* It is the historical precedent for replacing an expert with a crowd of cheap fallible annotators. SpecDx makes the same move with local LLMs and uses the crowd to audit the 'gold' itself.

<a id="t2_irt_benchmark_audit"></a>
## IRT, benchmark auditing, label errors and benchmark repair

- **[wang2026solved]** You Wang, Michael Pradel, Zhongxin Liu (2026). *Are “Solved Issues” in SWE-bench Really Solved Correctly? An Empirical Study*. ICSE (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.1145/3744916.3764576)
  - Relevance **high**; role: evaluation/benchmark; component prior art; also in t4_spec_defects_oracles, t9_agent_failure_attribution; components: defect taxonomy, refutation, real-defect benchmark
  - Introduces PatchDiff, differential patch testing that exposes behavioural differences between a generated patch and the ground-truth patch, and finds on SWE-bench Verified that 7.8% of 'correct' patches fail the developer test suite and 29.6% of plausible patches behave differently from the ground truth.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Evidence and a technique for the weak-check class in a top SE venue: differential testing produces a witness input where a passing behaviour diverges from the intended one, similar in spirit to SpecDx's witness-based refutation; it requires a reference patch, whereas SpecDx works from the NL specification and an executor population. | Quantifies weak checks in an agent benchmark and uses behavioural witnesses (differentiating inputs), similar in spirit to SpecDx's witness-backed refutation. | Differential testing produces witnesses that a check is too weak (a passing behaviour that differs from intended), the analogue of SpecDx's weak-check refutation; it compares to a single reference patch rather than to the consensus of an interpreter population.
- **[land2026auditing]** Sander Land, Daniel M. Bikel (2026). *Auditing LLM Benchmarks with Item Response Theory*. arXiv (preprint). [link](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.30504) · **baseline candidate**
  - Relevance **high**; role: direct competitor; also in t1_truth_inference; components: item difficulty/IRT, identifiability, baseline B3, real-defect benchmark, defect taxonomy, anchored cell model
  - Introduces an IRT mislabel indicator, fitted on responses of 114 models, that surfaces likely mislabels at 95% precision in the top 200 items across seven preference and multiple-choice benchmarks (beating a supervised classifier); the indicator is a forced-ceiling likelihood ratio from a constrained 4PL fit that asks whether stronger models become increasingly correct or increasingly incorrect on an item. Errors are traced to mechanical labelling heuristics, inherited upstream annotation mistakes and inherently ambiguous items. Accepted at EMNLP 2026 (arXiv comment); flagged items released on Hugging Face (Writer/IRT-mislabeled-items).
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* The closest 'statistics-only' competitor and the published counterpart of B3: a population of models plus IRT flags items whose key is wrong, using the same 'accuracy gradient' intuition as SpecDx (does correctness rise or fall with model ability?), and it notes that the indicator needs enough ability spread for θ to be identifiable. It treats each item as single-label with a fixed key, gives a ranked untyped flag without localisation or witnesses, and has no logic layer (no MUS/MCS, contradiction, weak-check detection or refutation). | It is the closest competitor for the statistical half of SpecDx: a population of models audits a fallible answer key and finds both mis-keys and ambiguous items. It differs in four ways: it works on static QA labels rather than executable specifications, has no set-valued truth or anchor-as-rater model, has no MUS/MCS contradiction analysis, and gives no refutation witnesses or localisation in T. It is the published counterpart of baseline B3.
- **[wang2026automated]** Junlin Wang, Federico Bianchi, Shang Zhu et al. (2026). *Automated Benchmark Auditing for AI Agents and Large Language Models*. arXiv (preprint). [link](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.26079) · [code](https://github.com/IsThatYou/auto-bench-audit) ★17 · **baseline candidate**
  - Relevance **high**; role: direct competitor; also in t9_agent_failure_attribution; components: defect taxonomy, real-defect benchmark, baseline B1
  - Auto Benchmark Audit (ABA) is an agentic LLM framework that audits individual benchmark tasks for hidden environment dependencies, specification gaps and limited grading logic; over 168 benchmarks in nine domains it flags critical issues (ambiguous design, environment conflicts, incorrect ground truth) in over 25.7% of tasks, validated by experts and upstream PRs, and filtering them shifts rankings and raises SWE-bench Verified and Terminal-Bench 2 scores by about 10%.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Fully automatic detection of specification gaps, ambiguity and wrong ground truth in benchmarks, so it threatens SpecDx's 'automatic' claim; but it is a single (frontier) LLM auditor reasoning over artefacts, i.e. a strong version of static review B1, with no executor-population statistics, no identifiability argument and no logical refutation or witnesses. | Directly targets specification and check defects in agent tasks, automatically, and validates against upstream fixes. ABA is a per-task LLM agent audit; SpecDx instead infers defects from the statistics of many fallible executors and confirms them with logical witnesses, and it types defects by satisfaction (contradiction, hidden requirement, ambiguity, weak check).
- **[cao2026policy]** Hongliu Cao (2026). *Policy Loopholes in Agent Evaluation: When Policy Ambiguity Masquerades as Agent Error*. arXiv (preprint). [link](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.14400)
  - Relevance **high**; role: direct competitor; components: defect taxonomy, real-defect benchmark, family effects/correlated errors
  - Argues that agent benchmarks wrongly assume each natural-language policy determines a unique correct action; audits two τ²-bench domains, builds a taxonomy of policy loopholes (silence, ambiguity, contradiction), and shows that affected tasks lower scores in model-specific ways and reduce every model's consistency across trials, with exploitability requiring both policy ambiguity and tool permissiveness. Accepted at the REALM workshop at EMNLP 2026 (arXiv comment).
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Directly threatens the 'ambiguity/contradiction in T' part of SpecDx's taxonomy on the same τ²-bench source, and uses cross-model inconsistency as its symptom. It is a manual audit with a qualitative taxonomy; SpecDx detects and types such defects automatically, localises the ambiguous sentences by ddmin, and certifies contradictions with MUS/MCS and witness states.
- **[yu2026swe]** Boxi Yu, Yang Cao, Yuzhong Zhang et al. (2026). *SWE-ABS: Adversarial Benchmark Strengthening Exposes Inflated Success Rates on Test-based Benchmark*. arXiv (preprint). [link](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.00520)
  - Relevance **high**; role: evaluation/benchmark; components: defect taxonomy, mutation evaluation, real-defect benchmark, refutation
  - SWE-ABS strengthens SWE-bench test suites by coverage-driven augmentation with program slicing and by mutation-driven adversarial testing that synthesises plausible but wrong patches; on SWE-bench Verified it strengthens 50.2% of instances, rejects 19.71% of previously passing patches, and drops the top agent from 78.80% to 62.20%.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Shows at scale that one in five 'solved' patches pass only because the checks are weak, i.e. SpecDx's weak-check class, and uses synthesised wrong behaviours as kill witnesses much like SpecDx's mutation/refutation step; it repairs code-benchmark tests given a reference patch, while SpecDx diagnoses weak clauses in business specifications without a reference implementation.
- **[nahum2025llms]** Omer Nahum, Nitay Calderon, Orgad Keller et al. (2025). *Are LLMs Better than Reported? Detecting Label Errors and Mitigating Their Effect on Model Performance*. EMNLP (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.18653/v1/2025.emnlp-main.1360) · **baseline candidate**
  - Relevance **high**; role: direct competitor; component prior art; also in t1_truth_inference; components: anchored cell model, family effects/correlated errors, baseline B4, baseline B2, real-defect benchmark
  - Uses an ensemble of LLMs (LLM-as-a-judge with diverse prompts and confidence scores) to flag likely mislabeled examples in four TRUE factual-consistency datasets and SummEval, compares expert, crowd and LLM annotation, and finds substantial label errors whose correction raises reported model performance; larger ensembles and high-confidence disagreement with the gold label are stronger error signals.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* The nearest label-level analogue of SpecDx's core idea: a population of LLMs disagreeing confidently with the given label flags the label, not the models. It assumes one correct label per item, uses LLMs as judges rather than as executors of a specification, and has no model of interpreter accuracy, item difficulty, set-valued truth or logical refutation. | An LLM committee auditing a dataset's given labels (a fallible anchor) is SpecDx's setting restricted to static labels. Its confidence-thresholded ensemble vote is a simple baseline for SpecDx's mis-key detection.
- **[gema2025done]** Aryo Pradipta Gema, Joshua Ong Jun Leang, Giwon Hong et al. (2025). *Are We Done with MMLU?*. NAACL (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A). [link](https://doi.org/10.18653/v1/2025.naacl-long.262)
  - Relevance **high**; role: evaluation/benchmark; components: defect taxonomy, set-valued truth, real-defect benchmark
  - Analyses ground-truth errors in MMLU with a hierarchical error-annotation protocol (bad question clarity, bad options clarity, no correct answer, multiple correct answers, wrong ground truth), finds e.g. 57% of analysed Virology questions erroneous, builds MMLU-Redux (5,700 manually re-annotated questions across 57 subjects), estimates 6.49% of MMLU questions contain errors, and shows that model metrics shift on the corrected set.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Its error taxonomy maps onto SpecDx's classes for a single-answer benchmark: wrong ground truth ≈ mis-key, multiple correct answers ≈ ambiguity (set-valued truth), no correct answer ≈ contradiction/empty entailment set. Detection is manual re-annotation, which SpecDx replaces with an executor population and logic.
- **[yao2025bench]** Shunyu Yao, Noah Shinn, Pedram Razavi et al. (2025). *τ-bench: A Benchmark for Tool-Agent-User Interaction in Real-World Domains*. ICLR (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://proceedings.iclr.cc/paper_files/paper/2025/hash/1b126cc38b8638e07bef37e7b2bb72bf-Abstract-Conference.html) · [code](https://github.com/sierra-research/tau-bench) ★1467
  - Relevance **high**; role: evaluation/benchmark; also in t8_bpm_agents_policies, t9_agent_failure_attribution; components: real-defect benchmark, defect taxonomy
  - τ-bench emulates conversations between an LLM-simulated user and a tool-using agent bound by domain policies (retail, airline), scores by comparing the final database state with an annotated goal state, and introduces pass^k for reliability; gpt-4o-class agents solve under 50% of tasks with pass^8 under 25% in retail.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* A prototypical executable business specification S = ⟨T, C, Π⟩: policies and tool descriptions are T, the golden end state is C, the tasks are Π. SpecDx treats this S as fallible, which later repairs (τ-Bench Verified, τ²-Bench-Verified, ABC's findings) confirm. | τ-bench is one of SpecDx's subjects: its policy text is T and its goal-state/expected-action checks are C. τ-bench treats S as ground truth; SpecDx asks when the goal state itself is defective, and its repeated-trial (pass^k) runs are exactly the population of executions SpecDx mines. | tau-bench is a direct instance of S = <T, C, Pi> (policy text, golden end state, scenarios) whose defects SpecDx targets; pass^k measures agent consistency but treats every repeated failure as the agent's, which is exactly the confound SpecDx separates.
- **[vendrow2025large]** Joshua Vendrow, Edward Vendrow, Sara Beery et al. (2025). *Do Large Language Model Benchmarks Test Reliability?*. arXiv (preprint). [link](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.03461) · [code](https://github.com/MadryLab/platinum-benchmarks) ★42
  - Relevance **high**; role: evaluation/benchmark; components: defect taxonomy, real-defect benchmark, set-valued truth
  - Argues that LLM benchmarks do not measure reliability because pervasive label errors and ambiguity hide genuine model failures; proposes 'platinum' benchmarks curated to minimise label errors and ambiguity, revises examples from fifteen popular benchmarks, and finds that frontier LLMs still fail simple tasks such as elementary math word problems.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Repairs benchmark items by flagging those where models disagree with the label and then manually removing mislabeled or ambiguous ones, i.e. a model-population-assisted manual audit; SpecDx automates the diagnosis, types the defect and keeps ambiguous items as set-valued truth instead of deleting them.
- **[cuadron2025saber]** Alejandro Cuadron, Pengfei Yu, Yang Liu et al. (2025). *SABER: Small Actions, Big Errors -- Safeguarding Mutating Steps in LLM Agents*. arXiv (preprint). [link](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.07850) · [code](https://github.com/amazon-agi/tau2-bench-verified) ★55
  - Relevance **high**; role: evaluation/benchmark; components: real-defect benchmark, defect taxonomy
  - Analyses τ-Bench (airline/retail) and SWE-bench Verified traces, finds that deviations in mutating (state-changing) actions drive failure, and proposes a test-time safeguard (SABER); it also identifies ceiling effects in τ-Bench caused by annotation errors and underspecified tasks and releases τ-Bench Verified with targeted revisions. This is the paper cited by the amazon-agi/tau2-bench-verified release, whose FIXES.md lists policy-compliance, database-accuracy, logical-consistency and evaluation-ambiguity fixes.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* The source of SpecDx's real-defect set (the τ²-Bench-Verified diff). The repairs were found manually from failure analysis; SpecDx aims to find the same contradictions, hidden requirements and ambiguities automatically.
- **[klie2023annotation]** Jan-Christoph Klie, Bonnie Webber, Iryna Gurevych (2023). *Annotation Error Detection: Analyzing the Past and Present for a More Coherent Future*. CL (peer-reviewed, CORE2020 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.1162/coli_a_00464) · [code](https://github.com/UKPLab/nessie) ★49 · **baseline candidate**
  - Relevance **high**; role: survey; also in t1_truth_inference; components: baseline B3, anchored cell model, real-defect benchmark, baseline B2
  - Reimplements 18 annotation error detection methods and evaluates them on 9 English datasets for text classification and token/span labelling, defines a uniform formalisation, evaluation protocol and best practices for the task, and releases the datasets and implementations as an open-source package (nessie).
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* The standard survey and toolkit for detecting wrong labels in a corpus; every method assumes one correct label per instance and needs a trained model or repeated annotations, whereas SpecDx diagnoses typed defects of an executable specification from interpreter behaviour. | Detecting errors in gold annotations is the dataset-level twin of detecting defects in a specification's checks. Its taxonomy (variation-based, model-based, training-dynamics) helps position SpecDx's population-of-interpreters approach.
- **[liu2023your]** Jiawei Liu, Chunqiu Steven Xia, Yuyao Wang et al. (2023). *Is Your Code Generated by ChatGPT Really Correct? Rigorous Evaluation of Large Language Models for Code Generation*. NeurIPS (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.52202/075280-0943) · [code](https://github.com/evalplus/evalplus) ★1832
  - Relevance **high**; role: evaluation/benchmark; components: defect taxonomy, mutation evaluation, real-defect benchmark
  - EvalPlus augments code-generation benchmarks with large numbers of tests from LLM- and mutation-based input generation; extending HumanEval's tests 80× (HumanEval+) catches much previously undetected wrong code (pass@k drops by up to 19.3–28.9%) and reveals that test insufficiency mis-ranks models.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Canonical evidence of the 'weak check' defect class: the benchmark's checks C are looser than its NL task T, so wrong programs pass.   - *Relation to SpecDx:* The natural runnable implementation of baseline B3; it fits a single binary response matrix against a fixed key and has no notion of a fallible anchor, set-valued truth or defect type.

[link](https://datasets-benchmarks-proceedings.neurips.cc/paper_files/paper/2021/hash/f2217062e9a397a1dca429e7d70bc6ca-Abstract-round1.html) · [code](https://github.com/cleanlab/label-errors) ★190

  - *Relation to SpecDx:* The canonical demonstration that benchmark 'ground truth' is itself defective, the premise SpecDx extends from labels to executable specifications; detection relies on a trained classifier's probabilities and human confirmation rather than an executor population with witnesses.
- **[plumed2019item]** Fernando Martínez-Plumed, Ricardo B.C. Prudêncio, Adolfo Martínez-Usó et al. (2019). *Item response theory in AI: Analysing machine learning classifiers at the instance level*. AIJ (peer-reviewed, CORE2020 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.artint.2018.09.004)

  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Foundational instance-level IRT in AI and an early observation that negative-discrimination instances signal noisy or anomalous items; SpecDx's per-cell difficulty β_g plays the same role, but SpecDx adds an explicit fallible anchor, a set-valued truth and an error distribution over wrong values.
- **[lalor2019learning]** John P. Lalor, Hao Wu, Hong Yu (2019). *Learning Latent Parameters without Human Response Patterns: Item Response Theory with Artificial Crowds*. EMNLP (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.18653/v1/d19-1434)

- **[lalor2016building]** John Lalor, Hao Wu, hong yu (2016). *Building an Evaluation Scale using Item Response Theory*. EMNLP (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.18653/v1/d16-1062)

  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Seminal import of IRT into NLP evaluation that SpecDx's σ(α_m − β_g) term and baseline B3 descend from; it treats the gold key as correct and models only respondent ability and item parameters, whereas SpecDx treats the specification's declared value as a fallible anchor with set-valued truth.
- **[bock1972estimating]** R. Darrell Bock (1972). *Estimating Item Parameters and Latent Ability when Responses are Scored in Two or More Nominal Categories*. Psychometrika (peer-reviewed). [link](https://doi.org/10.1007/bf02291411)

- **[li2026adaptive]** Peiyu Li, Xiuxiu Tang, Si Chen et al. (2026). *Adaptive Testing for LLM Evaluation: A Psychometric Alternative to Static Benchmarks*. ICML (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://proceedings.mlr.press/v306/li26fx.html) · [code](https://github.com/Peiyu-Georgia-Li/ATLAS) ★9

- **[agarwal2026efficient]** Tanish Agarwal, Kaustubh Shivshankar Shejole, Arpit Agarwal (2026). *An Efficient Joint Learning Approach for Item Response Theory*. ICML (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://proceedings.mlr.press/v306/agarwal26d.html)

- **[dong2026benchmarks]** Zihan Dong, Zhiyuan Ma, Zekun Wang et al. (2026). *How Benchmarks Mis-Score Computer-Use Agents*. arXiv (preprint). [link](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.28367)

- **[zhou2026lost]** Hongli Zhou, Hui Huang, Ziqing Zhao et al. (2026). *Lost in Benchmarks? Rethinking Large Language Model Benchmarking with Item Response Theory*. AAAI (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.1609/aaai.v40i41.40814)

- **[bock1981marginal]** R. Darrell Bock, Murray Aitkin (1981). *Marginal Maximum Likelihood Estimation of Item Parameters: Application of an EM Algorithm*. Psychometrika (peer-reviewed). [link](https://doi.org/10.1007/bf02293801)

  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Treats LLMs as measurement instruments whose reliability is estimated with IRT, analogous to SpecDx estimating interpreter accuracy α_m; it audits the judges, not the specification.
- **[lalor2018understanding]** John Lalor, Hao Wu, Tsendsuren Munkhdalai et al. (2018). *Understanding Deep Learning Performance through an Examination of Test Set Difficulty: A Psychometric Case Study*. EMNLP (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.18653/v1/d18-1500)
  - Relevance **low**; role: context; components: item difficulty/IRT
  - Models test-item difficulty with IRT fitted to human response patterns and shows on NLI and sentiment analysis that DNN correctness depends on item difficulty, with easy items learned faster as training data grows.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Supports the premise that item difficulty modulates model correctness (SpecDx's β_g term); it does not address key errors.
- **[martin2017identification]** Ernesto San Martín (2017). *Identification of Item Response Theory Models*. Handbook of IRT (book). [link](https://doi.org/10.1201/b19166-8)
  - Relevance **low**; role: foundation; components: identifiability
  - Handbook chapter on the identification problems of binary IRT models, arguing that each model should be applied to data whose design satisfies the identification restrictions.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Background reference for the identifiability conditions SpecDx states for its anchored cell model.
- **[kolen2004test]** Michael J. Kolen, Robert L. Brennan (2004). *Test Equating, Scaling, and Linking*. Springer (book). [link](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-1-4757-4310-4)
  - Relevance **low**; role: foundation; components: identifiability
  - Standard reference book on test equating, scaling and linking, covering equating designs with common (anchor) items, IRT linking methods, standard errors of equating and practical issues.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Background for the anchor-item and identifiability vocabulary SpecDx borrows; SpecDx does not equate test forms but uses the declared specification value as an anchor in a truth-inference model.
- **[dickinson2003detecting]** Markus Dickinson, W. Detmar Meurers (2003). *Detecting errors in part-of-speech annotation*. EACL (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A). [link](https://doi.org/10.3115/1067807.1067823)
  - Relevance **low**; role: foundation
  - Seminal method for detecting errors in gold-standard part-of-speech annotation by locating n-grams that occur in the corpus with multiple taggings (variation n-grams), plus closed-class analysis and finite-state tagging guide patterns, illustrated on the Penn Treebank WSJ corpus.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Founding 'inconsistency signals error' idea in AED; SpecDx inverts it, treating consistent deviation of many interpreters from the specification as the defect signal.

<a id="t3_requirements_nlp"></a>
## Requirements quality: ambiguity, incompleteness, inconsistency (NLP4RE/LLM4RE)

- **[nair2026retrieval]** Pavithra PM Nair, Preethu Rose Anish (2026). *A Retrieval-Augmented Framework for Detecting and Resolving Pragmatic Ambiguities in Natural Language Requirements*. arXiv (preprint). [link](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04436) · **baseline candidate**
  - Relevance **high**; role: direct competitor; components: baseline B4, baseline B1
  - Detects pragmatic ambiguity in NL requirements by simulating stakeholders of novice, intermediate and expert domain knowledge with retrieval-augmented LLMs and flagging discrepancies between their interpretations; evaluated on two PURE documents with GPT-4o-mini, Mistral-7B, Llama-3.1-8B and Qwen2.5-7B.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Shares the idea of a population of simulated readers whose disagreement signals ambiguity. It compares stated interpretations rather than executed behaviour, has no arbiter C, no model of reader accuracy, and covers only ambiguity.
- **[vijayvargiya2026ambig]** Sanidhya Vijayvargiya, Xuhui Zhou, Akhila Yerukola et al. (2026). *Ambig-SWE: Interactive Agents to Overcome Underspecificity in Software Engineering*. ICLR (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://iclr.cc/virtual/2026/poster/10009007)
  - Relevance **high**; role: evaluation/benchmark; also in t6_llm_disagreement_ambiguity; components: mutation evaluation, defect taxonomy
  - Ambig-SWE is an underspecified variant of SWE-Bench Verified for evaluating whether agents detect underspecificity, ask targeted clarification questions and use the answers; models struggle to distinguish well-specified from underspecified tasks, but interaction improves results by up to 74%.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Seeds underspecification into an agent benchmark derived by human verification, close to SpecDx's seeded-defect evaluation on τ²-style tasks; it tests agents' reactions rather than diagnosing the task specification. | Shows that single agents cannot reliably detect underspecification on their own, which motivates SpecDx's population-level detection; its underspecified SWE-bench variants are seeded 'hidden requirement' defects that could serve as external evaluation material. Accepted at ICLR 2026 according to the arXiv comment.
- **[hall2026neurosymbolic]** Bethel Hall, William Eiers (2026). *Neurosymbolic Auditing of Natural-Language Software Requirements*. arXiv (preprint). [link](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.13817) · **baseline candidate**
  - Relevance **high**; role: direct competitor; components: baseline B1, baseline B4, baseline B5, refutation
  - VERIMED pairs LLMs with an SMT solver to audit medical-device software requirements: ambiguity is detected from stochastic variation across independent LLM formalisations, and inconsistency, vacuity and safety violations are exposed by solver queries; counterexample feedback raises verified accuracy from 55.4% to 98.5% on a hemodialysis benchmark.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Threatens the novelty claim most directly in this theme: it uses disagreement among several LLM samples as the ambiguity signal and logic (SMT, counterexamples) for inconsistency. Differences: its samples are formalisations of T by one model rather than executions by a multi-family population, there is no accuracy/difficulty model to separate interpreter error from genuine ambiguity, no anchored checks C, and no Hidden-requirement or Weak-check types.
- **[richter2026underspecification]** Cedric Richter, Mike Papadakis (2026). *Underspecification does not imply Incoherence: The Risks of Semantic Collapse in Coding Models*. arXiv (preprint). [link](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.01953)
  - Relevance **high**; role: context; component prior art; also in t6_llm_disagreement_ambiguity; components: identifiability, family effects/correlated errors, anchored cell model, baseline B4
  - Shows that underspecified task descriptions do not necessarily produce incoherent (divergent) samples: LLMs often collapse onto one coherent but wrong interpretation ('detrimental semantic collapse'), affecting over 10% of MBPP, 3% of HumanEval and 32% of LiveCodeBench tasks, and more than five times as often when underspecification is injected.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Directly relevant to SpecDx's identification assumptions: sampling disagreement alone (ClarifyGPT, SpecFix, VERIMED, B4) misses ambiguity when interpreters collapse, whereas SpecDx also uses consistent deviation from the anchor C (Hidden requirement) and across-family diversity. It also warns that correlated readings can make an agent error look like a spec defect. | Strong evidence that disagreement-only detectors (ClarifyGPT's consistency check, semantic entropy, SpecDx's B4) miss ambiguity when interpreters collapse; SpecDx's anchor d_c and cross-family population are designed to catch exactly this case (consensus that contradicts the specification's checks), so this paper motivates the anchored cell model.
- **[cunha2026validating]** Alcino Cunha, Nuno Macedo (2026). *Validating Formal Specifications with LLM-Generated Test Cases*. FM (book, CORE2023 A). [link](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-032-26204-2_15) · **baseline candidate**
  - Relevance **high**; role: component prior art; components: refutation, defect taxonomy, baseline B1
  - Empirical study of LLMs (mainly GPT-5) generating positive and negative test cases from NL requirements to validate Alloy formalisations of domain-model structural requirements; the generated tests are largely correct and expose many flawed specifications written by human developers.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Shares SpecDx's T-versus-C setting: NL requirements, an executable/formal specification, and LLM-generated witness instances that a flawed formal specification wrongly accepts (Weak check) or rejects (Hidden requirement). It relies on one LLM's reading as the reference and assumes T is right, whereas SpecDx treats both T and C as fallible and uses a population of interpreters.
- **[jia2025automated]** Haoxiang Jia, Robbie Morris, He Ye et al. (2025). *Automated Repair of Ambiguous Problem Descriptions for LLM-Based Code Generation*. ASE (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.1109/ase63991.2025.00038) · [code](https://github.com/msv-lab/SpecFix) ★9 · **baseline candidate**
  - Relevance **high**; role: direct competitor; also in t6_llm_disagreement_ambiguity; components: baseline B4, set-valued truth, anchored cell model, ddmin
  - SpecFix treats ambiguity in a problem description as uncertainty in the distribution of programs an LLM generates: it analyses that distribution by testing against the input-output examples, repairs it, and then refines the description by contrastive specification inference so that the LLM's interpretation aligns with the examples; it modifies 43.6% of descriptions and raises Pass@1 on them by 30.9% across four LLMs and three benchmarks.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Threatens the novelty claim for Ambiguity and for 'checks as arbiter': it executes a distribution of sampled interpretations and uses executable examples (a small C) to judge them, then edits T. Differences: interpretations come from one model at a time (no multi-family identification of reader error), the unit is a single function description, defects are not typed (no Contradiction/Hidden requirement/Weak check), there is no MUS/witness reasoning, and the goal is repair for code generation rather than diagnosis of a benchmark specification. | Threatens part of the novelty claim: fully automatic, uses the distribution of LLM interpretations of an NL specification, and anchors it to executable examples (the analogue of SpecDx's checks C) to detect and repair ambiguity. It differs in being single-model, treating the examples as ground truth (SpecDx treats C as a fallible rater and can blame it: hidden requirement, weak check, contradiction), having no executor-reliability model or witnesses, and repairing rather than localising and typing defects.
- **[pawagi2025rfcscope]** Mrigank Pawagi, Lize Shao, Hyeonmin Lee et al. (2025). *RFCScope: Detecting Logical Ambiguities in Internet Protocol Specifications*. ASE (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.1109/ase63991.2025.00106) · **baseline candidate**
  - Relevance **high**; role: component prior art; components: baseline B1, defect taxonomy, real-defect benchmark
  - Studies 11 years of verified technical errata in Standards Track RFCs, identifying seven subtypes of logical ambiguity (including inconsistency and under-specification), and introduces RFCScope, an LLM pipeline with cross-document context, semantic partitioning, bug-type-aware prompts and reasoning-based false-positive filtering; it finds 31 new ambiguities in 14 RFCs, 8 confirmed by authors and 3 accepted as errata.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* The most advanced static LLM review of specifications (a strong B1 design: map-reduce over partitions plus validation), with a real-defect taxonomy mined from errata, analogous to SpecDx's real defects from TonomyX history and the τ²-Bench-Verified diff. It never executes the specification or uses checks as arbiter.
- **[gartner2024automated]** Alexander Elenga Gärtner, Dietmar Göhlich (2024). *Automated requirement contradiction detection through formal logic and LLMs*. ASE journal (peer-reviewed, CORE2020 B). [link](https://doi.org/10.1007/s10515-024-00452-x) · **baseline candidate**
  - Relevance **high**; role: component prior art; components: defect taxonomy, baseline B1, baseline B5
  - ALICE detects and classifies contradictions in requirements written in controlled natural language by combining formal logic with LLMs, guided by an expanded contradiction taxonomy and a seven-question decision tree; it detects 60% of all contradictions and clearly outperforms an LLM-only approach.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Closest NL-requirements counterpart of SpecDx's Contradiction type with logic plus LLMs, but contradictions are sought among requirement statements of T, not as unsatisfiability of executable checks C over behaviours, and nothing is executed or refuted with a witness.
- **[rahman2024cellularlint]** Mirza Masfiqur Rahman, Imtiaz Karim, Elisa Bertino (2024). *CellularLint: A Systematic Approach to Identify Inconsistent Behavior in Cellular Network Specifications*. USENIX Security (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://www.usenix.org/conference/usenixsecurity24/presentation/rahman)
  - Relevance **high**; role: component prior art; components: baseline B1
  - CellularLint is a semi-automatic framework that detects inconsistencies in 4G/5G standards using few-shot learning on LLMs domain-adapted to cellular protocol text; on NAS and security specifications it uncovers 157 inconsistencies with 82.67% accuracy, validated on open-source implementations and commercial devices.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* A large-scale static LLM detector of contradictions within a standard (B1-style), with impact confirmed on implementations; SpecDx instead finds contradictions among executable checks and confirms them with witnesses, and separates spec defects from interpreter errors.
- **[mu2024clarifygpt]** Fangwen Mu, Lin Shi, Song Wang et al. (2024). *ClarifyGPT: A Framework for Enhancing LLM-Based Code Generation via Requirements Clarification*. FSE (PACMSE) (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A* (as FSE)). [link](https://doi.org/10.1145/3660810) · [code](https://github.com/ClarifyGPT/ClarifyGPT) ★32 · **baseline candidate**
  - Relevance **high**; role: direct competitor; also in t6_llm_disagreement_ambiguity; components: baseline B4, baseline B2, set-valued truth, active synthesis
  - ClarifyGPT detects whether a code-generation requirement is ambiguous by a code consistency check (sample several programs from the LLM, run them on generated test inputs, and call the requirement ambiguous if their outputs disagree), then asks targeted clarifying questions and regenerates code from the refined requirement.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Threatens the novelty claim for the Ambiguity type: it detects ambiguity by executing several sampled interpretations and measuring behavioural disagreement, which is SpecDx's core signal. Differences: one model's samples (no multi-family population or accuracy model, so model error and genuine ambiguity are conflated), no executable checks C as an arbiter, a single-function requirement rather than a multi-document specification, no localisation within T, and no Contradiction, Hidden-requirement or Weak-check types. | Uses exactly SpecDx's behavioural signal (sampled interpreters of one NL requirement diverge on the same input => the requirement admits several readings) to detect ambiguity automatically. It is single-model, has no anchor (it cannot detect a hidden requirement where all samples agree but disagree with the checks), does not separate agent error from ambiguity, does not localise the ambiguous sentence or type other defects, and resolves ambiguity through a (simulated) user.
- **[fazelnia2024translation]** Mohamad Fazelnia, Mehdi Mirakhorli, Hamid Bagheri (2024). *Translation Titans, Reasoning Challenges: Satisfiability-Aided Language Models for Detecting Conflicting Requirements*. ASE (NIER) (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A* (NIER track)). [link](https://doi.org/10.1145/3691620.3695302) · **baseline candidate**
  - Relevance **high**; role: component prior art; context; also in t5_diagnosis_mus_dd; components: baseline B5, baseline B1, MUS/MCS
  - SAT-LLM has an LLM translate NL requirements into SMT constraints and an SMT solver decide conflicts, outperforming ChatGPT-only conflict detection in early experiments.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Shares the LLM-plus-solver pattern for contradictions; SpecDx avoids relying on one LLM's formalisation of T: contradictions are found among the already-executable clauses C from sampled correction sets, and candidates are refuted by witness states. | LLM + solver for requirement conflicts, i.e. a combination of LLM and logic for specification defects. It uses one LLM as a translator and the solver for consistency only; SpecDx uses a population of LLM executors as statistical evidence and covers four defect types with witnesses.
- **[fantechi2023inconsistency]** Alessandro Fantechi, Stefania Gnesi, Lucia Passaro et al. (2023). *Inconsistency Detection in Natural Language Requirements using ChatGPT: a Preliminary Evaluation*. RE (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A). [link](https://doi.org/10.1109/re57278.2023.00045)
  - Relevance **high**; role: component prior art; components: baseline B1
  - Measures how well GPT-3.5 detects inconsistency defects in NL requirements, comparing its output with expert judgements on a few example requirements documents; early experiments indicate it finds inconsistencies that traditional NLP tools miss.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* The earliest direct form of B1 (static LLM review for contradictions). SpecDx's Contradiction is defined by unsatisfiability of C over behaviours and is confirmed by MUS candidates and witness-based refutation, not by an LLM's judgement on text.
- **[cosler2023nl2spec]** Matthias Cosler, Christopher Hahn, Daniel Mendoza et al. (2023). *nl2spec: Interactively Translating Unstructured Natural Language to Temporal Logics with Large Language Models*. CAV (book, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-031-37703-7_18) · [code](https://github.com/realChrisHahn2/nl2spec) ★66 · **baseline candidate**
  - Relevance **high**; role: component prior art; components: ddmin, baseline B1
  - nl2spec uses LLMs to translate unstructured natural language into temporal-logic formulas and detects and resolves ambiguity by mapping subformulas back to NL fragments, which users interactively add, delete or edit.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* A structural analogue: ambiguity is localised to NL fragments through their formal sub-translations. SpecDx localises ambiguous sentences of T automatically (ddmin with an LLM-population oracle) and judges readings by C, with no human in the loop.
- **[katis2022capture]** Andreas Katis, Anastasia Mavridou, Dimitra Giannakopoulou et al. (2022). *Capture, Analyze, Diagnose: Realizability Checking Of Requirements in FRET*. CAV (book, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-031-13188-2_24) · [code](https://github.com/NASA-SW-VnV/fret) ★489
  - Relevance **high**; role: component prior art; components: MUS/MCS, refutation, baseline B5
  - Presents FRET's realizability analysis framework for formalised requirements, with state-of-the-art algorithms over infinite theories and a diagnosis process that visualises conflicts between requirements and simulates counterexamples, applied to industrial case studies.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Conflict diagnosis by minimal conflicting requirement sets plus counterexamples is the formal counterpart of SpecDx's MUS candidates and witness refutation; SpecDx obtains candidates from sampled behaviours of LLM executors over database clauses rather than from temporal-logic requirements, and must also separate executor error.
- **[yen2021semi]** Jane Yen, Tamás Lévai, Qinyuan Ye et al. (2021). *Semi-automated protocol disambiguation and code generation*. SIGCOMM (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.1145/3452296.3472910)
  - Relevance **high**; role: component prior art; also in t6_llm_disagreement_ambiguity; components: defect taxonomy, ddmin, baseline B1
  - Sage parses RFC text with a CCG semantic parser and flags sentences with multiple logical forms (ambiguous) or that fail unit tests after code generation (under-specified); after the author clarifies them, it generates protocol code. On the ICMP RFC it finds 5 ambiguous and 6 under-specified sentences and then generates code interoperable with Linux.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* The strongest structural analogue: ambiguity = several readings of a sentence, under-specification exposed by executing generated code against tests, and sentence-level localisation. Sage uses one symbolic parser (readings = parses) and a human to resolve; SpecDx uses a population of LLM executors as readers, C as the test oracle, a statistical model to separate reader error, and automatic ddmin localisation. | Localises ambiguity to individual sentences of a natural-language specification by counting readings, as SpecDx's ddmin localisation does with an LLM-population oracle; Sage uses a semantic parser and human clarification rather than behavioural evidence and checks.
- **[femmer2017rapid]** Henning Femmer, Daniel Méndez Fernández, Stefan Wagner et al. (2017). *Rapid quality assurance with Requirements Smells*. JSS (peer-reviewed, CORE2020 A). [link](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.jss.2016.02.047)
  - Relevance **high**; role: component prior art; components: baseline B1, defect taxonomy
  - Transfers code smells to requirements as Requirements Smells and implements a lightweight static detector (Smella); in three industrial cases and one academic case it reaches on average 59% precision and 82% recall, as a supplement to reviews.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* The canonical static, rule-based reading of T for quality defects. SpecDx differs by detecting defects relative to C and to behaviour (e.g. a vague phrase matters only if executors diverge on a checked cell), and by distinguishing spec defects from reader error.
- **[chantree2006identifying]** Francis Chantree, Bashar Nuseibeh, Anne de Roeck et al. (2006). *Identifying Nocuous Ambiguities in Natural Language Requirements*. RE (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A). [link](https://doi.org/10.1109/re.2006.31)
  - Relevance **high**; role: foundation; components: defect taxonomy, set-valued truth
  - Introduces nocuous versus innocuous ambiguity: an ambiguity is nocuous when human readers disagree on its reading, judged from the distribution of multiple human interpretations, and heuristics are trained to predict this for coordination ambiguities in requirements.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* The closest conceptual ancestor of SpecDx's ambiguity signal: ambiguity is defined by disagreement in a population of readers. SpecDx replaces human judgements with a population of LLM executors and models reading accuracy, so that disagreement due to reader error is separated from genuine multiple entailment.
- **[gervasi2005reasoning]** Vincenzo Gervasi, Didar Zowghi (2005). *Reasoning about inconsistencies in natural language requirements*. TOSEM (peer-reviewed, CORE2020 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.1145/1072997.1072999)
  - Relevance **high**; role: foundation; components: baseline B5, defect taxonomy
  - Integrates NL parsing with nonmonotonic default reasoning to discover inconsistencies among requirements from multiple stakeholders, using theorem proving and model checking, implemented in the CARL prototype.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* The classic pipeline NL requirements -> logic -> automatic inconsistency discovery. SpecDx does not translate T into logic; it uses C as the logic and treats T only through the behaviour of interpreters.
- **[berry2003contract]** Daniel M. Berry, Erik Kamsties, Michael M. Krieger (2003). *From Contract Drafting to Software Specification: Linguistic Sources of Ambiguity*. Handbook (other). [link](https://cs.uwaterloo.ca/~dberry/handbook/ambiguityHandbook.pdf)
  - Relevance **high**; role: foundation; components: defect taxonomy
  - A handbook on unintended ambiguity in software requirements specifications and legal contracts, cataloguing linguistic sources (quantifiers such as all/each/every, only/also/even placement, and/or precedence, that/which, plurals, pronouns) with strategies for avoiding and detecting them. It frames ambiguity as text that admits several interpretations, so that different implementers build interfacing code under different assumptions.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Supplies SpecDx's definition of Ambiguity (T admits two or more readings) and the motivating mechanism (different implementers diverge). SpecDx operationalises it empirically: divergent readings are observed in executions by a population of interpreters rather than detected by a human inspector using linguistic patterns.
- **[yang2026clarity]** Di Yang, Xinou Xie, Xiuwen Yang et al. (2026). *Clarity Is Not Assumed: Understanding LLM-Based Code Generation under Ambiguous Requirements*. ASE (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.1145/3832783.3834403)
  - Relevance **medium**; role: evaluation/benchmark; components: defect taxonomy, mutation evaluation
  - Orchid is a benchmark of 1,304 function-level tasks with lexical, syntactic, semantic and vagueness ambiguity; ambiguity degrades all evaluated LLMs, most strongly the advanced ones, which frequently produce functionally divergent implementations and do not detect or resolve the ambiguity themselves.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Empirical support for SpecDx's premise that ambiguous text produces divergent executions; it measures the effect on interpreters rather than diagnosing the specification.
- **[broccia2026human]** Giovanna Broccia, Julian Frattini, Chetan Arora et al. (2026). *Human-AI Collaboration in Requirements Engineering: Evidence of the Negative Effect of LLMs on Requirements Inspection*. arXiv (preprint). [link](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.21298)
  - Relevance **medium**; role: evaluation/benchmark; components: baseline B1
  - Controlled experiment with 34 participants inspecting specifications with and without LLM support: LLM support lowers smell-detection accuracy, does not change classification or duration, and may impede learning of inspection skills.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Evidence that static LLM review of requirements (B1-style) is unreliable even with a human in the loop, supporting SpecDx's choice of behaviour-grounded, check-arbitrated diagnosis.
- **[larbi2026prompts]** Maya Larbi, Amal Akli, Mike Papadakis et al. (2026). *When Prompts Go Wrong: Evaluating Code Model Robustness to Ambiguous, Contradictory, and Incomplete Task Descriptions*. ICSE (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.1145/3744916.3773186)
  - Relevance **medium**; role: evaluation/benchmark; components: mutation evaluation, defect taxonomy
  - Extends code-generation benchmarks by systematically mutating task descriptions to be ambiguous, contradictory or incomplete, and finds that even minor flaws cause notable performance drops, contradictions most of all, with larger models more resilient but still vulnerable.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Uses specification mutation operators for ambiguity, contradiction and incompleteness, as SpecDx's evaluation does, but to stress interpreters instead of to evaluate a defect detector.
- **[berry2025detecting]** Daniel M. Berry, Erik Kamsties, Cristina Ribeiro et al. (2025). *Detecting Defects in Natural Language Requirements Specifications*. NLP4RE Handbook (book). [link](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-031-73143-3_5)
  - Relevance **medium**; role: survey; components: defect taxonomy, baseline B1
  - Handbook chapter on detecting defects (notably ambiguity) in natural-language requirements specifications.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Up-to-date overview of static defect detection in NL specifications, the family SpecDx's B1 belongs to.
- **[zadenoori2025large]** Mohammad Amin Zadenoori, Jacek Dąbrowski, Waad Alhoshan et al. (2025). *Large Language Models (LLMs) for Requirements Engineering (RE): A Systematic Literature Review*. arXiv (preprint). [link](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.11446)
  - Relevance **medium**; role: survey
  - Systematic literature review of 74 LLM4RE studies (2023-2024): most target elicitation and validation, use GPT models with zero/few-shot prompting, and are evaluated in controlled rather than industrial settings.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Maps LLM4RE validation work, all of which reviews requirement text statically; none executes a specification with a population of agents.
- **[vogelsang2025impact]** Andreas Vogelsang, Alexander Korn, Giovanna Broccia et al. (2025). *On the Impact of Requirements Smells in Prompts: The Case of Automated Traceability*. ICSE (NIER) (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A* (NIER track)). [link](https://doi.org/10.1109/icse-nier66352.2025.00016)

  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Evidence that textual smells do not uniformly change LLM behaviour, supporting SpecDx's choice to define defects by behavioural impact rather than by smell presence.
- **[bashir2025requirements]** Sarmad Bashir, Alessio Ferrari, Abbas Khan et al. (2025). *Requirements Ambiguity Detection and Explanation with LLMS: An Industrial Study*. ICSME (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A). [link](https://doi.org/10.1109/icsme64153.2025.00063)
  - Relevance **medium**; role: component prior art; components: baseline B1
  - Evaluates LLMs with in-context learning for detecting and explaining ambiguity in three industrial requirement datasets; 10-shot prompting improves classification by about 20% over zero-shot, and experts rate the generated explanations.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* A modern instance of B1 (static LLM review for ambiguity). SpecDx reports ambiguity only where readings change checked behaviour and gives the competing readings as executed alternatives.
- **[vogelsang2025using]** Andreas Vogelsang, Jannik Fischbach (2025). *Using Large Language Models for Natural Language Processing Tasks in Requirements Engineering: A Systematic Guideline*. NLP4RE Handbook (book). [link](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-031-73143-3_16)
  - Relevance **medium**; role: survey; components: baseline B1
  - Chapter introducing how LLMs work and giving a systematic guideline for selecting and adapting LLMs (prompting, fine-tuning, architectures) to NLP4RE tasks.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Methodological reference for designing the B1 static LLM-review baseline fairly.
- **[zheng2025validating]** Mingwei Zheng, Danning Xie, Qingkai Shi et al. (2025). *Validating Network Protocol Parsers with Traceable RFC Document Interpretation*. ISSTA (PACMSE) (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A* (as FSE)). [link](https://doi.org/10.1145/3728955)

  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Uses formal verification to repair LLM misreadings of a spec (interpreter error); SpecDx asks the inverse question of whether consistent misreadings are spec defects.
- **[ma2025bridging]** Zhi Ma, Cheng Wen, Zhexin Su et al. ASE (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.1109/ase63991.2025.00104)
  - Relevance **low**; role: context
  - Req2LTL translates industrial NL requirements into LTL via a hierarchical intermediate representation (OnionL), using LLMs for semantic decomposition and rule-based synthesis for validity; 88.4% semantic accuracy on aerospace requirements.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* State of the art in LLM requirement formalisation, which a VERIMED- or SAT-LLM-style baseline would build on.
- **[rong2025code]** Guoping Rong, Yongda Yu, Song Liu et al. (2025). *Code Comment Inconsistency Detection and Rectification Using a Large Language Model*. ICSE (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.1109/icse55347.2025.00035)

- **[oghabi2025multi]** Paniz Oghabi, Nafiseh Kahani (2025). *Multi-Label Ambiguity Detection in Software Requirements Using Language Models*. CASCON (peer-reviewed). [link](https://doi.org/10.1109/cascon66301.2025.00076)

- **[shen2025psmbench]** Zilin Shen, Xinyu Luo, Imtiaz Karim et al. (2025). *PSMBench: A Benchmark and Dataset for Evaluating LLMs Extraction of Protocol State Machines from RFC Specifications*. - **[xuan2024tool]** Zhen Xuan, Tianci Wang, Chunhui Wang et al. (2024). *A Tool for Automatically Identifying Semantic Conflicts in User Stories by Combining NLP and BERT Model*. RE (demo) (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A (demo track)). [link](https://doi.org/10.1109/re59067.2024.00057)

- **[arora2024advancing]** Chetan Arora, John Grundy, Mohamed Abdelrazek (2024). *Advancing Requirements Engineering Through Generative AI: Assessing the Role of LLMs*. GenAI4SE book (book). [link](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-031-55642-5_6)

  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Frames LLM-based requirements validation as text review; SpecDx proposes validation by execution.
- **[henkel2024scalable]** Elisabeth Henkel, Nico Hauff, Lena Funk et al. (2024). *Scalable Redundancy Detection for Real-Time Requirements*. RE (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A). [link](https://doi.org/10.1109/re59067.2024.00027) · [code](https://github.com/ultimate-pa/hanfor) ★12

- **[chen2023nl2tl]** Yongchao Chen, Rujul Gandhi, Yang Zhang et al. (2023). *NL2TL: Transforming Natural Languages to Temporal Logics using Large Language Models*. EMNLP (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.18653/v1/2023.emnlp-main.985) · [code](https://github.com/yongchao98/NL2TL) ★43

- **[panthaplackel2021deep]** Sheena Panthaplackel, Junyi Jessy Li, Milos Gligoric et al. (2021). *Deep Just-In-Time Inconsistency Detection Between Comments and Source Code*. AAAI (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.1609/aaai.v35i1.16119)

- **[wen2019large]** Fengcai Wen, Csaba Nagy, Gabriele Bavota et al. (2019). *A Large-Scale Empirical Study on Code-Comment Inconsistencies*. ICPC (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A). [link](https://doi.org/10.1109/icpc.2019.00019)
  - Relevance **low**; role: context; components: defect taxonomy, real-defect benchmark
  - Mines 1.3 billion AST-level changes from 1,500 systems and manually analyses 500 commits to build a taxonomy of code-comment inconsistencies that developers fix.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Shows how a defect taxonomy can be grounded in real fixes from project history, as SpecDx grounds real defects in TonomyX history and the τ²-Bench-Verified diff.
- **[ferrari2019nlp]** Alessio Ferrari, Andrea Esuli (2019). *An NLP approach for cross-domain ambiguity detection in requirements engineering*. ASE journal (peer-reviewed, CORE2020 B). [link](https://doi.org/10.1007/s10515-019-00261-7)
  - Relevance **low**; role: component prior art
  - Ranks terms by cross-domain ambiguity by comparing word embeddings from domain-specific language models, so that terms used differently by stakeholders of different domains are flagged; evaluated on seven elicitation scenarios against manual and crowd rankings (Kendall tau up to 0.88).
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Measures reading divergence across reader populations (domains) through embeddings; SpecDx measures divergence across executor families through behaviour.
- **[blasi2018translating]** Arianna Blasi, Alberto Goffi, Konstantin Kuznetsov et al. (2018). *Translating code comments to procedure specifications*. ISSTA (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A). [link](https://doi.org/10.1145/3213846.3213872)

  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Turns NL documentation into executable checks, i.e. builds C from T; SpecDx assumes both exist and diagnoses their disagreement.
- **[crapo2017requirements]** Andrew Crapo, Abha Moitra, Craig McMillan et al. (2017). *Requirements Capture and Analysis in ASSERT(TM)*. RE (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A). [link](https://doi.org/10.1109/re.2017.54)
  - Relevance **low**; role: component prior art; components: baseline B5
  - Describes requirements capture and formal analysis in GE's ASSERT tool.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Industrial formal requirements analysis tool; see the RE 2018 ASSERT paper for the defect classes it targets.
- **[fifarek2017spear]** Aaron W. Fifarek, Lucas G. Wagner, Jonathan A. Hoffman et al. (2017). *SpeAR v2.0: Formalized Past LTL Specification and Analysis of Requirements*. NFM (book). [link](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-319-57288-8_30) · [code](https://github.com/lgwagner/SpeAR) ★21
  - Relevance **low**; role: component prior art; components: baseline B5
  - SpeAR v2.0 captures requirements in a formal language designed to read like natural language with Past LTL semantics, and proves properties of requirement sets (e.g. logical consistency) by model checking.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Formal-by-construction requirement capture with consistency analysis; a B5-style symbolic baseline rather than an NL-interpretation method.
- **[arora2015automated]** Chetan Arora, Mehrdad Sabetzadeh, Lionel Briand et al. (2015). *Automated Checking of Conformance to Requirements Templates Using Natural Language Processing*. TSE (peer-reviewed, CORE2020 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.1109/tse.2015.2428709)
  - Relevance **low**; role: component prior art
  - Casts requirements templates (Rupp's and EARS) into NLP pattern matchers to check conformance automatically, robustly and accurately even without a glossary, evaluated on four case studies.

- **[marneffe2008finding]** Marie-Catherine de Marneffe, Anna N. Rafferty, Christopher D. Manning (2008). *Finding Contradictions in Text*. ACL (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://aclanthology.org/P08-1118/)
  - Relevance **low**; role: foundation
  - Defines contradiction detection between text pairs as an NLP task, characterises types of contradiction, and builds a feature-based detector on top of textual-entailment alignment.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* NLP foundation of text-level contradiction detection used by requirements tools (ALICE, NLI-based RE); SpecDx's contradictions are among executable clauses, not sentences.
- **[tan2007icomment]** Lin Tan, Ding Yuan, Gopal Krishna et al. (2007). */*icomment: bugs or bad comments?*/*. SOSP (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.1145/1294261.1294276)
  - Relevance **low**; role: foundation
  - iComment extracts programming rules from code comments (lock-related topics) with NLP and checks the code against them, reporting each mismatch as either a bug or a bad comment; applied to Linux and Mozilla.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Origin of the 'bugs or bad comments?' attribution question: a doc-code mismatch may indict either side. SpecDx asks the analogous question for executions versus specifications and answers it statistically over a population of executors.
- **[fabbrini2001linguistic]** F. Fabbrini, M. Fusani, S. Gnesi et al. (2001). *The linguistic approach to the natural language requirements quality: benefit of the use of an automatic tool*. SEW (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 C). [link](https://doi.org/10.1109/sew.2001.992662)
  - Relevance **low**; role: foundation; components: baseline B1
  - Presents a linguistic quality model for NL requirements and the automatic tool QuARS that flags expressiveness defects (vagueness, optionality, subjectivity, implicitness, weakness) in requirement sentences.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Lexical indicator-based ambiguity detection; SpecDx detects ambiguity only when it affects checked behaviour.

<a id="t4_spec_defects_oracles"></a>
## Defects in specifications and test oracles; specification mutation

- **[wang2026androids]** Hao Wang, Hanchen Li, Qiuyang Mang et al. (2026). *Do Androids Dream of Breaking the Game? Systematically Auditing AI Agent Benchmarks with BenchJack*. arXiv (preprint). [link](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.12673) · [code](https://github.com/benchjack/benchjack) ★50 · **baseline candidate**
  - Relevance **high**; role: direct competitor; component prior art; also in t9_agent_failure_attribution; components: defect taxonomy, refutation, baseline B1, mutation evaluation
  - Derives an eight-class taxonomy of benchmark flaws from past reward hacks, proposes an Agent-Eval Checklist, and builds BenchJack, an automated red-teaming system in which coding agents audit benchmarks and synthesise reward-hacking exploits; finds 219 flaws across 10 agent benchmarks and iteratively patches several.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Automatically audits agent-benchmark checkers with agents and produces exploit witnesses, overlapping SpecDx's weak-check detection with witnesses; it is adversarial (one agent seeks exploits) rather than a statistical diagnosis over a population of honest executors, and covers only checks that are too loose. | BenchJack's synthesised exploits are witnesses of weak checks (a wrong behaviour that passes every clause), the defect type SpecDx calls 'weak check'. SpecDx finds weak checks from the population plus symbolic search on the database; BenchJack finds them adversarially and does not address contradictions, hidden requirements or ambiguity.
- **[amaral2026mutdafny]** Isabel Amaral, Alexandra Mendes, José Campos (2026). *MutDafny: A Mutation-Based Approach to Assess Dafny Specifications*. ICSE (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.1145/3744916.3787816) · [code](https://github.com/MutDafny/mutdafny) ★2 · **baseline candidate**
  - Relevance **high**; role: component prior art; components: mutation evaluation, defect taxonomy
  - MutDafny reveals weak Dafny specifications by mutating code: if a mutant still verifies, the specification may be too weak; 40 operators (from existing tools plus ones mined from Dafny bug fixes), evaluated on 794 real programs, revealing five weak real-world specifications.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* State of the art for detecting weak checks by mutation in verified code; SpecDx detects weak checks in executable business specifications and additionally types and localises hidden requirements, ambiguities and contradictions without a reference implementation.
- **[misu2026spec]** Md Rakib Hossain Misu, Cristina V. Lopes, Iris Ma et al. (2026). *Spec-Harness: Assessing and Guiding LLM-Synthesized Formal Specifications Beyond Verifiability*. arXiv (preprint). [link](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.00280) · [code](https://github.com/Mondego/vACT) ★5 · **baseline candidate**
  - Relevance **high**; role: component prior art; components: mutation evaluation, defect taxonomy
  - Shows that verifier acceptance of LLM-synthesised JML specifications says little about quality (22.7% of verified specs had no postcondition) and introduces Spec-Harness, which measures behavioural adequacy along precondition/postcondition correctness and completeness using symbolic verification and input/output mutation, revealing over- and under-constraining specs.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Closest recent work on clause-level over/under-constraint diagnosis of specifications; it relies on generated test suites as ground truth and Java code, whereas SpecDx uses an LLM-executor population against NL requirements and also handles ambiguity and contradiction.
- **[yu2025utboost]** Boxi Yu, Yuxuan Zhu, Pinjia He et al. (2025). *UTBoost: Rigorous Evaluation of Coding Agents on SWE-Bench*. ACL (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.18653/v1/2025.acl-long.189) · [code](https://github.com/CUHK-Shenzhen-SE/UTBoost) ★36 · **baseline candidate**
  - Relevance **high**; role: evaluation/benchmark; component prior art; also in t2_irt_benchmark_audit, t9_agent_failure_attribution; components: defect taxonomy, real-defect benchmark, refutation
  - Introduces UTGenerator, an LLM-driven test generator, and UTBoost for test augmentation of SWE-Bench; finds 36 tasks with insufficient tests and 345 wrongly passed patches, changing 40.9% of SWE-Bench Lite and 24.4% of Verified leaderboard entries.
