  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Automatic weak-check detection in an agent benchmark via LLM-generated extra oracles; SpecDx covers all four defect types and separates spec defects from agent errors statistically rather than by trusting generated tests. | Automated weak-check repair for code benchmarks by generating tests that refute wrong patches; SpecDx detects weak clauses in business specifications by executor-population statistics and confirms them with witness states. | Detects weak checks (C looser than T) and repairs them with generated tests, using agents' wrongly accepted patches as witnesses; SpecDx detects the same class from interpreter populations on business specifications and certifies by witness states.
- **[endres2024large]** Madeline Endres, Sarah Fakhoury, Saikat Chakraborty et al. (2024). *Can Large Language Models Transform Natural Language Intent into Formal Method Postconditions?*. FSE (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.1145/3660791) · **baseline candidate**
  - Relevance **high**; role: component prior art; components: mutation evaluation, defect taxonomy, baseline B1
  - Defines nl2postcond, using LLMs to turn natural-language intent into formal postconditions (assertions), and introduces metrics based on correctness on tests and discriminative power against buggy mutants; finds LLM postconditions are often correct and discriminating and can catch real historical bugs.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Assesses executable checks derived from NL intent along the same two axes as SpecDx's Hidden requirement (rejects correct behaviour) and Weak check (accepts buggy behaviour), using tests and mutants as ground truth; SpecDx assumes no labelled tests and infers the intended behaviour from a fallible executor population.
- **[lahiri2024evaluating]** Shuvendu K. Lahiri (2024). *Evaluating LLM-driven User-Intent Formalization for Verification-Aware Languages*. FMCAD (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 B). [link](https://doi.org/10.34727/2024/isbn.978-3-85448-065-5_19) · **baseline candidate**
  - Relevance **high**; role: component prior art; components: mutation evaluation, defect taxonomy, refutation
  - Argues that evaluating NL-to-specification formalisation in Dafny/F* cannot rely on dynamic execution, and proposes symbolically testing specifications against input-output examples, measuring soundness and completeness of a specification (including against mutated outputs); the metric agrees closely with human labels on Dafny specifications from MBPP-DFY.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Formalises specification soundness/completeness checking, which maps to SpecDx's hidden-requirement and weak-check types; it presumes trusted tests, whereas SpecDx diagnoses clauses whose intended behaviour must itself be inferred from T via fallible interpreters.
- **[goldweber2024ironspec]** Eli Goldweber, Weixin Yu, Seyed Armin Vakil Ghahani et al. (2024). *IronSpec: Increasing the Reliability of Formal Specifications*. OSDI (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://www.usenix.org/conference/osdi24/presentation/goldweber) · [code](https://github.com/GLaDOS-Michigan/IronSpec) ★7 · **baseline candidate**
  - Relevance **high**; role: component prior art; components: mutation evaluation, defect taxonomy, real-defect benchmark
  - IronSpec is a framework of automatic and manual techniques to make formal specifications more reliable: automated sanity checking, a methodology for spec-testing proofs, and automated spec mutation testing; on 14 specs, including six from real verified systems, it found ten specification bugs.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Directly targets bugs in trusted specifications with mutation and sanity checks, like SpecDx, but for Dafny specs with human-written spec tests; SpecDx is automatic, works on NL+SQL business specs, and uses executor-population statistics.
- **[zheng2021flack]** Guolong Zheng, ThanhVu Nguyen, Simon Gutierrez Brida et al. (2021). *FLACK: Counterexample-Guided Fault Localization for Alloy Models*. ICSE (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.1109/icse43902.2021.00065) · **baseline candidate**
  - Relevance **high**; role: component prior art; also in t5_diagnosis_mus_dd; components: MUS/MCS, refutation, baseline B5
  - FLACK localises faults in Alloy models with a violated assertion by analysing differences between counterexamples and satisfying instances, returning a ranked list of suspicious expressions; it handles large real models within seconds and ranks buggy expressions in the top 1.9% on average.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Contrasts violating and satisfying instances to localise a specification fault, which resembles SpecDx's use of passing vs violating behaviours; SpecDx's instances come from fallible LLM executors, so it must first separate interpreter error from specification defect. | Fault localisation inside a formal specification using counterexample/witness pairs, related to SpecDx's witness-based refutation; FLACK needs a formal model and an assertion, while SpecDx localises defects between natural-language T and executable C.
- **[terragni2020evolutionary]** Valerio Terragni, Gunel Jahangirova, Paolo Tonella et al. (2020). *Evolutionary improvement of assertion oracles*. FSE (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.1145/3368089.3409758)
  - Relevance **high**; role: component prior art; components: defect taxonomy, mutation evaluation
  - GAssert: a co-evolutionary algorithm that repairs assertion oracles so they have no false positives on correct executions and fewer false negatives on mutant executions, outperforming oracles inferred by Daikon-like tools.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Repairs oracle deficiencies of both kinds using correct executions and mutants as evidence; SpecDx diagnoses (and localises) the analogous clause defects without a trusted program, using LLM-executor runs as noisy evidence of the intended behaviour.
- **[wang2020fault]** Kaiyuan Wang, Allison Sullivan, Darko Marinov et al. (2020). *Fault Localization for Declarative Models in Alloy*. ISSRE (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A). [link](https://doi.org/10.1109/issre5003.2020.00044) · **baseline candidate**
  - Relevance **high**; role: component prior art; components: MUS/MCS, baseline B5
  - AlloyFL, a fault-localisation technique for Alloy models that brings spectrum-based and mutation-based fault localisation to declarative models using multiple test formulas; on 90 real faulty models it is much more effective than Alloy's built-in unsat core.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Localises faults inside a specification from test outcomes, as SpecDx localises defects to clauses and cells; AlloyFL trusts its tests, whereas SpecDx's 'tests' are fallible executor runs whose reliability it estimates.
- **[wang2018mualloy]** Kaiyuan Wang, Allison Sullivan, Sarfraz Khurshid (2018). *MuAlloy: a mutation testing framework for alloy*. ICSE (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.1145/3183440.3183488) · [code](https://github.com/kaiyuanw/MuAlloy) ★8
  - Relevance **high**; role: component prior art; components: mutation evaluation
  - Tool paper for MuAlloy, an open-source Java command-line tool defining mutation testing for Alloy: mutation operators, mutant generation, and equivalent-mutant checking with the Alloy Analyzer, used to assess AUnit test suites.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Mutation operators and solver-based equivalent-mutant checking for a specification language, close to SpecDx's specification mutation operators with kill witnesses; MuAlloy evaluates tests of a spec, SpecDx evaluates a diagnoser of specs.
- **[jahangirova2018oasis]** Gunel Jahangirova, David Clark, Mark Harman et al. (2018). *OASIs: oracle assessment and improvement tool*. ISSTA (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A). [link](https://doi.org/10.1145/3213846.3229503) · **baseline candidate**
  - Relevance **high**; role: component prior art; components: defect taxonomy, mutation evaluation
  - Tool demo of OASIs, a search-based Java tool that assists oracle assessment and improvement by combining test generation to reveal false positives and mutation testing to reveal false negatives of assertions.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Directly operationalises the two oracle deficiencies SpecDx types as Hidden requirement and Weak check, but assumes the implementation is the reference; SpecDx has no reference implementation and uses a fallible executor population plus anchored truth inference.
- **[fonseca2017empirical]** Pedro Fonseca, Kaiyuan Zhang, Xi Wang et al. (2017). *An Empirical Study on the Correctness of Formally Verified Distributed Systems*. EuroSys (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A). [link](https://doi.org/10.1145/3064176.3064183)
  - Relevance **high**; role: evaluation/benchmark; components: defect taxonomy, real-defect benchmark
  - First empirical study of correctness of formally verified distributed systems (IronFleet, Verdi, Chapar): 16 bugs found, many serious, caused by violated assumptions in the trusted base, including specification and shim-layer issues, showing verification guarantees rest on unverified specifications.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Evidence that 'the specification is the bug' even in verified systems; motivates SpecDx's premise that executable specifications treated as ground truth are themselves defective.
- **[jahangirova2016test]** Gunel Jahangirova, David Clark, Mark Harman et al. (2016). *Test oracle assessment and improvement*. ISSTA (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A). [link](https://doi.org/10.1145/2931037.2931062)
  - Relevance **high**; role: component prior art; components: defect taxonomy, mutation evaluation, refutation
  - Proposes assessing assertion oracles by finding false positives (assertion fails on a correct execution, found by test generation) and false negatives (assertion passes on an incorrect execution, found by mutation), and iteratively improving them with human feedback; fault detection rose 48.6% on five subjects.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* The closest SE analogue of SpecDx's Hidden requirement (false positive) and Weak check (false negative) types, each backed by a concrete witness execution. It assumes the program is correct and a human adjudicates; SpecDx has no trusted implementation and replaces the human with a population of LLM executors.
- **[mcminn2015effectiveness]** Phil Mcminn, Chris J. Wright, Gregory M. Kapfhammer (2015). *The Effectiveness of Test Coverage Criteria for Relational Database Schema Integrity Constraints*. TOSEM (peer-reviewed, CORE2020 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.1145/2818639) · [code](https://github.com/schemaanalyst/schemaanalyst) ★25 · **baseline candidate**
  - Relevance **high**; role: component prior art; components: mutation evaluation, baseline B5
  - Proposes logic-coverage-derived criteria for testing integrity constraints of relational schemas (from accepted/rejected INSERTs to multi-column keys and arbitrary CHECK constraints), accounting for DBMS-specific semantics, with search-based data generation and mutation analysis to evaluate them.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Mutation analysis of SQL constraints is the nearest prior art for SpecDx's SQL clause mutation operators and for searching witness rows on PostgreSQL; it tests a schema against intent given by a tester, while SpecDx infers intent from NL requirements via executors.
- **[barr2015oracle]** Earl T. Barr, Mark Harman, Phil McMinn et al. (2015). *The Oracle Problem in Software Testing: A Survey*. TSE (peer-reviewed, CORE2020 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.1109/tse.2014.2372785)
  - Relevance **high**; role: survey; components: defect taxonomy
  - Surveys the test oracle problem, classifying oracles as specified, derived, implicit, or human, and reviewing techniques and open challenges for deciding whether observed behaviour is correct.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* SpecDx's clause set C is a specified oracle and its Weak check / Hidden requirement types are oracle deficiencies (too loose / too strict); SpecDx adds a derived oracle from a population of LLM executors to diagnose the specified oracle itself.
- **[huo2014improving]** Chen Huo, James Clause (2014). *Improving oracle quality by detecting brittle assertions and unused inputs in tests*. FSE (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.1145/2635868.2635917) · **baseline candidate**
  - Relevance **high**; role: component prior art; components: defect taxonomy
  - Dynamic-tainting technique (OraclePolish) that detects brittle assertions, which depend on uncontrolled inputs, and unused inputs, which no assertion checks; finds 164 brittle and 1,618 unused-input tests among 4,000+ real JUnit tests.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Names both oracle failure directions (checks too much vs too little) that SpecDx types as Hidden requirement and Weak check; it is purely structural and per test, while SpecDx decides from behaviour across a population whether the extra or missing check contradicts T.
- **[schuler2011assessing]** David Schuler, Andreas Zeller (2011). *Assessing Oracle Quality with Checked Coverage*. ICST (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A). [link](https://doi.org/10.1109/icst.2011.32)
  - Relevance **high**; role: component prior art; components: defect taxonomy
  - Introduces checked coverage: the dynamic slice of executed statements that actually influence an oracle check; shows on seven open-source projects that it indicates oracle quality better than statement coverage.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* A weak-check detector by construction: computations not reaching any check are unchecked. The SpecDx analogue is cells (table, row, column) that agents write but no clause reads; SpecDx instead detects weak checks behaviourally, via passing runs that the population deems wrong.
- **[staats2011programs]** Matt Staats, Michael W. Whalen, Mats P.E. Heimdahl (2011). *Programs, tests, and oracles: the foundations of testing revisited*. ICSE (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.1145/1985793.1985847)
  - Relevance **high**; role: foundation; components: defect taxonomy
  - Extends Gourlay's functional framework of testing with an explicit test oracle, uses it to clarify earlier concepts and define new oracle-related ones, and argues that oracle selection must be considered when judging the efficacy of a testing process.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Gives the formal vocabulary for oracles that are wrong in either direction, which matches SpecDx's satisfaction-based taxonomy (weak check = oracle accepts incorrect behaviour; hidden requirement = oracle rejects behaviour allowed by T); SpecDx adds automatic detection of such oracle defects.
- **[torlak2008finding]** Emina Torlak, Felix Sheng-Ho Chang, Daniel Jackson (2008). *Finding Minimal Unsatisfiable Cores of Declarative Specifications*. FM (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A). [link](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-540-68237-0_23) · **baseline candidate**
  - Relevance **high**; role: component prior art; also in t5_diagnosis_mus_dd; components: MUS/MCS, baseline B5, defect taxonomy
  - Presents recycling core extraction, a coverage analysis that pinpoints an irreducible (minimal) unsatisfiable core of a declarative specification to expose overconstrained axioms or underconstrained conjectures; based on resolution refutation proofs, proved correct for a general language and implemented and evaluated for Alloy.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* The direct logic-only analogue of SpecDx's contradiction detection (MUS of the clause set); SpecDx obtains candidate MUSes as hitting sets of correction sets sampled from executor behaviours, which also works when clauses are not translatable to a solver, and then refutes candidates with witness states. | Minimal unsat cores as a diagnostic for defects of declarative specifications (over-constraint ~ contradiction / hidden requirement) is the symbolic counterpart of SpecDx's contradiction step; it needs a formal specification, whereas SpecDx diagnoses NL text against executable checks via interpreters.
- **[ball2008vacuity]** Thomas Ball, Orna Kupferman (2008). *Vacuity in Testing*. TAP (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 C). [link](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-540-79124-9_2)
  - Relevance **high**; role: component prior art; components: defect taxonomy, mutation evaluation
  - Transfers vacuity from model checking to testing: defines vacuous satisfaction of a specification by a test suite (a mutated specification is still satisfied, so some specification elements play no role) and shows how vacuity analysis improves specifications and test suites.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* The closest formal definition of a weak check in a testing setting: a clause that can be mutated without any test noticing. SpecDx detects weak checks without mutating clauses, via runs that pass every clause yet contradict T according to the interpreter population; the mutation view is used in SpecDx's evaluation.
- **[shlyakhter2003debugging]** I. Shlyakhter, R. Seater, D. Jackson et al. (2003). *Debugging overconstrained declarative models using unsatisfiable cores*. ASE (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.1109/ase.2003.1240298)
  - Relevance **high**; role: component prior art; also in t5_diagnosis_mus_dd; components: MUS/MCS, baseline B5, defect taxonomy
  - Introduces core extraction for declarative models: the SAT solver's unsatisfiable core is mapped back to the model's syntax to show fragments irrelevant to the result, helping to discover and localise unintended overconstraint, with a soundness argument and case studies.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* First use of unsat cores to localise overconstraint (contradictions and hidden requirements) in a specification; SpecDx's MUS/MCS step addresses the same defect from behavioural correction sets, and adds statistical typing of non-contradiction defects. | The original "unsat cores for specification debugging" idea; it targets overconstrained (vacuous) formal models, a defect class related to SpecDx's contradictions and hidden requirements, but it needs a formal model and cannot see NL ambiguity or weak checks.
- **[kupferman2003vacuity]** Orna Kupferman, Moshe Y. Vardi (2003). *Vacuity detection in temporal model checking*. STTT (peer-reviewed, CORE2020 B). [link](https://doi.org/10.1007/s100090100062)
  - Relevance **high**; role: foundation; components: defect taxonomy, refutation, mutation evaluation
  - Gives a general method to detect vacuity and generate interesting witnesses for CTL* specifications, defining vacuity as some subformula not affecting the specification's truth in the system; also studies alternative definitions, linear witnesses for branching logic and complexity.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Vacuity is the model-checking form of a weak check: the specification passes for an uninteresting reason. SpecDx's weak-check type likewise asks whether a clause constrains anything, but decides it from a population of executions of a natural-language spec rather than by mutating temporal subformulas against a fixed model.
- **[black2000mutation]** P.E. Black, V. Okun, Y. Yesha (2000). *Mutation operators for specifications*. ASE (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.1109/ase.2000.873653)
  - Relevance **high**; role: foundation; components: mutation evaluation
  - Defines an extensive set of syntactic mutation operators for formal (model-checker) specifications, used to generate test suites via mutation analysis, studies theoretically and experimentally which fault classes each operator detects, and recommends reduced operator sets.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Canonical source for mutating the specification itself rather than the program; SpecDx's evaluation seeds defects with specification mutation operators, but its operators target typed business-spec defects (contradiction, hidden requirement, ambiguity, weak check) and each mutant carries a kill witness against a population of LLM executors rather than a model-checker counterexample.
- **[hossain2025togll]** Soneya Binta Hossain, Matthew B. Dwyer (2025). *TOGLL: Correct and Strong Test Oracle Generation with LLMS*. ICSE (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.1109/icse55347.2025.00098)
  - Relevance **medium**; role: context; components: mutation evaluation
  - Large study of LLMs for test oracle generation (seven fine-tuned code LLMs, six prompts) yielding TOGLL, which produces correct, diverse and strong oracles with far fewer false positives than TOGA, assessed on 25 Java projects including mutation-based strength.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Shows LLM-written checks suffer both false positives (too strict) and weak oracles (too loose), the deficiencies SpecDx diagnoses in benchmark clauses.
- **[alhanahnah2024empirical]** Mohannad Alhanahnah, Md Rashedul Hasan, Lisong Xu et al. (2024). *An Empirical Evaluation of Pre-trained Large Language Models for Repairing Declarative Formal Specifications*. EMSE (preprint). [link](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.11050)
  - Relevance **medium**; role: component prior art; components: baseline B1
  - Systematically evaluates LLMs for repairing Alloy declarative specifications in six settings (single/dual agent, feedback levels, auto-prompting) on 1,974 faulty specifications from ARepair and Alloy4Fun; a dual-agent auto-prompting setup beats state-of-the-art Alloy repair tools.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* LLMs reasoning over a faulty specification with solver feedback is close to SpecDx's static LLM review baseline (B1) plus refutation; it assumes the defect exists and a reference oracle, whereas SpecDx must first detect and type defects without one.
- **[tian2024large]** Zhao Tian, Honglin Shu, Dong Wang et al. (2024). *Large Language Models for Equivalent Mutant Detection: How Far Are We?*. ISSTA (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A). [link](https://doi.org/10.1145/3650212.3680395)
  - Relevance **medium**; role: context; components: mutation evaluation
  - Empirical study of LLMs for equivalent mutant detection on a large benchmark, comparing prompting and fine-tuning strategies with prior detection techniques.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Shows LLMs can judge semantic equivalence of mutants; SpecDx could use a similar LLM check as a pre-filter for seeded specification mutants, but relies on executable kill witnesses rather than LLM judgement for soundness.
- **[jovanovic2024right]** Ana Jovanovic, Allison Sullivan (2024). *Right or Wrong – Understanding How Users Write Software Models in Alloy*. SEFM (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 B). [link](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-031-77382-2_18)
  - Relevance **medium**; role: evaluation/benchmark; components: defect taxonomy, real-defect benchmark
  - Empirical study of over 97,000 Alloy models written by novices (Alloy4Fun) analysing how correct and incorrect models are written, separating underconstrained errors (model allows scenarios it should prevent) from overconstrained ones (prevents scenarios it should allow), and releasing benchmarks of faulty models.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Its under/over-constraint split maps onto SpecDx's weak check vs hidden requirement/contradiction types, giving external evidence that the taxonomy reflects real specification errors.
- **[zheng2022atr]** Guolong Zheng, ThanhVu Nguyen, Simón Gutiérrez Brida et al. (2022). *ATR: template-based repair for Alloy specifications*. ISSTA (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A). [link](https://doi.org/10.1145/3533767.3534369) · [code](https://github.com/guolong-zheng/atmprep) ★1
  - Relevance **medium**; role: component prior art; components: refutation
  - ATR repairs Alloy specifications with a violated assertion by contrasting counterexamples with satisfying instances to guide template-based repair candidates and prune them; repairs 66.3% of 1,974 faulty specifications, with code and benchmarks released.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Uses instance contrasts to repair a specification; its 1,974-specification benchmark (ARepair + Alloy4Fun) is the standard real-defect benchmark for declarative specifications that SpecDx could cite for the logic component.
- **[molina2022fuzzing]** Facundo Molina, Marcelo d'Amorim, Nazareno Aguirre (2022). *Fuzzing class specifications*. ICSE (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.1145/3510003.3510120)
  - Relevance **medium**; role: component prior art; components: mutation evaluation
  - SpecFuzzer infers class specifications by combining grammar-based fuzzing of candidate assertions, Daikon-based filtering of assertions falsified by a test suite, and a mutation-based mechanism that clusters assertions and ranks the stronger ones first; evaluated on 43 Java methods against GAssert and EvoSpex.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Uses mutants to measure whether inferred specifications are strong enough, the same strength criterion SpecDx applies to seeded weak-check mutants; SpecDx does not synthesise clauses.
- **[brida2022icebar]** Simón Gutiérrez Brida, Germán Regis, Guolong Zheng et al. (2022). *ICEBAR: Feedback-Driven Iterative Repair of Alloy Specifications*. ASE (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.1145/3551349.3556944)
  - Relevance **medium**; role: component prior art; components: refutation
  - ICEBAR repairs Alloy specifications using property-based oracles (predicates and assertions) for patch assessment instead of tests, iterating with counterexample feedback to reduce the overfitting typical of test-based repair.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Shows that weak (test-based) oracles cause overfit specification repairs, i.e. weak checks; SpecDx diagnoses such weak checks directly in the oracle.
- **[jahangirova2021empirical]** Gunel Jahangirova, David Clark, Mark Harman et al. (2021). *An Empirical Validation of Oracle Improvement*. TSE (peer-reviewed, CORE2020 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.1109/tse.2019.2934409)
  - Relevance **medium**; role: component prior art; components: defect taxonomy
  - Human studies (68 participants) of oracle assessment and improvement; developers judge assertion correctness poorly (29% accuracy) unaided, and the proposed false-positive/false-negative guided process helps them produce better oracles.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Evidence that humans are unreliable at judging oracle (clause) correctness, which supports SpecDx's choice of fully automatic, statistically aggregated diagnosis.
- **[brida2021bounded]** Simon Gutierrez Brida, German Regis, Guolong Zheng et al. (2021). *Bounded Exhaustive Search of Alloy Specification Repairs*. ICSE (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.1109/icse43902.2021.00105)
  - Relevance **medium**; role: component prior art; components: mutation evaluation
  - BeAFix repairs faulty Alloy models by bounded-exhaustive yet scalable exploration of fix candidates (mutations of suspicious expressions) with sound pruning, using the model's own assertions instead of unit tests; repairs thousands of real faulty models better than prior work.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Uses mutation operators over a specification to search for fixes; SpecDx uses specification mutation for evaluation (seeding defects) and does not repair, but the repair space hints at which mutation operators produce realistic defects.
- **[macedo2021experiences]** Nuno Macedo, Alcino Cunha, José Pereira et al. (2021). *Experiences on teaching alloy with an automated assessment platform*. SCP (peer-reviewed, CORE2020 A). [link](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.scico.2021.102690)
  - Relevance **medium**; role: evaluation/benchmark; components: real-defect benchmark
  - Presents Alloy4Fun, a web platform for editing, sharing and automatically assessing Alloy specification challenges via secret paragraphs and commands; it stores all model versions and derivation trees, a dataset that can be mined for typical specification mistakes.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Source of the Alloy4Fun dataset of real faulty specifications with ground-truth oracles used by ATR, ICEBAR and LLM repair studies; useful as an external real-defect set for SpecDx's logic components.
- **[terragni2021gassert]** Valerio Terragni, Gunel Jahangirova, Paolo Tonella et al. (2021). *GAssert: A Fully Automated Tool to Improve Assertion Oracles*. ICSE (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.1109/icse-companion52605.2021.00042) · [code](https://github.com/valerio-terragni/gassert) ★2 · **baseline candidate**
  - Relevance **medium**; role: component prior art; components: defect taxonomy
  - Tool paper for GAssert, which automatically improves assertion oracles (executable boolean expressions) by evolving them to reduce false positives and false negatives.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Same relation as the FSE 2020 paper; the tool's input/output shape (assertion plus labelled executions) is close to a clause plus labelled runs in SpecDx.
- **[perez2018comprehensive]** Oscar Luis Vera-Pérez, Benjamin Danglot, Martin Monperrus et al. (2018). *A comprehensive study of pseudo-tested methods*. EMSE (peer-reviewed, CORE2020 A). [link](https://doi.org/10.1007/s10664-018-9653-2)
  - Relevance **medium**; role: component prior art; components: mutation evaluation
  - Replicates the pseudo-tested-method study on 28K+ methods (methods covered by tests yet no test fails when the body is removed) and characterises 101 of them manually, finding they are less tested than other methods but often not worth fixing to developers.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Pseudo-tested code is the program-side symptom of weak oracles; SpecDx's weak-check operator (deleting or loosening a clause so a wrong behaviour passes) is the specification-side analogue.
- **[wang2018automated]** Kaiyuan Wang, Allison Sullivan, Sarfraz Khurshid (2018). *Automated model repair for Alloy*. ASE (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.1145/3238147.3238162) · [code](https://github.com/kaiyuanw/ARepair) ★6
  - Relevance **medium**; role: component prior art; components: mutation evaluation, real-defect benchmark
  - ARepair, the first repair technique for Alloy models: given a faulty model and a test suite with failing tests, it combines mutation testing and program synthesis to output a model passing the tests; fixes 28 of 38 real faulty models.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Repairs specification defects but assumes trusted tests as the oracle; SpecDx has no trusted oracle and stops at typed, localised diagnosis. Its benchmark of real faulty Alloy models is a candidate external benchmark for the logic half of SpecDx.
- **[just2014mutants]** René Just, Darioush Jalali, Laura Inozemtseva et al. (2014). *Are mutants a valid substitute for real faults in software testing?*. FSE (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.1145/2635868.2635929)
  - Relevance **medium**; role: evaluation/benchmark; components: mutation evaluation, real-defect benchmark
  - Empirically tests whether mutant detection correlates with real-fault detection, using real faults from open-source projects and generated test suites; finds a significant correlation that coverage alone does not explain, while some real-fault types are not coupled to common mutants.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Motivates SpecDx's two-pronged evaluation: seeded specification mutants plus real defects (TonomyX history, tau2-Bench-Verified diff), since mutant results alone may not transfer to real specification defects.
- **[madeyski2014overcoming]** Lech Madeyski, Wojciech Orzeszyna, Richard Torkar et al. (2014). *Overcoming the Equivalent Mutant Problem: A Systematic Literature Review and a Comparative Experiment of Second Order Mutation*. TSE (peer-reviewed, CORE2020 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.1109/tse.2013.44)
  - Relevance **medium**; role: survey; components: mutation evaluation, refutation
  - Systematic literature review of techniques that detect, suggest or avoid equivalent mutants (17 techniques in 22 articles), plus an experiment comparing second-order mutation strategies against first-order mutation for reducing equivalent mutants.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* A seeded specification mutant that changes no admissible behaviour is an equivalent mutant and would be scored as a missed defect; SpecDx requires a kill witness (a behaviour or state separating mutant from original) for each seeded mutant, which is its answer to this problem.
- **[schuler2013checked]** David Schuler, Andreas Zeller (2013). *Checked coverage: an indicator for oracle quality*. STVR (peer-reviewed, CORE2020 B). [link](https://doi.org/10.1002/stvr.1497)
  - Relevance **medium**; role: component prior art; components: defect taxonomy
  - Journal version of checked coverage: the dynamic slice of covered statements that influence an oracle, evaluated as an indicator of oracle quality that also reveals parts of the computation left unchecked.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Same relation as the ICST paper: structural detection of unchecked state, versus SpecDx's statistical detection of weak checks over agent runs.
- **[polikarpova2013good]** Nadia Polikarpova, Carlo A. Furia, Yu Pei et al. (2013). *What good are strong specifications?*. ICSE (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.1109/icse.2013.6606572)
  - Relevance **medium**; role: context; components: defect taxonomy
  - Studies the cost and benefit of writing strong (complete functional) contracts in Eiffel compared with typical weak contracts, showing stronger specifications reveal more faults with tools such as automated testing and verification.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Empirical evidence that weak specifications let faults pass, the program-level counterpart of SpecDx's Weak check type.
- **[staats2012automated]** Matt Staats, Gregory Gay, Mats P. E. Heimdahl (2012). *Automated oracle creation support, or: How I learned to stop worrying about fault propagation and love mutation testing*. ICSE (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.1109/icse.2012.6227132)
  - Relevance **medium**; role: component prior art; components: mutation evaluation
  - Supports oracle creation by using mutation analysis to rank which program variables an oracle should check, so that a small expected-value oracle detects most faults.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Chooses which state to check by mutant sensitivity; in SpecDx terms it proposes where clauses should exist, whereas SpecDx flags where existing clauses are too loose (weak check) using behavioural evidence.
- **[jia2011analysis]** Yue Jia, Mark Harman (2011). *An Analysis and Survey of the Development of Mutation Testing*. TSE (peer-reviewed, CORE2020 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.1109/tse.2010.62)
  - Relevance **medium**; role: survey; components: mutation evaluation
  - Comprehensive survey of three decades of mutation testing: approaches, tools, empirical results, and trend analyses showing the technique maturing toward practical applicability.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Standard reference for the mutation-analysis methodology that SpecDx's seeded-defect evaluation borrows; SpecDx mutates specifications (T and C) and measures diagnosis (typed, localised) rather than test-suite adequacy.
- **[kupferman2008theory]** Orna Kupferman, Wenchao Li, Sanjit A. Seshia (2008). *A Theory of Mutations with Applications to Vacuity, Coverage, and Fault Tolerance*. FMCAD (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 B). [link](https://doi.org/10.1109/fmcad.2008.ecp.29)
  - Relevance **medium**; role: foundation; components: mutation evaluation, defect taxonomy
  - Develops a theory of mutations of finite-state systems and specifications, unifying vacuity and coverage checks (both evaluate specification quality through mutations) and showing how orders among mutations improve specifications and reason about fault-tolerance coverage.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Theoretical grounding for using specification and system mutations to evaluate specification quality; relevant to designing SpecDx's mutation operators and their subsumption, though SpecDx's mutants are on SQL clauses and NL requirements.
- **[tuya2007mutating]** Javier Tuya, Ma José Suárez-Cabal, Claudio de la Riva (2007). *Mutating database queries*. IST (peer-reviewed, CORE2020 A). [link](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.infsof.2006.06.009)
  - Relevance **medium**; role: component prior art; components: mutation evaluation
  - Defines mutation operators for SQL SELECT queries covering many SQL features including NULL handling, evaluated on NIST SQL conformance queries, with selective mutation and mutant ordering to cut cost; usable to assess database test adequacy or to inject faults.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Operator catalogue directly applicable to mutating SpecDx's SQL invariants and policy functions when seeding weak-check and hidden-requirement defects.
- **[ernst2007daikon]** Michael D. Ernst, Jeff H. Perkins, Philip J. Guo et al. (2007). *The Daikon system for dynamic detection of likely invariants*. SCP (peer-reviewed, CORE2020 A). [link](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.scico.2007.01.015) · [code](https://github.com/codespecs/daikon) ★267 · **baseline candidate**
  - Relevance **medium**; role: component prior art; components: baseline B5
  - Describes Daikon, which runs a program (or reads record-structured data), observes computed values and reports properties true over the observed executions as likely invariants (constants, ranges, linear relations, orderings, containment, sortedness, etc.); outputs have been used for test generation, data-stream validity checking and more.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Inferring invariants from runs and comparing them with declared clauses is a natural non-statistical baseline for weak checks and hidden requirements; SpecDx instead models fallible readings with an anchored, set-valued truth model and interpreter accuracy, and also types contradictions and ambiguities.
- **[kupferman2006sanity]** Orna Kupferman (2006). *Sanity Checks in Formal Verification*. CONCUR (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A). [link](https://doi.org/10.1007/11817949_3)
  - Relevance **medium**; role: survey; components: defect taxonomy, mutation evaluation
  - Surveys sanity checks for passed verification: vacuity (the specification is satisfied trivially) and coverage (parts of the system unused by the specification), arguing both amount to re-verifying with mutated specifications or mutated systems.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Frames specification-quality checks as mutation-based re-verification, the same framing SpecDx's evaluation uses; SpecDx applies it to executable business specifications with an LLM-population oracle rather than a model checker.
- **[chockler2001coverage]** Hana Chockler, Orna Kupferman, Moshe Y. Vardi (2001). *Coverage Metrics for Temporal Logic Model Checking*. TACAS (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A). [link](https://doi.org/10.1007/3-540-45319-9_36)
  - Relevance **medium**; role: foundation; components: defect taxonomy, mutation evaluation
  - Proposes coverage metrics for model checking that ask how complete a specification is, by modifying (mutating) the system and checking which parts were relevant for verification to succeed; distinguishes state-based vs logic-based coverage and system vs environment, with algorithms to find uncovered parts.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Specification incompleteness measured by system mutations is the formal-methods analogue of SpecDx's weak-check detection (behaviour that differs yet still passes all clauses); SpecDx obtains such behaviour from fallible LLM executors instead of syntactic system mutations.
- **[ernst1999dynamically]** Michael D. Ernst, Jake Cockrell, William G. Griswold et al. (1999). *Dynamically discovering likely program invariants to support program evolution*. ICSE (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.1145/302405.302467)
  - Relevance **medium**; role: foundation; components: baseline B5
  - Original Daikon paper: dynamically discovers likely program invariants from execution traces to support program evolution.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Foundation for inferring specifications from behaviour; SpecDx infers an entailment set per cell from fallible LLM behaviours instead of trusted program traces.
- **[ammann1998using]** Paul E Ammann, Paul E Black, William Majurski (1998). *Using model checking to generate tests from specifications*. NIST IR (other). [link](https://doi.org/10.6028/nist.ir.6166)
  - Relevance **medium**; role: foundation; components: mutation evaluation, refutation
  - Applies syntactic mutation operators to a model-checker specification; the model checker produces counterexamples distinguishing each mutant from the original, which become complete test cases with inputs and expected results, in two classes (tests a correct implementation must fail or must pass).
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Origin of the kill-witness idea for specification mutants: a counterexample separating mutant and original. SpecDx's kill witnesses play the same role for seeded business-spec defects, but are found by symbolic search on PostgreSQL or directed LLM probes.
- **[beer1997efficient]** Ilan Beer, Shoham Ben-David, Cindy Eisner et al. (1997). *Efficient detection of vacuity in ACTL formulas*. CAV (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.1007/3-540-63166-6_28)
  - Relevance **medium**; role: foundation; components: defect taxonomy, refutation
  - Formalises vacuity and interesting witnesses (e.g. antecedent failure making an implication trivially true) and gives an efficient detection and witness-generation procedure for a useful subset of ACTL.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Introduces the idea that a passing check needs a witness of non-trivial satisfaction; SpecDx's sound refutation similarly attaches witness states to candidate defects.
- **[barros2024alloy]** Ana Barros, Henrique Neto, Alcino Cunha et al. (2024). *Alloy Repair Hint Generation Based on Historical Data*. FM (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A). [link](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-031-71177-0_8)
  - Relevance **low**; role: context; components: real-defect benchmark
  - Data-driven next-step hint generation for novices writing Alloy specifications, built from historical Alloy4Fun submissions.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Uses historical faulty-to-correct specification edits; analogous to SpecDx mining TonomyX history for real defects, but for education rather than diagnosis.
- **[molina2021evospex]** Facundo Molina, Pablo Ponzio, Nazareno Aguirre et al. (2021). *EvoSpex: An Evolutionary Algorithm for Learning Postconditions*. ICSE (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.1109/icse43902.2021.00112)
  - Relevance **low**; role: context; components: mutation evaluation
  - EvoSpex learns method postconditions with an evolutionary algorithm that uses valid executions and mutation-generated invalid executions as positive and negative evidence.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Learns specifications from executions labelled valid/invalid; SpecDx instead takes the specification as given and asks which clauses disagree with the behaviour of competent interpreters.
- **[sullivan2018aunit]** Allison Sullivan, Kaiyuan Wang, Sarfraz Khurshid (2018). *AUnit: A Test Automation Tool for Alloy*. ICST (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A). [link](https://doi.org/10.1109/icst.2018.00047)
  - Relevance **low**; role: context
  - AUnit extension of the Alloy Analyzer: grammar for test cases (valuations) of Alloy models, test execution, test-suite coverage and automatic test generation.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Unit tests for specifications, i.e. checking a specification against expected instances; SpecDx uses agent behaviours as unlabelled 'tests' of the specification instead of hand-written expected instances.
- **[niedermayr2016will]** Rainer Niedermayr, Elmar Juergens, Stefan Wagner (2016). *Will my tests tell me if I break this code?*. CSED (peer-reviewed). [link](https://doi.org/10.1145/2896941.2896944)
  - Relevance **low**; role: context; components: mutation evaluation
  - Introduces pseudo-tested methods, detected by removing a method's body and checking whether any covering test fails, as a sign of inadequate test oracles.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Extreme mutation to expose missing checks; SpecDx's weak-check detection does not need a mutated implementation, only agent runs that pass all clauses yet deviate from T.
- **[zhang2015assertions]** Yucheng Zhang, Ali Mesbah (2015). *Assertions are strongly correlated with test suite effectiveness*. FSE (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.1145/2786805.2786858)
  - Relevance **low**; role: context
  - Shows that the number and kind of assertions in tests correlate strongly with mutation-based test-suite effectiveness, more than test-suite size.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Context: oracle strength, not coverage, determines fault detection; SpecDx's clauses are the oracle of the benchmark, so their strength determines what wrong agent behaviour is caught.
- **[papadakis2015trivial]** Mike Papadakis, Yue Jia, Mark Harman et al. (2015). *Trivial Compiler Equivalence: A Large Scale Empirical Study of a Simple, Fast and Effective Equivalent Mutant Detection Technique*. ICSE (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.1109/icse.2015.103)
  - Relevance **low**; role: context; components: mutation evaluation
  - Proposes Trivial Compiler Equivalence (TCE), which flags equivalent and duplicated mutants by comparing compiled binaries, and evaluates it on large open-source and benchmark programs.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Cheap equivalence filter for program mutants; the analogue for SQL clause mutants would be checking semantic equivalence of mutated clauses (e.g. by query-equivalence or SMT) before requiring a kill witness, which SpecDx does by search rather than by compilation.
- **[fraser2010mutation]** Gordon Fraser, Andreas Zeller (2010). *Mutation-driven generation of unit tests and oracles*. ISSTA (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A). [link](https://doi.org/10.1145/1831708.1831728)
  - Relevance **low**; role: context; components: mutation evaluation
  - Generates unit tests and minimal assertion oracles that kill mutants of object-oriented classes, so test suites target defects rather than coverage.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Kill-driven oracle synthesis; SpecDx uses mutant killing only for evaluation (each seeded spec defect needs a kill witness), not for generating clauses.
- **[fraser2008testing]** Gordon Fraser, Franz Wotawa, Paul E. Ammann (2008). *Testing with model checkers: a survey*. STVR (peer-reviewed, CORE2020 B). [link](https://doi.org/10.1002/stvr.402)
  - Relevance **low**; role: survey; components: mutation evaluation, refutation
  - Surveys a decade of work that uses model-checker counterexamples as test cases, including coverage- and mutation-based test generation from specifications, and lists open problems.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Background for witness generation from specifications; SpecDx's refutation step similarly searches for witness states, but on SQL clauses over a live database.
- **[ammann1999specification]** Paul E Ammann, Paul E Black (1999). *A specification-based coverage metric to evaluate test sets*. NIST IR (other). [link](https://doi.org/10.6028/nist.ir.6403)
  - Relevance **low**; role: foundation; components: mutation evaluation
  - Defines a specification-based coverage metric for test sets, computed with a model checker over mutants of the specification, to assure that a test set is sensitive to the structure of the specification.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Measures test-set adequacy against a trusted specification; SpecDx inverts the trust direction and uses behaviours to assess whether the specification is adequate.

<a id="t5_diagnosis_mus_dd"></a>
## Model-based diagnosis, MUS/MCS, fault localisation and delta debugging

- **[valle2026delta]** Pablo Valle, Shaukat Ali, Aitor Arrieta (2026). *Delta Debugging for Cyber-Physical Systems with Flaky Test Executions*. arXiv (preprint). [link](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.25695)
  - Relevance **high**; role: component prior art; components: ddmin
  - Presents three delta debugging algorithms for stochastic cyber-physical systems that combine statistical failure analysis, repeated executions and environment-aware reduction to isolate minimal failure-inducing inputs despite flaky executions.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* One of few works on ddmin with a non-deterministic oracle, which is SpecDx's situation (an LLM population). SpecDx's oracle is a statistical test over interpreters rather than repeated simulator runs.
- **[zhu2026delta]** Hao-Nan Zhu, Muhammad Numair Mansur, Martin Schäf et al. (2026). *Delta Debugging for LLM-integrated Systems*. ICSE-SEIP (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A* (SEIP track)). [link](https://doi.org/10.1145/3786583.3786854)
  - Relevance **high**; role: component prior art; components: ddmin
  - Investigates delta debugging of natural-language instruction prompts in LLM-integrated systems: semantic markers embedded in the input and traced through chain-of-thought identify causal fragments, letting delta debugging isolate minimal prompt subsets responsible for incorrect outputs in development and production benchmarks.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Closest prior art for SpecDx step 5: ddmin over the natural-language text that instructs an LLM, with LLM behaviour as the oracle. It debugs one system's prompt for a known wrong output; SpecDx reduces a specification T to localise ambiguity, defined by disagreement across a population of interpreters.
- **[wang2021probabilistic]** Guancheng Wang, Ruobing Shen, Junjie Chen et al. (2021). *Probabilistic Delta debugging*. ESEC/FSE (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.1145/3468264.3468625) · [code](https://github.com/Amocy-Wang/ProbDD) ★19 · **baseline candidate**
  - Relevance **high**; role: component prior art; components: ddmin, active synthesis
  - ProbDD replaces ddmin's fixed partitioning with a probabilistic model of how likely each element is to be kept, selecting deletions that maximise expected gain and updating the model from test outcomes; it reduces programs faster and to smaller results than ddmin and CHISEL.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* A query-efficient minimiser that suits SpecDx's expensive population oracle; neither ProbDD nor its follow-ups model an oracle that is itself noisy, which SpecDx's LLM committee is.
- **[cazes2020model]** Dean Cazes, Meir Kalech (2020). *Model-Based Diagnosis with Uncertain Observations*. AAAI (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.1609/aaai.v34i03.5664)
  - Relevance **high**; role: component prior art; components: hitting sets, MUS/MCS
  - Formalises model-based diagnosis when observations come from noisy sensors that return incorrect values with known probability, for both consistency-based and abductive models; gives two complete algorithms that find and rank all diagnoses and a third that returns the most probable diagnosis directly.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* The closest MBD prior art to SpecDx's noisy setting: SpecDx's "sensors" are LLM interpreters whose readings are wrong with model-dependent probability. Cazes and Kalech assume known sensor error rates; SpecDx estimates interpreter accuracy and item difficulty jointly (IRT-style) and uses the specification as an anchor. | See the AAAI 2020 version: the nearest MBD formulation of SpecDx's noisy-reader setting, with known rather than estimated error rates.
- **[ignatiev2019model]** Alexey Ignatiev, Antonio Morgado, Georg Weissenbacher et al. (2019). *Model-Based Diagnosis with Multiple Observations*. IJCAI (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.24963/ijcai.2019/155)
  - Relevance **high**; role: component prior art; components: hitting sets, MUS/MCS
  - Shows that existing approaches for jointly diagnosing multiple observations (e.g. many failing tests in continuous integration) fail to compute the set of minimal diagnoses, corrects them, and proposes a new algorithm with large performance gains.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Closest formal analogue of SpecDx's setting, where many runs (observations) must be jointly diagnosed over a common clause set. Ignatiev et al. treat every observation as correct; SpecDx must tolerate observations corrupted by interpreter errors and separate them statistically before hitting.
- **[ignatiev2018pysat]** Alexey Ignatiev, Antonio Morgado, Joao Marques-Silva (2018). *PySAT: A Python Toolkit for Prototyping with SAT Oracles*. SAT (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A). [link](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-319-94144-8_26) · [code](https://github.com/pysathq/pysat) ★465 · **baseline candidate**
  - Relevance **high**; role: component prior art; components: MUS/MCS, hitting sets, baseline B5
  - Presents PySAT, a Python toolkit giving a simple API to several state-of-the-art SAT oracles plus cardinality encodings, aimed at fast prototyping of SAT-based problem-solving methods (MUS/MCS extraction, MaxSAT, hitting sets).
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Likely implementation substrate for SpecDx's hitting-set step and for symbolic baselines; it supplies algorithms, not the statistical treatment of noisy correction sets.
- **[liffiton2016fast]** Mark H. Liffiton, Alessandro Previti, Ammar Malik et al. (2016). *Fast, flexible MUS enumeration*. Constraints (peer-reviewed, CORE2020 A). [link](https://doi.org/10.1007/s10601-015-9183-0) · [code](https://github.com/liffiton/MARCO) ★28 · **baseline candidate**
  - Relevance **high**; role: component prior art; components: MUS/MCS, hitting sets, baseline B5
  - Presents MARCO, an anytime algorithm that enumerates MUSes and MCSes of any constraint system by exploring the power set with a map formula, producing MUSes steadily with per-MUS cost close to a single MUS extraction.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Ready-made symbolic contradiction finder that SpecDx can be compared with on the same clause set: MARCO needs a formal encoding of C and finds only logical contradictions, whereas SpecDx works from executed behaviours and also types hidden requirements, ambiguity and weak checks.
- **[silva2013minimal]** Joao Marques-Silva, Mikoláš Janota, Anton Belov (2013). *Minimal Sets over Monotone Predicates in Boolean Formulae*. CAV (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-642-39799-8_39)
  - Relevance **high**; role: component prior art; components: MUS/MCS, ddmin
  - Shows that computing MUSes, MCSes, prime implicates and minimal models are all instances of finding a minimal set subject to a monotone predicate (MSMP), solvable by the same algorithms, and gives an asymptotically optimal MSMP algorithm that beats state-of-the-art MUS extractors.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* MSMP unifies SpecDx's two minimisation steps: MUS candidates over clauses (step 3) and ddmin over sentences of T (step 5) are both minimal sets for a monotone predicate; SpecDx's predicates are statistical and only approximately monotone.
- **[silva2013computing]** Joao Marques-Silva, Federico Heras, Mikolas Janota et al. (2013). *On Computing Minimal Correction Subsets*. IJCAI (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://www.ijcai.org/Abstract/13/098)
  - Relevance **high**; role: component prior art; components: MUS/MCS, baseline B5
  - Shows that existing MCS algorithms (MaxSAT-based or iterative SAT) can be inefficient, develops techniques to speed them up and proposes a new algorithm (clause D, CLD) for computing minimal correction subsets, evaluated as the most efficient and robust.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Defines the MCS object that SpecDx samples from behaviour; in a B5 baseline the MCSes of C would be computed with these algorithms instead of being observed.
- **[stern2012exploring]** Roni Stern, Meir Kalech, Alexander Feldman et al. (2012). *Exploring the Duality in Conflict-Directed Model-Based Diagnosis*. AAAI (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.1609/aaai.v26i1.8231)
  - Relevance **high**; role: component prior art; components: hitting sets, MUS/MCS
  - Exploits the duality that a diagnosis is a hitting set of conflicts and a conflict is a hitting set of diagnoses, interleaving the search for both; the resulting algorithm finds minimal-cardinality diagnoses faster than Conflict-Directed A*.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* SpecDx uses exactly this duality in reverse: sampled correction sets (diagnosis-like objects) are hit to obtain conflict (MUS) candidates. SpecDx adds soft (noise-tolerant) hitting and sound refutation, which the deterministic setting does not need.
- **[jose2011cause]** Manu Jose, Rupak Majumdar (2011). *Cause clue clauses: error localization using maximum satisfiability*. PLDI (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.1145/1993498.1993550)
  - Relevance **high**; role: component prior art; components: MUS/MCS, hitting sets
  - BugAssist encodes a bounded unrolling of a C program plus a failing test and the assertion as an unsatisfiable formula, uses MAX-SAT to find a maximal satisfiable set of clauses, and reports the complement (a minimal correction set) as the likely cause of the error.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Error localisation as MCS computation over a formula that a failing execution makes unsatisfiable is the logical core SpecDx reuses at the specification level; BugAssist trusts its test oracle, while SpecDx's "tests" are behaviours of fallible interpreters.
- **[abreu2010diagnosing]** Rui Abreu, Arjan J.C. van Gemund (2010). *Diagnosing multiple intermittent failures using maximum likelihood estimation*. AIJ (peer-reviewed, CORE2020 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.artint.2010.09.003)
  - Relevance **high**; role: component prior art; components: hitting sets, identifiability
  - Intermittent-failure models give each faulty component a probability of behaving correctly; Barinel estimates these unknown parameters by maximum likelihood within the Bayesian computation of posterior candidate probabilities, outperforming persistent and earlier intermittent models on synthetic systems, Siemens and real programs.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Jointly estimating per-component goodness and candidate posteriors from noisy pass/fail spectra parallels SpecDx's joint estimation of interpreter accuracy and cell entailment sets. Barinel has no notion of interpreter ability or item difficulty and no anchor, so it cannot separate "the specification is wrong" from "the executor is weak".
- **[abreu2009spectrum]** Rui Abreu, Peter Zoeteweij, Arjan J.C. van Gemund (2009). *Spectrum-Based Multiple Fault Localization*. ASE (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.1109/ase.2009.25) · [code](https://github.com/ncfxy/zoltar) ★3 · **baseline candidate**
  - Relevance **high**; role: component prior art; components: hitting sets, MUS/MCS, baseline B3
  - Proposes Barinel, which models a program by abstractions of execution traces (as in SFL) and uses Bayesian reasoning to compute multiple-fault candidates and their probabilities (as in MBD), with a probabilistic component model in which faulty components may fail intermittently.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Barinel is the most direct "statistics + hitting sets" precedent: candidates are minimal hitting sets of failing spectra, ranked by a noise-aware likelihood. SpecDx differs by diagnosing a specification rather than code, by modelling interpreter accuracy and item difficulty, by using the specification as an anchor, and by typing defects and refuting candidates with witnesses.
- **[liffiton2008algorithms]** Mark H. Liffiton, Karem A. Sakallah (2008). *Algorithms for Computing Minimal Unsatisfiable Subsets of Constraints*. JAR (peer-reviewed, CORE2020 A). [link](https://doi.org/10.1007/s10817-007-9084-z)
  - Relevance **high**; role: foundation; components: MUS/MCS, hitting sets, baseline B5
  - Gives a sound and complete algorithm (CAMUS) for all MUSes of an unsatisfiable constraint system, based on the relationship between satisfiable and unsatisfiable subsets: all MCSes are computed first, and the MUSes are their minimal hitting sets.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* SpecDx's step 3 is CAMUS's second phase applied to sampled, noisy correction sets instead of exactly computed MCSes. Running CAMUS on the SQL/clause encoding of C would give the exact symbolic answer (a B5-style baseline) when the clauses can be encoded.
- **[flesch2007conflict]** Ildikó Flesch, Peter Lucas, Theo van der Weide (2007). *Conflict-based Diagnosis: Adding Uncertainty to Model-based Diagnosis*. IJCAI (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://www.ijcai.org/Abstract/07/059)
  - Relevance **high**; role: component prior art; components: hitting sets, MUS/MCS
  - Represents logical diagnostic systems in a probabilistic (Bayesian-network) theory and shows that consistent and inconsistent states can still be determined as in consistency-based diagnosis, while a Bayesian conflict measure ranks diagnoses, adding uncertainty reasoning to consistency-based diagnosis.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* A "soft conflict" view of consistency-based diagnosis, close to SpecDx's soft hitting sets over noisy correction sets; SpecDx's uncertainty comes from fallible interpreters rather than component behaviour, and it adds witness refutation.
- **[bailey2005discovery]** James Bailey, Peter J. Stuckey (2005). *Discovery of Minimal Unsatisfiable Subsets of Constraints Using Hitting Set Dualization*. PADL (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 C). [link](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-540-30557-6_14)
  - Relevance **high**; role: component prior art; components: MUS/MCS, hitting sets
  - Discovers all minimal unsatisfiable subsets of a constraint set by hitting set dualization, alternating between maximal satisfiable subsets (whose complements are correction sets) and their minimal hitting sets (abstract not read; summary from title and citing literature).
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* The dualization that SpecDx step 3 relies on (MUS = minimal hitting set of the MCS family) was introduced here for exact, solver-computed sets; SpecDx observes the MCS family noisily and so needs soft hitting.
- **[felfernig2004consistency]** Alexander Felfernig, Gerhard Friedrich, Dietmar Jannach et al. (2004). *Consistency-based diagnosis of configuration knowledge bases*. AIJ (peer-reviewed, CORE2020 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.1016/s0004-3702(03)00117-6)
  - Relevance **high**; role: component prior art; components: MUS/MCS, hitting sets, defect taxonomy
  - Shows that debugging configuration knowledge bases can be done by consistency-based diagnosis: valid (positive) and invalid (negative) examples serve as test cases, and diagnoses are minimal sets of knowledge-base constraints whose removal makes the knowledge base consistent with the examples; a conflict-based algorithm is given.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Very close in spirit: a declarative specification (the knowledge base) is diagnosed with example behaviours, much as SpecDx diagnoses C with interpreter behaviours. Felfernig et al. need correctly labelled examples; SpecDx's behaviours come from fallible LLM interpreters and must be separated into specification defects and agent errors.
- **[junker2004quickxplain]** Ulrich Junker (2004). *QUICKXPLAIN: Preferred Explanations and Relaxations for Over-Constrained Problems*. AAAI (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://aaai.org/?p=43725)
  - Relevance **high**; role: component prior art; components: MUS/MCS, ddmin
  - Defines preferred explanations (conflicts) and relaxations of over-constrained problems from user preferences between constraints and computes them with a generic divide-and-conquer method that works with any CP, SAT or description-logic solver.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* QuickXplain is the divide-and-conquer MUS extractor that is structurally the same as ddmin; SpecDx uses ddmin over sentences with an LLM-population oracle where QuickXplain uses a consistency checker.
- **[zeller2002simplifying]** A. Zeller, R. Hildebrandt (2002). *Simplifying and isolating failure-inducing input*. TSE (peer-reviewed, CORE2020 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.1109/32.988498)
  - Relevance **high**; role: foundation; components: ddmin
  - Presents the ddmin algorithm, which simplifies a failing test case to a 1-minimal one that still fails, and dd, which isolates the minimal difference between a passing and a failing input; e.g. a Mozilla crash after 95 user actions is reduced to 3.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* The algorithm SpecDx applies to the sentences of T, with "the interpreter population still splits into two readings" as the test; ddmin assumes a deterministic, monotone oracle, which a population oracle only approximates.
- **[reiter1987theory]** Raymond Reiter (1987). *A theory of diagnosis from first principles*. AIJ (peer-reviewed, CORE2020 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.1016/0004-3702(87)90062-2)
  - Relevance **high**; role: foundation; components: hitting sets, MUS/MCS
  - Defines consistency-based diagnosis from first principles: given a logical system description and an observation that conflicts with intended behaviour, a diagnosis is a minimal set of components whose abnormality restores consistency; diagnoses are the minimal hitting sets of the conflict sets, computed with the HS-tree.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* SpecDx step 3 rests on this duality: each LLM behaviour's violated-clause set plays the role of a conflict/correction set and minimal hitting sets give candidate MUSes. Reiter assumes noise-free observations and a known system description; SpecDx samples noisy correction sets from fallible interpreters and must refute candidates with witnesses.
- **[kleer1987diagnosing]** Johan de Kleer, Brian C. Williams (1987). *Diagnosing multiple faults*. AIJ (peer-reviewed, CORE2020 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.1016/0004-3702(87)90063-4)
  - Relevance **high**; role: foundation; components: hitting sets, active synthesis
  - Introduces the General Diagnostic Engine (GDE), which diagnoses multiple faults by representing candidates through minimal sets of violated assumptions (minimal conflicts) and combines model-based prediction with probabilistic, entropy-based measurement selection to localise faults sequentially.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Provides both the conflict/candidate machinery and the information-theoretic choice of the next measurement, which parallels SpecDx's hitting-set candidates and its Thompson-sampling scenario synthesis; GDE measures a physical device, whereas SpecDx "measures" by running LLM interpreters on synthesised scenarios.
- **[zhou2026ddor]** Qinyan Zhou, Peixin Zhang, Jun Sun et al. (2026). *DDOR: Delta Debugging for Explainable Overrefusal Testing and Repair*. arXiv (preprint). [link](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.03601)
  - Relevance **medium**; role: component prior art; components: ddmin
  - DDOR applies delta debugging to prompts in a black-box setting to localise minimal refusal-triggering fragments that explain why an LLM over-refuses a benign query, then uses them to generate overrefusal test suites and repair prompts.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* ddmin over natural-language text with an LLM as oracle, as in SpecDx step 5. DDOR localises fragments that trigger one model's refusal; SpecDx localises sentences of a specification that make a population of models split into two readings.
- **[tao2025accelerating]** Yonggang Tao, Jingling Xue (2025). *Accelerating Delta Debugging through Probabilistic Monotonicity Assessment*. EASE (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A). [link](https://doi.org/10.1145/3756681.3756940)
  - Relevance **medium**; role: component prior art; components: ddmin
  - Probabilistic Monotonicity Assessment (PMA) models whether the search space is monotone from prior tests and uses a confidence function to skip subsets probabilistically, cutting redundant tests of ddmin-style tools without hurting reduction quality.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Monotonicity is doubtful for SpecDx's ambiguity predicate (removing a sentence can create or remove a reading); PMA is one of the few works that relaxes it.
- **[chen2025llm]** Boqi Chen, Aren A. Babikian, Shuzhao Feng et al. (2025). *LLM-based Satisfiability Checking of String Requirements by Consistent Data and Checker Generation*. RE (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A). [link](https://doi.org/10.1109/re63999.2025.00030)
  - Relevance **medium**; role: component prior art; components: refutation, baseline B1
  - Hybrid approach that checks satisfiability of natural-language requirements over strings by having LLMs derive a verdict, generate consistent data (witnesses) and generate declarative (SMT) and imperative (Python) checkers that validate the outcome; this doubles generation success and F1 in some settings.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Uses LLM-generated witness data validated by executable checkers to decide satisfiability of requirements, the same idea as SpecDx's refutation by witness states; it works on string requirements with one LLM, without population statistics or defect typing.
- **[zhang2025better]** Mengxiao Zhang, Zhenyang Xu, Yongqiang Tian et al. (2025). *Toward a Better Understanding of Probabilistic Delta Debugging*. ICSE (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.1109/icse55347.2025.00117)
  - Relevance **medium**; role: component prior art; components: ddmin
  - First in-depth theoretical analysis of ProbDD, showing that its probabilities essentially act as monotonically increasing counters; proposes CDD, a much simpler counter-based variant that matches ProbDD on 76 minimisation and debloating benchmarks.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* CDD is the simplest query-efficient replacement for ddmin in SpecDx's ambiguity localisation.
- **[zhou2025wdd]** Xintong Zhou, Zhenyang Xu, Mengxiao Zhang et al. (2025). *WDD: Weighted Delta Debugging*. ICSE (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.1109/icse55347.2025.00071)
  - Relevance **medium**; role: component prior art; components: ddmin
  - WDD makes ddmin and ProbDD weight-aware: elements of a test input differ in size, so partitioning by weight rather than count improves efficiency and effectiveness of delta debugging.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Relevant if SpecDx's units of T (sentences, paragraphs, tool descriptions) differ greatly in length.
- **[christopher2024critical]** Cody James Christopher, Alban Grastien (2024). *Critical observations in model-based diagnosis*. AIJ (peer-reviewed, CORE2020 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.artint.2024.104116)
  - Relevance **medium**; role: component prior art; components: ddmin, hitting sets
  - Defines a sub-observation as an abstraction of the observations and calls it sufficient if it yields the same minimal diagnosis; a critical observation is a maximally abstracted sufficient sub-observation, and the paper shows how to compute one for state-based and event-based observations.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Minimising the observations needed to support a diagnosis mirrors SpecDx's ddmin over the sentences of T needed to sustain an ambiguity.
- **[zhang2024lpr]** Mengxiao Zhang, Yongqiang Tian, Zhenyang Xu et al. (2024). *LPR: Large Language Models-Aided Program Reduction*. ISSTA (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A). [link](https://doi.org/10.1145/3650212.3652126)
  - Relevance **medium**; role: component prior art; components: ddmin
  - LPR alternates a language-generic reducer (Perses/Vulcan) with LLM-driven, language-specific program transformations, producing smaller programs than Vulcan on C, Rust and JavaScript benchmarks.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* LLMs used inside a reduction loop as transformers; SpecDx uses LLMs as the oracle of the reduction (does the ambiguity persist?), not as transformers.
- **[rodler2023dynamichs]** Patrick Rodler (2023). *DynamicHS: Streamlining Reiter’s Hitting-Set Tree for Sequential Diagnosis*. Inf. Sci. (peer-reviewed, CORE2020 A). [link](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.ins.2022.08.029)
  - Relevance **medium**; role: component prior art; components: hitting sets, active synthesis
  - DynamicHS is a stateful variant of Reiter's HS-tree for sequential diagnosis that keeps the search tree between measurement iterations and minimises costly reasoner calls; it beats HS-tree in 96% of sessions while keeping its guarantees.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* SpecDx also alternates hitting-set computation with new observations (new scenarios); incremental hitting-set maintenance like DynamicHS avoids recomputation as correction sets arrive.
- **[mitchell2022enhancing]** Eric Mitchell, Joseph Noh, Siyan Li et al. (2022). *Enhancing Self-Consistency and Performance of Pre-Trained Language Models through Natural Language Inference*. EMNLP (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.18653/v1/2022.emnlp-main.115)
  - Relevance **medium**; role: context; components: MUS/MCS, family effects/correlated errors
  - ConCoRD boosts the consistency and accuracy of pre-trained QA/VQA models by using an NLI model to detect relations between candidate answers to related questions and choosing a globally consistent set of answers with a MaxSAT solver over a factor graph, without fine-tuning.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Combines statistics over many model outputs with weighted MaxSAT, as SpecDx combines population statistics with logic; ConCoRD repairs a model's beliefs assuming the questions are well posed, whereas SpecDx diagnoses the specification.
- **[zeng2022fault]** Muhan Zeng, Yiqian Wu, Zhentao Ye et al. (2022). *Fault localization via efficient probabilistic modeling of program semantics*. ICSE (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.1145/3510003.3510073)
  - Relevance **medium**; role: component prior art; components: anchored cell model
  - SmartFL models only the correctness of program values (not full semantics) with a probabilistic graphical model built from static analysis and execution traces, balancing the precision of semantic approaches and the scalability of SBFL; best top-1 accuracy on Defects4J.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Probabilistic inference over the correctness of values (cells) from passing and failing runs is close to SpecDx's cell-level latent model; SmartFL localises code faults and trusts the test oracle, whereas SpecDx's observations come from fallible readers.
- **[rodler2022random]** Patrick Rodler (2022). *Random vs. Best-First: Impact of Sampling Strategies on Decision Making in Model-Based Diagnosis*. AAAI (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.1609/aaai.v36i5.20531)
  - Relevance **medium**; role: component prior art; components: hitting sets, active synthesis
  - Empirically examines whether biased best-first samples of fault explanations, commonly used to estimate diagnosis probabilities and choose the most informative measurement, are representative, comparing random and best-first sampling methods and sample sizes.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* SpecDx also estimates over sampled correction sets and chooses informative scenarios; this work questions the statistical validity of such samples in MBD.
- **[bendik2020must]** Jaroslav Bendík, Ivana Černá (2020). *MUST: Minimal Unsatisfiable Subsets Enumeration Tool*. TACAS (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A). [link](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-030-45190-5_8) · [code](https://github.com/jar-ben/mustool) ★15
  - Relevance **medium**; role: component prior art; components: MUS/MCS, baseline B5
  - MUST is a tool that implements several domain-agnostic online MUS enumeration algorithms (MARCO, ReMUS, TOME) for SAT, SMT and LTL constraints in one framework.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* A runnable alternative to MARCO for a B5-style symbolic baseline on an SMT encoding of the clauses.
- **[vancsics2020simulating]** Béla Vancsics, Tamás Gergely, Árpád Beszédes (2020). *Simulating the Effect of Test Flakiness on Fault Localization Effectiveness*. VST (peer-reviewed). [link](https://doi.org/10.1109/vst50071.2020.9051636)
  - Relevance **medium**; role: component prior art; components: baseline B3
  - Simulation study of how increasing levels of test flakiness (non-deterministic outcomes) degrade the effectiveness of spectrum-based fault localisation.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Quantifies SFL under noisy test outcomes, the regime of SpecDx's interpreter x clause matrix; it motivates SpecDx's explicit noise model over plain suspiciousness ranking.
- **[kalhauge2019binary]** Christian Gram Kalhauge, Jens Palsberg (2019). *Binary reduction of dependency graphs*. ESEC/FSE (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.1145/3338906.3338956)
  - Relevance **medium**; role: component prior art; components: ddmin
  - Recasts input reduction for inputs with internal dependencies as reduction of a dependency graph and introduces Binary Reduction (tool J-Reduce for Java bytecode), 12x faster than delta debugging with more reduction.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* If sentences of T depend on each other (definitions, cross-references), SpecDx's ddmin could reduce closures in a dependency graph as Binary Reduction does.
- **[elmishali2018artificial]** Amir Elmishali, Roni Stern, Meir Kalech (2018). *An Artificial Intelligence paradigm for troubleshooting software bugs*. EAAI (peer-reviewed, CORE2020 B). [link](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.engappai.2017.12.011)
  - Relevance **medium**; role: component prior art; components: hitting sets, active synthesis
  - LDP integrates machine-learned fault predictions (from code structure, history and past failures) as priors for automated software diagnosis, and automated planning of additional tests to improve diagnostic accuracy, demonstrated on real faults in four open-source projects.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Same pipeline shape as SpecDx (priors -> diagnosis -> active test planning); LDP diagnoses code with Barinel-style reasoning, SpecDx diagnoses specifications with an IRT-style population model.
- **[narodytska2018core]** Nina Narodytska, Nikolaj Bjørner, Maria-Cristina Marinescu et al. (2018). *Core-Guided Minimal Correction Set and Core Enumeration*. IJCAI (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.24963/ijcai.2018/188)
  - Relevance **medium**; role: component prior art; components: MUS/MCS
  - Proposes an algorithm that extracts minimal unsatisfiable cores and correction sets simultaneously within the relaxation-and-strengthening framework; the solver outperforms state-of-the-art MCS extractors and compares favourably on cores.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Simultaneous MUS/MCS enumeration, a stronger symbolic engine for a B5-style baseline.
- **[bendik2018recursive]** Jaroslav Bendík, Ivana Černá, Nikola Beneš (2018). *Recursive Online Enumeration of All Minimal Unsatisfiable Subsets*. ATVA (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 B). [link](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-030-01090-4_9)
  - Relevance **medium**; role: component prior art; components: MUS/MCS
  - Recursive online algorithm (ReMUS) that enumerates MUSes one by one in any constraint domain, reducing the number of expensive satisfiability tests compared with earlier domain-agnostic enumerators.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Domain-agnostic MUS enumeration only needs a satisfiability oracle, so in principle the oracle could be a witness search on PostgreSQL as in SpecDx's refutation step.
- **[troya2018spectrum]** Javier Troya, Sergio Segura, Jose Antonio Parejo et al. (2018). *Spectrum-Based Fault Localization in Model Transformations*. TOSEM (peer-reviewed, CORE2020 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.1145/3241744)
  - Relevance **medium**; role: component prior art; components: baseline B3
  - Applies SBFL to locate faulty rules in model transformations, using test cases and rule coverage, and compares 18 SBFL techniques; Kulczynski2, Mountford, Ochiai and Zoltar perform best.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Shows SFL working on declarative rule sets rather than statements, close to SpecDx's clause-level spectra; tests are assumed to have correct oracles.
- **[perez2017test]** Alexandre Perez, Rui Abreu, Arie van Deursen (2017). *A Test-Suite Diagnosability Metric for Spectrum-Based Fault Localization Approaches*. ICSE (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.1109/icse.2017.66)
