  - Relevance **medium**; role: component prior art; components: active synthesis
  - Proposes DDU (density, diversity, uniqueness), a test-suite diagnosability metric that measures how well tests support spectrum-based fault localisation; optimising suites for DDU gives a 34% gain in SFL accuracy over branch coverage.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Diagnosability of a spectrum matrix is what SpecDx's active scenario synthesis tries to maximise; DDU could serve as a cheap scenario-selection criterion or a diagnostic of the interpreter x clause matrix.
- **[pearson2017evaluating]** Spencer Pearson, Jose Campos, Rene Just et al. (2017). *Evaluating and Improving Fault Localization*. ICSE (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.1109/icse.2017.62)
  - Relevance **medium**; role: evaluation/benchmark; components: mutation evaluation, real-defect benchmark
  - Re-evaluates seven fault localisation techniques on both artificial (mutant) and real faults from Defects4J and finds that results on artificial faults do not carry over to real faults; proposes improved techniques.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Directly informs SpecDx's evaluation design, which uses both seeded specification mutations and real defects; it warns that mutation-only results may not predict real-defect performance.
- **[dewar2017minimal]** Andrew Gainer-Dewar, Paola Vera-Licona (2017). *The Minimal Hitting Set Generation Problem: Algorithms and Computation*. SIAM J. Discrete Math. (peer-reviewed, CORE2020 A). [link](https://doi.org/10.1137/15m1055024)
  - Relevance **medium**; role: survey; components: hitting sets
  - Surveys and benchmarks algorithms that generate all inclusion-minimal hitting sets (hypergraph transversals), comparing many implementations on diverse instances and giving guidance on which to use.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Reference for choosing the hitting-set enumerator in SpecDx step 3 and for its complexity.
- **[wong2016survey]** W. Eric Wong, Ruizhi Gao, Yihao Li et al. (2016). *A Survey on Software Fault Localization*. TSE (peer-reviewed, CORE2020 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.1109/tse.2016.2521368)
  - Relevance **medium**; role: survey
  - Comprehensive survey of software fault localisation techniques (spectrum-based, slicing, statistical, program-state, machine-learning and model-based), with key issues, concerns and evaluation practices.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Reference survey for positioning SpecDx's clause-level localisation against code-level fault localisation.
- **[hodovan2016practical]** Renáta Hodován, Ákos Kiss (2016). *Practical Improvements to the Minimizing Delta Debugging Algorithm*. ICSOFT (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 C). [link](https://doi.org/10.5220/0005988602410248) · [code](https://github.com/renatahodovan/picire) ★54 · **baseline candidate**
  - Relevance **medium**; role: component prior art; components: ddmin
  - Revisits the minimizing delta debugging algorithm and proposes practical improvements, especially parallel testing of subsets and complements, with formal variants and pseudo-code; on more than 1000 real minimisation sessions, ddmin reaches 1-minimal results in a quarter to a fifth of the original time.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Makes ddmin cheaper in oracle wall-clock time, the bottleneck when each test is a population of LLM runs.
- **[groce2015cause]** Alex Groce, Mohammad Amin Alipour, Chaoqiang Zhang et al. (2015). *Cause reduction: delta debugging, even without bugs*. STVR (peer-reviewed, CORE2020 B). [link](https://doi.org/10.1002/stvr.1574)
  - Relevance **medium**; role: component prior art; components: ddmin
  - Generalises delta debugging to cause reduction: tests are reduced while preserving an arbitrary effect (coverage, behaviour) rather than a failure, yielding effective quick test suites and better seeded symbolic execution.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* SpecDx's ddmin also preserves a non-failure property (the persistence of a two-reading split among interpreters), which is cause reduction in Groce et al.'s sense.
- **[silva2015efficient]** Joao Marques-Silva, Mikoláš Janota, Alexey Ignatiev et al. (2015). *Efficient Model Based Diagnosis with Maximum Satisfiability*. IJCAI (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://www.ijcai.org/Abstract/15/279)
  - Relevance **medium**; role: component prior art; components: MUS/MCS, hitting sets
  - Proposes an optimised MaxSAT encoding of model-based diagnosis, building on SAT-based MBD, plus a set of harder MBD benchmarks, with clear performance gains over the state of the art.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* MaxSAT is the natural engine for SpecDx's soft hitting sets (weighted, violable sets to hit); this paper shows MBD at scale through MaxSAT but with hard observations.
- **[mencia2015literal]** Carlos Mencía, Alessandro Previti, Joao Marques-Silva (2015). *Literal-Based MCS Extraction*. IJCAI (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://www.ijcai.org/Abstract/15/280) · [code](https://github.com/pysathq/pysat) ★465
  - Relevance **medium**; role: component prior art; components: MUS/MCS, baseline B5

- **[hofer2015empirical]** Birgit Hofer, Alexandre Perez, Rui Abreu et al. (2015). *On the empirical evaluation of similarity coefficients for spreadsheets fault localization*. ASE journal (peer-reviewed, CORE2020 B). [link](https://doi.org/10.1007/s10515-014-0145-3)
  - Relevance **medium**; role: component prior art; components: baseline B3
  - Studies 42 similarity coefficients for SFL applied to spreadsheets (cells as components, correct/incorrect output cells as observations); Ochiai, Jaccard and Sorensen-Dice need the least inspection effort and are interchangeable.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* SFL on a declarative, cell-based artefact is a close analogue of SpecDx's cells; the observations here are user-judged output cells, assumed correct.
- **[ignatiev2015smallest]** Alexey Ignatiev, Alessandro Previti, Mark Liffiton et al. (2015). *Smallest MUS Extraction with Minimal Hitting Set Dualization*. CP (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A). [link](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-319-23219-5_13)
  - Relevance **medium**; role: component prior art; components: MUS/MCS, hitting sets
  - Computes a smallest MUS of an inconsistent constraint set by minimal hitting set dualization, exploiting the MUS/MCS duality (abstract not available to us; summary from title and citing descriptions).
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* The implicit-hitting-set route from correction sets to a minimum MUS is the exact analogue of SpecDx's soft hitting over sampled correction sets.
- **[bacchus2015using]** Fahiem Bacchus, George Katsirelos (2015). *Using Minimal Correction Sets to More Efficiently Compute Minimal Unsatisfiable Sets*. CAV (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-319-21668-3_5)

  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Same MCS-to-MUS direction as SpecDx step 3; SpecDx's MCSes are not computed by a solver but observed (noisily) as violated-clause sets of interpreter behaviours.
- **[mortensen2015using]** Jonathan M Mortensen, Evan P Minty, Michael Januszyk et al. (2015). *Using the wisdom of the crowds to find critical errors in biomedical ontologies: a study of SNOMED CT*. JAMIA (peer-reviewed, CORE2020 A). [link](https://doi.org/10.1136/amiajnl-2014-002901)
  - Relevance **medium**; role: component prior art; components: anchored cell model, real-defect benchmark

  - *Relation to SpecDx:* A population of fallible raters, aggregated statistically, detects defects in a knowledge base, which is SpecDx's core statistical idea with humans instead of LLMs; it has no logical localisation, typing or witnesses.
- **[liffiton2013enumerating]** Mark H. Liffiton, Ammar Malik (2013). *Enumerating Infeasibility: Finding Multiple MUSes Quickly*. CPAIOR (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 B). [link](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-642-38171-3_11)
  - Relevance **medium**; role: component prior art; components: MUS/MCS

- **[rodler2013rio]** Patrick Rodler, Kostyantyn Shchekotykhin, Philipp Fleiss et al. (2013). *RIO: Minimizing User Interaction in Ontology Debugging*. RR (peer-reviewed). [link](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-642-39666-3_12)

  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Addresses the reliability of probabilistic guidance in sequential diagnosis, a concern SpecDx shares when its interpreter accuracies are estimated rather than known.
- **[felfernig2012efficient]** A. Felfernig, M. Schubert, C. Zehentner (2012). *An efficient diagnosis algorithm for inconsistent constraint sets*. AI EDAM (peer-reviewed). [link](https://doi.org/10.1017/s0890060411000011)

- **[shchekotykhin2012interactive]** Kostyantyn Shchekotykhin, Gerhard Friedrich, Philipp Fleiss et al. (2012). *Interactive ontology debugging: Two query strategies for efficient fault localization*. J. Web Semant. (peer-reviewed, CORE2020 C). [link](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.websem.2011.12.006)
  - Relevance **medium**; role: component prior art; components: hitting sets, active synthesis
  - Interactive ontology debugging that identifies the target diagnosis by asking an oracle (the user) about entailments of the intended ontology, with split-in-half and entropy-based query selection that exploits fault probabilities of typical user errors.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Sequential, information-gain-driven discrimination among diagnoses, as in SpecDx's active scenario synthesis; the oracle here is a human assumed to answer correctly, whereas SpecDx's oracle is a noisy LLM committee.
- **[naish2011model]** Lee Naish, Hua Jie Lee, Kotagiri Ramamohanarao (2011). *A model for spectra-based software diagnosis*. TOSEM (peer-reviewed, CORE2020 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.1145/2000791.2000795)
  - Relevance **medium**; role: component prior art; components: baseline B3
  - Builds a simple single-bug model program to analyse spectral ranking metrics in idealised conditions, identifies groups of equivalent metrics and derives an optimal ranking metric that also performs best on Siemens and Space benchmarks.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Spectral suspiciousness metrics (Ochiai, Naish, etc.) are trivial baselines on SpecDx's interpreter x clause matrix; they rank clauses but cannot distinguish specification defects from agent errors.
- **[davies2011solving]** Jessica Davies, Fahiem Bacchus (2011). *Solving MAXSAT by Solving a Sequence of Simpler SAT Instances*. CP (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A). [link](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-642-23786-7_19)

  - New MaxSAT algorithm that solves a sequence of simpler SAT instances: it collects cores (conflicting soft clauses) and computes minimum-cost hitting sets of them, avoiding the growing difficulty and weighting problems of earlier SAT-sequence approaches; the basis of MaxHS.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Implicit hitting sets with weights is the algorithmic template for SpecDx's soft (weighted) minimal hitting sets over observed correction sets.
- **[feldman2010model]** A. Feldman, G. Provan, A. Van Gemund (2010). *A Model-Based Active Testing Approach to Sequential Diagnosis*. JAIR (peer-reviewed, CORE2020 A). [link](https://doi.org/10.1613/jair.3031)

  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Active test selection to discriminate diagnoses, the MBD counterpart of SpecDx's scenario synthesis; SpecDx chooses business scenarios by Thompson sampling over a statistical model.
- **[feldman2010approximate]** A. Feldman, G. Provan, A. Van Gemund (2010). *Approximate Model-Based Diagnosis Using Greedy Stochastic Search*. JAIR (peer-reviewed, CORE2020 A). [link](https://doi.org/10.1613/jair.3025)

  - *Relation to SpecDx:* An approximate route to diagnoses when exact hitting-set enumeration is too costly, relevant if SpecDx's clause sets grow large; SpecDx's approximation is statistical (soft hitting under noise), not search-based.
- **[benavides2010automated]** David Benavides, Sergio Segura, Antonio Ruiz-Cortés (2010). *Automated analysis of feature models 20 years later: A literature review*. Inf. Syst. (peer-reviewed, CORE2020 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.is.2010.01.001)

  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Feature-model anomalies (void model = contradiction, dead feature, false-optional) form a defect taxonomy for a declarative variability specification and are found symbolically; SpecDx targets executable business specifications and finds defects from executed LLM behaviour.
- **[silva2010minimal]** Joao Marques-Silva (2010). *Minimal Unsatisfiability: Models, Algorithms and Applications (Invited Paper)*. ISMVL (peer-reviewed). [link](https://doi.org/10.1109/ismvl.2010.11)
  - Relevance **medium**; role: survey; components: MUS/MCS
  - Invited survey paper on minimal unsatisfiability: models (MUS, MCS, MSS), algorithms for their extraction and enumeration, and applications (abstract not read; summary from title and venue record).
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Survey entry point for MUS/MCS concepts used in SpecDx's contradiction step.
- **[abreu2009low]** Rui Abreu, Arjan J. C. van Gemund (2009). *A Low-Cost Approximate Minimal Hitting Set Algorithm and its Application to Model-Based Diagnosis*. SARA (peer-reviewed). [link](https://aaai.org/Library/SARA/sara09contents.php)
  - Relevance **medium**; role: component prior art; components: hitting sets
  - Staccato is a low-cost approximate minimal hitting set algorithm that orders elements by a spectrum-based (SFL) statistical heuristic and can stop early with a partial set of approximately minimal hitting sets; orders of magnitude faster than brute force while capturing the important solutions, scaling to millions of variables.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Statistics-guided approximate hitting sets are the nearest prior art to SpecDx's "soft" minimal hitting sets; Staccato prunes by suspiciousness but still treats every failing observation as a hard set to hit.
- **[abreu2009practical]** Rui Abreu, Peter Zoeteweij, Rob Golsteijn et al. (2009). *A practical evaluation of spectrum-based fault localization*. JSS (peer-reviewed, CORE2020 A). [link](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.jss.2009.06.035)
  - Relevance **medium**; role: evaluation/benchmark; components: baseline B3
  - Evaluates the diagnostic accuracy of SFL, which rests on statistical coincidences between failures and component activity, on the Siemens set and the space program as a function of spectrum quality and quantity, parameters that relate to test design.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Standard reference for SFL accuracy limits; SpecDx must beat this kind of statistical coincidence analysis on its interpreter x clause spectra.
- **[kleer2009diagnosing]** Johan de Kleer (2009). *Diagnosing Multiple Persistent and Intermittent Faults*. IJCAI (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://www.ijcai.org/Abstract/09/127)

  - Extends model-based diagnosis, which usually presumes non-intermittent behaviour over few time instants, to intermittent failures observed over time, and shows where to insert probes to isolate the faults that best explain the symptoms at minimum expected cost.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Intermittent faults make the same component fail in some observations but not others, as a specification defect shows up in some interpreter runs but not all; SpecDx models this through interpreter accuracy rather than component intermittency.
- **[kalyanpur2007finding]** Aditya Kalyanpur, Bijan Parsia, Matthew Horridge et al. (2007). *Finding All Justifications of OWL DL Entailments*. ISWC (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 Journal Published). [link](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-540-76298-0_20)

  - Presents algorithms that compute all justifications (minimal axiom sets sufficient for an entailment) of OWL-DL entailments, essential for debugging unsatisfiable classes and contradictions, and shows that a reasoner-independent hitting-set approach works well on real ontologies.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Justification enumeration via Reiter-style hitting-set trees is MUS enumeration for knowledge bases; SpecDx enumerates the analogous minimal clause sets but from sampled behaviour, not from a reasoner.
- **[abreu2007accuracy]** Rui Abreu, Peter Zoeteweij, Arjan J.C. van Gemund (2007). *On the Accuracy of Spectrum-based Fault Localization*. TAIC PART (peer-reviewed). [link](https://doi.org/10.1109/taic.part.2007.13)
  - Relevance **medium**; role: component prior art; components: baseline B3
  - Studies the diagnostic accuracy of spectrum-based fault localisation on the Siemens set as a function of the quality and quantity of spectra and error observations; the Ochiai coefficient is consistently best, and near-optimal accuracy is reached even with low-quality error observations and few tests.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Early evidence that spectrum statistics tolerate poor-quality (noisy) error observations, relevant to SpecDx's noisy interpreter verdicts; Ochiai on the interpreter x clause matrix is the simplest B3-like baseline.
- **[misherghi2006hdd]** Ghassan Misherghi, Zhendong Su (2006). *HDD: hierarchical delta debugging*. ICSE (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.1145/1134285.1134307)

  - HDD applies delta debugging level by level to tree-structured inputs (e.g. XML), from coarse to fine, so all candidates stay syntactically valid; it produces simpler outputs with orders of magnitude fewer tests than flat ddmin.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* A specification T is hierarchical (document, section, clause, sentence); HDD-style reduction would cut SpecDx's oracle calls.
- **[jones2005empirical]** James A. Jones, Mary Jean Harrold (2005). *Empirical evaluation of the tarantula automatic fault-localization technique*. ASE (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.1145/1101908.1101949)
  - Relevance **medium**; role: foundation; components: baseline B3
  - Compares the Tarantula fault-localisation technique with four other techniques on the same subjects; Tarantula is consistently more effective and comparable in cost to the most efficient techniques.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Tarantula is the canonical suspiciousness metric; on SpecDx's interpreter x clause matrix it is a naive baseline that cannot separate agent errors from specification defects.
- **[liblit2005scalable]** Ben Liblit, Mayur Naik, Alice X. Zheng et al. (2005). *Scalable statistical bug isolation*. PLDI (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.1145/1064978.1065014)
  - Relevance **medium**; role: component prior art; components: baseline B3
  - Statistical debugging algorithm that isolates multiple undiagnosed bugs from sampled predicate data of many runs, separating the effects of different bugs and finding predictors associated with each.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Statistics over a population of (user) runs to separate several causes of failure, analogous to SpecDx separating specification defects from scattered agent errors; Liblit et al. localise code predicates and assume a correct failure oracle.
- **[zeller1999yesterday]** Andreas Zeller (1999). *Yesterday, my program worked. Today, it does not. Why?*. ESEC/FSE (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.1145/318774.318946)
  - Relevance **medium**; role: foundation; components: ddmin
  - Introduces delta debugging: given a working "yesterday" version and a failing "today" version, an efficient algorithm isolates a minimal set of failure-inducing changes by systematically testing subsets.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Origin of the ddmin family that SpecDx uses to localise ambiguity among the sentences of T.
- **[eiter1995identifying]** Thomas Eiter, Georg Gottlob (1995). *Identifying the Minimal Transversals of a Hypergraph and Related Problems*. SIAM J. Comput. (peer-reviewed, CORE2020 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.1137/s0097539793250299)
  - Relevance **medium**; role: foundation; components: hitting sets
  - Studies hypergraph saturation and recognition of the transversal hypergraph, relates them to many search problems in computer science (including model-based diagnosis and minimal keys), and analyses their complexity.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Complexity foundation of the minimal-hitting-set/transversal problem that SpecDx's MUS-candidate step must solve.
- **[kleer1992characterizing]** Johan de Kleer, Alan K. Mackworth, Raymond Reiter (1992). *Characterizing diagnoses and systems*. AIJ (peer-reviewed, CORE2020 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.1016/0004-3702(92)90027-u)
  - Relevance **medium**; role: foundation; components: hitting sets
  - Analyses the concept of diagnosis with (prime) implicates and implicants, shows that minimal diagnoses are inadequate in general because supersets of a diagnosis need not be diagnoses, and introduces kernel diagnoses, valid for both consistency-based and abductive views.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Clarifies when minimal hitting sets characterise all diagnoses; relevant because SpecDx's violated-clause sets need not be upward closed.
- **[kleer1990using]** Johan de Kleer (1990). *Using crude probability estimates to guide diagnosis*. AIJ (peer-reviewed, CORE2020 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.1016/0004-3702(90)90012-o)
  - Relevance **medium**; role: foundation; components: hitting sets
  - Shows how crude (order-of-magnitude) prior failure probabilities of components can focus model-based diagnosis on the most probable candidates; the approach generalises to partitions of components with equal failure probability (abstract not available to us beyond this).
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Probability-guided focusing on likely diagnoses, analogous to SpecDx ranking soft hitting sets by posterior weight.
- **[greiner1989correction]** Russell Greiner, Barbara A. Smith, Ralph W. Wilkerson (1989). *A correction to the algorithm in reiter's theory of diagnosis*. AIJ (peer-reviewed, CORE2020 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.1016/0004-3702(89)90079-9)
  - Relevance **medium**; role: foundation; components: hitting sets
  - Shows that Reiter's hitting-set-tree algorithm can miss diagnoses under certain conditions and presents a corrected algorithm (the HS-DAG) with a proof of correctness.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* The corrected hitting-set procedure that any exact implementation of SpecDx's step 3 should follow.
- **[yang2025input]** Boyang Yang, Luyao Ren, Xin Yin et al. (2025). *Input Reduction Enhanced LLM-based Program Repair*. arXiv (preprint). [link](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.15251)
  - Relevance **low**; role: context; components: ddmin
  - ReduceFix prompts an LLM to generate a reducer that minimises failure-inducing test inputs and feeds the reduced inputs to LLM-based program repair, improving pass@10 on a new long-input benchmark (LFTBench).
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Reduction to fight long-context degradation of LLMs; relevant only as LLM-era input reduction.
- **[ren2025validity]** Luyao Ren, Xing Zhang, Ziyue Hua et al. (2025). *Validity-Preserving Delta Debugging via Generator Trace Reduction*. TOSEM (peer-reviewed, CORE2020 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.1145/3705305)
  - Relevance **low**; role: context; components: ddmin
  - GReduce reduces bug-inducing inputs by searching over executions of the input generator, so that reduced inputs remain valid with respect to specifications beyond syntax.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Validity-preserving reduction; SpecDx's reduced T must stay a readable specification, a validity constraint of the same kind.
- **[wang2023probabilistic]** Guancheng Wang, Yiqian Wu, Qihao Zhu et al. (2023). *A Probabilistic Delta Debugging Approach for Abstract Syntax Trees*. ISSRE (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A). [link](https://doi.org/10.1109/issre59848.2023.00060)
  - Relevance **low**; role: context; components: ddmin
  - Integrates ProbDD's probabilistic model into AST-based, domain-specific delta debugging (Perses-style), improving efficiency and effectiveness of program minimisation.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* ProbDD follow-up for hierarchical inputs; relevant if T is reduced hierarchically (document > section > sentence).
- **[gruber2023debugging]** Martin Gruber, Gordon Fraser (2023). *Debugging Flaky Tests using Spectrum-based Fault Localization*. AST (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 C). [link](https://doi.org/10.1109/ast58925.2023.00017)
  - Relevance **low**; role: context; components: baseline B3
  - SFFL extends coverage-based SFL to locate the root causes of flaky tests, exploiting the observation that most flaky tests show varying coverage across runs and distinguishing stable from flaky coverage.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Uses run-to-run variation of spectra as signal, as SpecDx uses interpreter-to-interpreter variation.
- **[kalhauge2021logical]** Christian Gram Kalhauge, Jens Palsberg (2021). *Logical bytecode reduction*. PLDI (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.1145/3453483.3454091)
  - Relevance **low**; role: context; components: ddmin
  - Specifies valid sub-inputs with a propositional formula over fine-grained dependencies and runs a reduction algorithm that finds a small, valid, failure-inducing input, giving much more reduction than J-Reduce on Java bytecode.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Validity-preserving reduction via logic; relevant if reduced versions of T must stay well-formed.
- **[qin2021impact]** Yihao Qin, Shangwen Wang, Kui Liu et al. (2021). *On the Impact of Flaky Tests in Automated Program Repair*. SANER (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A). [link](https://doi.org/10.1109/saner50967.2021.00035)
  - Relevance **low**; role: context; components: baseline B3
  - Reintroduces flaky tests into Defects4J and shows that although they are few they affect most bugs and drastically hinder spectrum-based fault localisation and automated program repair.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Evidence that SFL breaks under even small amounts of outcome noise, as in SpecDx's noisy behavioural spectra.
- **[kreutzer2021test]** Patrick Kreutzer, Tom Kunze, Michael Philippsen (2021). *Test Case Reduction: A Framework, Benchmark, and Comparative Study*. ICSME (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A). [link](https://doi.org/10.1109/icsme52107.2021.00012)
  - Relevance **low**; role: evaluation/benchmark; components: ddmin
  - Provides a framework with tuned implementations of six language-agnostic reducers, a benchmark of 321 bug-inducing programs in two languages (110 real compiler bugs), and a comparative study finding that no reducer is best across all cases.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Methodological model for comparing ddmin variants (ddmin vs ProbDD/CDD) in SpecDx's ambiguity-localisation ablation.
- **[aldridge2019group]** Matthew Aldridge, Oliver Johnson, Jonathan Scarlett (2019). *Group Testing: An Information Theory Perspective*. FnT CIT (peer-reviewed). [link](https://doi.org/10.1561/9781680835977)
  - Relevance **low**; role: survey; components: hitting sets, identifiability
  - Monograph surveying group testing (identifying a few defective items from tests on pools, positive if a pool contains a defective) from an information-theoretic view: practical algorithms, achievability and converse bounds, including noisy test models.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Group testing with noisy tests is the information-theoretic cousin of SpecDx's problem (which clauses are defective, from noisy pooled pass/fail behaviour); its bounds could inform how many interpreter runs are needed.
- **[sun2018perses]** Chengnian Sun, Yuanbo Li, Qirun Zhang et al. (2018). *Perses: syntax-guided program reduction*. ICSE (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.1145/3180155.3180236) · [code](https://github.com/uw-pluverse/perses) ★215
  - Relevance **low**; role: context; components: ddmin
  - Perses performs syntax-guided program reduction, using the formal grammar so that every reduction step considers only smaller, syntactically valid variants, outperforming DD, HDD and C-Reduce in speed and output size while remaining language-general.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Validity-aware reduction framework; the uw-pluverse/perses repository also hosts later reducers from the same group.
- **[hodovan2017coarse]** Renata Hodovan, Akos Kiss, Tibor Gyimothy (2017). *Coarse Hierarchical Delta Debugging*. ICSME (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A). [link](https://doi.org/10.1109/icsme.2017.26) · [code](https://github.com/renatahodovan/picireny) ★60
  - Relevance **low**; role: context; components: ddmin
  - Proposes Coarse HDD, a variant of hierarchical delta debugging for tree-structured (grammar-parsed) inputs; the authors maintain the picireny HDD framework (abstract not read; summary from the publication record and tool description).
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* picireny/picire (by the same authors) are maintained Python ddmin/HDD frameworks that SpecDx could reuse for sentence- or section-level reduction of T with a custom population oracle.
- **[jannach2015parallelized]** Dietmar Jannach, Thomas Schmitz, Kostyantyn Shchekotykhin (2015). *Parallelized Hitting Set Computation for Model-Based Diagnosis*. AAAI (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.1609/aaai.v29i1.9389)
  - Relevance **low**; role: context; components: hitting sets
  - Parallelises the construction of Reiter's breadth-first hitting-set tree on multi-core CPUs without losing diagnoses, significantly reducing running times on benchmark problems, and studies how problem structure affects the gains.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Engineering option for exact hitting-set enumeration if SpecDx's candidate step becomes a bottleneck.
- **[shaikh2014feedback]** Asadullah Shaikh, Uffe Kock Wiil (2014). *A feedback technique for unsatisfiable UML/OCL class diagrams*. SPE (peer-reviewed, CORE2020 B). [link](https://doi.org/10.1002/spe.2211)
  - Relevance **low**; role: context; components: MUS/MCS, baseline B5
  - Feedback technique that slices a UML/OCL class diagram into submodels, detects unsatisfiable submodels and reports the integrity constraints responsible, so developers can focus on the faulty part of the model.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Unsatisfiable-core feedback for a declarative data model with integrity constraints, close to SpecDx's database invariants; it is purely symbolic and finds only contradictions.
- **[regehr2012test]** John Regehr, Yang Chen, Pascal Cuoq et al. (2012). *Test-case reduction for C compiler bugs*. PLDI (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.1145/2254064.2254104) · [code](https://github.com/csmith-project/creduce) ★1673
  - Relevance **low**; role: context; components: ddmin
  - Presents C-Reduce and related domain-specific reducers that go beyond substring-removing delta debugging by applying C-aware transformations, producing much smaller compiler-bug test cases while avoiding undefined behaviour.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Shows the value of domain-aware reduction transformations; SpecDx's sentence-level ddmin over T is generic and could in principle use semantics-aware transformations of requirement text.
- **[siddiqi2011sequential]** S. A. Siddiqi, J. Huang (2011). *Sequential Diagnosis by Abstraction*. JAIR (peer-reviewed, CORE2020 A). [link](https://doi.org/10.1613/jair.3296)
  - Relevance **low**; role: context; components: active synthesis
  - Sequential diagnosis framework that chooses measurement points with an efficiently computed heuristic not requiring the full set of diagnoses, plus abstraction techniques, scaling entropy-style measurement selection to much larger systems.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Scalable next-measurement selection, related to SpecDx's choice of the next scenario.
- **[white2010automated]** J. White, D. Benavides, D.C. Schmidt et al. (2010). *Automated diagnosis of feature model configurations*. JSS (peer-reviewed, CORE2020 A). [link](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.jss.2010.02.017)
  - Relevance **low**; role: context; components: MUS/MCS
  - Transforms feature models and invalid product-line configurations into constraint satisfaction problems to automatically diagnose configuration errors and propose minimal repairs of feature selections (the CURE approach).
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Diagnosis of a configuration against a variability specification by CSP; SpecDx diagnoses the specification itself rather than a configuration.
- **[felfernig2009plausible]** Alexander Felfernig, Gerhard Friedrich, Monika Schubert et al. (2009). *Plausible Repairs for Inconsistent Requirements*. IJCAI (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://www.ijcai.org/Abstract/09/136)
  - Relevance **low**; role: context; components: MUS/MCS
  - For knowledge-based recommenders whose user requirements admit no recommendation, integrates the computation of minimal faulty-requirement explanations with collaborative problem solving to propose personalised, plausible repairs.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Uses population (collaborative) data to rank diagnoses of an inconsistent requirement set, a distant analogue of SpecDx's population statistics; the requirements are user preferences, not a specification.
- **[lamperti2002diagnosis]** Gianfranco Lamperti, Marina Zanella (2002). *Diagnosis of discrete-event systems from uncertain temporal observations*. AIJ (peer-reviewed, CORE2020 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.1016/s0004-3702(02)00123-6)
  - Relevance **low**; role: context
  - Diagnosis of discrete-event (active) systems when the observation channel may add spurious messages, lose messages or lose temporal ordering, so observations are uncertain and underconstrained; illustrated on power transmission networks.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* An early treatment of corrupted observations in diagnosis; SpecDx's observations are corrupted by interpreter errors rather than a lossy channel.

<a id="t6_llm_disagreement_ambiguity"></a>
## LLM disagreement, uncertainty and ambiguity; differential and N-version testing

- **[mehta2026agents]** Aman Mehta (2026). *When Agents Disagree With Themselves: Behavioral Consistency as an Uncertainty Signal for LLM Agents*. arXiv (preprint). [link](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.11619) · **baseline candidate**
  - Relevance **high**; role: component prior art; components: baseline B4, family effects/correlated errors
  - Runs the same LLM agent repeatedly on identical inputs and uses the number of distinct action sequences as a training-free uncertainty signal; on HotpotQA (8,000 runs, four models) and SWE-bench, consistent tasks are far more accurate, divergence concentrates at early steps, and agreement-based selective prediction matches split-conformal baselines.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Uses the variance of agent trajectories across repeated runs as a signal, as SpecDx does with its run populations; it attributes divergence to task difficulty/agent uncertainty, while SpecDx localises divergence to cells and steps and separates specification defects from agent errors.
- **[kim2025correlated]** Elliot Myunghoon Kim, Avi Garg, Kenny Peng et al. (2025). *Correlated Errors in Large Language Models*. ICML (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://proceedings.mlr.press/v267/kim25e.html)
  - Relevance **high**; role: foundation; component prior art; also in t1_truth_inference; components: family effects/correlated errors, identifiability
  - Evaluates more than 350 LLMs on two leaderboards and a resume-screening task and finds strongly correlated errors (models agree 60% of the time when both err); shared architecture and provider drive correlation, but larger, more accurate models are highly correlated even across providers, with consequences for LLM-as-judge and hiring.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Direct empirical challenge to SpecDx's assumption that errors are independent across model families: consensus deviations may reflect shared model biases rather than specification defects. SpecDx should cite it, measure within/across-family error correlation on its populations, and rely on anchor contrasts and refutation witnesses rather than consensus alone. | It is direct empirical evidence against SpecDx's cross-family independence assumption, and especially strong for capable models. SpecDx must quantify residual cross-family correlation, e.g. by a CAPA-style statistic on its matrix, and show that its defect calls survive it.
- **[goel2025great]** Shashwat Goel, Joschka Strüber, Ilze Amanda Auzina et al. (2025). *Great Models Think Alike and this Undermines AI Oversight*. ICML (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://proceedings.mlr.press/v267/goel25b.html)
  - Relevance **high**; role: component prior art; also in t1_truth_inference; components: family effects/correlated errors, identifiability, baseline B4
  - Proposes CAPA (Chance Adjusted Probabilistic Agreement), a metric of LM similarity based on overlap in mistakes; shows that LLM judges favour similar models, weak-to-strong gains are larger for dissimilar models, and model mistakes become more similar as capability grows.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* CAPA is a ready metric for quantifying error correlation between SpecDx's model families, which its identifiability theory assumes away; the finding that stronger models err more alike suggests that SpecDx's accuracy-gradient signatures must account for capability-dependent correlation. | CAPA is a ready-made statistic for SpecDx's family effects: compute it between interpreters on clause outcomes to check the independence assumption and to weight families. It is also a reminder that a committee of similar models over-agrees.
- **[wu2025humanevalcomm]** Jie Jw Wu, Fatemeh H. Fard (2025). *HumanEvalComm: Benchmarking the Communication Competence of Code Generation for LLMs and LLM Agent*. TOSEM (peer-reviewed, CORE2020 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.1145/3715109) · [code](https://github.com/jie-jw-wu/human-eval-comm) ★11
  - Relevance **high**; role: evaluation/benchmark; also in t3_requirements_nlp; components: defect taxonomy, mutation evaluation
  - Builds HumanEvalComm by modifying HumanEval problem descriptions to be ambiguous, inconsistent or incomplete, and measures whether code LLMs and an LLM agent (Okanagan) ask clarifying questions instead of coding; most models rarely ask questions even when the requirement is defective.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Seeds requirement defects (ambiguity, inconsistency, incompleteness) into specifications, which mirrors SpecDx's specification mutation operators and its defect taxonomy; HumanEvalComm evaluates individual models' communication, whereas SpecDx uses many fallible executors to detect and type the seeded defect, and its mutated problems could serve as an external evaluation set. | Builds seeded requirement defects (ambiguity, inconsistency, incompleteness) by mutating problem statements, which parallels SpecDx's mutation operators; it evaluates interpreters' reactions to defects rather than diagnosing the defects.
- **[zhang2025stress]** Jifan Zhang, Henry Sleight, Andi Peng et al. (2025). *Stress-Testing Model Specs Reveals Character Differences among Language Models*. arXiv (preprint). [link](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.07686) · **baseline candidate**
  - Relevance **high**; role: direct competitor; components: baseline B4, family effects/correlated errors, defect taxonomy
  - Stress-tests AI model specifications by generating value-tradeoff scenarios that pit pairs of principles against each other, collecting responses from twelve frontier LLMs, and measuring behavioural disagreement; high cross-model divergence (over 70,000 cases) is shown to predict specification problems such as direct contradictions and interpretive ambiguities.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Closest in spirit: cross-model behavioural disagreement used to find contradictions and ambiguities in a natural-language specification. It differs in that the specification has no executable checks (no anchor), disagreement is the only signal (no latent executor-accuracy model, no hidden-requirement or weak-check types), diagnosis is qualitative rather than localised with witnesses, and it targets value principles rather than business specifications.
- **[farquhar2024detecting]** Sebastian Farquhar, Jannik Kossen, Lorenz Kuhn et al. (2024). *Detecting hallucinations in large language models using semantic entropy*. Nature (peer-reviewed). [link](https://doi.org/10.1038/s41586-024-07421-0) · [code](https://github.com/jlko/semantic_uncertainty) ★430 · **baseline candidate**
  - Relevance **high**; role: component prior art; components: baseline B4
  - Uses semantic entropy, computed over entailment-based clusters of sampled answers, to detect confabulations (arbitrary, incorrect generations) of LLMs across datasets and domains without task-specific data, extending it to longer passages by decomposing them into factual claims.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* The main published, code-backed form of the entropy-of-meanings signal that SpecDx's B4 baseline uses; it diagnoses the model's own uncertainty, whereas SpecDx attributes cross-family disagreement to the specification versus the executors.
- **[lin2024generating]** Zhen Lin, Shubhendu Trivedi, Jimeng Sun (2024). *Generating with Confidence: Uncertainty Quantification for Black-box Large Language Models*. TMLR (peer-reviewed). [link](https://iclr.cc/virtual/2025/poster/31486) · [code](https://github.com/zlin7/UQ-NLG) ★106 · **baseline candidate**
  - Relevance **high**; role: component prior art; components: baseline B4
  - Studies uncertainty quantification for black-box LLM generation, distinguishes uncertainty (dispersion of possible outputs) from confidence (in a given output), and proposes similarity-graph measures (e.g. number of semantic sets, eigenvalue-based dispersion, degree) for selective generation; simple semantic-dispersion measures predict response quality well.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Its graph-based dispersion measures over sampled outputs are drop-in disagreement statistics for SpecDx's B4; like semantic entropy, they measure a model's uncertainty, not whether a specification's declared value is defective.
- **[fakhoury2024llm]** Sarah Fakhoury, Aaditya Naik, Georgios Sakkas et al. (2024). *LLM-Based Test-Driven Interactive Code Generation: User Study and Empirical Evaluation*. TSE (peer-reviewed, CORE2020 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.1109/tse.2024.3428972)
  - Relevance **high**; role: component prior art; context; also in t3_requirements_nlp; components: active synthesis, set-valued truth
  - Proposes TiCoder, an interactive workflow in which an LLM generates candidate code and tests, the user approves or rejects tests to partially formalise intent, and code suggestions are pruned and ranked accordingly; a 15-programmer user study and benchmark evaluation show large gains in pass@1 with few interactions.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Uses tests that discriminate among sampled program interpretations to resolve NL intent ambiguity, which parallels SpecDx's active scenario synthesis that chooses scenarios to maximise information about latent readings; TiCoder needs a human oracle and targets code suggestions, whereas SpecDx is fully automatic and diagnoses defects in the specification itself, including its checks. | Uses executable tests to disambiguate NL intent, choosing tests that discriminate between candidate programs, akin to SpecDx's information-gain scenario synthesis; it relies on a user as the arbiter rather than checks C.
- **[li2023nuances]** Tsz-On Li, Wenxi Zong, Yibo Wang et al. (2023). *Nuances are the Key: Unlocking ChatGPT to Find Failure-Inducing Tests with Differential Prompting*. ASE (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.1109/ase56229.2023.00089) · **baseline candidate**
  - Relevance **high**; role: component prior art; components: refutation, baseline B4
  - Shows that ChatGPT rarely finds failure-inducing tests directly (28.8%) and proposes Differential Prompting: the LLM infers the intended behaviour of a buggy program, synthesises several reference versions, and tests inputs on which the versions and the program under test disagree; it reaches about 75% success on QuixBugs and 66.7% on Codeforces.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Uses LLM-generated program versions as an N-version oracle and treats behavioural disagreement as a failure signal, the same intuition as SpecDx's population of executors; SpecDx asks a different question (is the specification, not the program, defective?) and adds a statistical latent model plus logical refutation to separate executor error from specification defects.
- **[zhang2023sac3]** Jiaxin Zhang, Zhuohang Li, Kamalika Das et al. (2023). *SAC3: Reliable Hallucination Detection in Black-Box Language Models via Semantic-aware Cross-check Consistency*. EMNLP Findings (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.18653/v1/2023.findings-emnlp.1032) · **baseline candidate**
  - Relevance **high**; role: component prior art; components: baseline B4, family effects/correlated errors
  - Finds that self-consistency checks miss question-level and model-level hallucinations and proposes SAC3, which adds semantically equivalent question perturbations and cross-model response checks with a verifier LM to sampling-based consistency.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Combines input-rephrasing consistency (like SpecDx's Tell variants) with cross-model consistency (like SpecDx's model families), the closest NLP analogue of B4; it scores answer reliability, whereas SpecDx contrasts consensus against an anchored specification value to type specification defects.
- **[manakul2023selfcheckgpt]** Potsawee Manakul, Adian Liusie, Mark Gales (2023). *SelfCheckGPT: Zero-Resource Black-Box Hallucination Detection for Generative Large Language Models*. EMNLP (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.18653/v1/2023.emnlp-main.557) · [code](https://github.com/potsawee/selfcheckgpt) ★635 · **baseline candidate**
  - Relevance **high**; role: component prior art; components: baseline B4, family effects/correlated errors
  - Proposes SelfCheckGPT, a zero-resource black-box hallucination detector that samples several responses from the same LLM and scores each sentence of the main response by its consistency (BERTScore, QA, n-gram, NLI or prompting variants) with the samples; inconsistent facts are flagged as likely hallucinations.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Shares the premise that disagreement among stochastic samples signals unreliability; SpecDx instead uses the cross-family pattern of agreement against an anchored specification value to separate specification defects (consistent deviation) from agent errors (scattered deviation), whereas SelfCheckGPT treats consistency only as evidence of model correctness.
- **[kuhn2023semantic]** Lorenz Kuhn, Yarin Gal, Sebastian Farquhar (2023). *Semantic Uncertainty: Linguistic Invariances for Uncertainty Estimation in Natural Language Generation*. ICLR (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://iclr.cc/virtual/2023/poster/11013) · [code](https://github.com/lorenzkuhn/semantic_uncertainty) ★189 · **baseline candidate**
  - Relevance **high**; role: component prior art; components: baseline B4
  - Introduces semantic entropy: sample several generations, cluster them into meaning-equivalence classes with bidirectional entailment, and compute entropy over the classes; it predicts QA accuracy better than token-level baselines without model modification.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Semantic entropy over meaning clusters is the single-model analogue of SpecDx's B4 inter-family disagreement entropy; SpecDx goes further by anchoring readings to the specification's declared value, modelling executor accuracy and item difficulty, and separating ambiguity (two stable readings) from agent noise, which entropy alone conflates.
- **[liu2023afraid]** Alisa Liu, Zhaofeng Wu, Julian Michael et al. (2023). *We’re Afraid Language Models Aren’t Modeling Ambiguity*. EMNLP (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.18653/v1/2023.emnlp-main.51) · [code](https://github.com/alisawuffles/ambient) ★72
  - Relevance **high**; role: foundation; components: set-valued truth, defect taxonomy
  - Characterises ambiguity in a sentence by its effect on entailment relations and releases AmbiEnt, a linguist-annotated benchmark of 1,645 NLI examples with diverse ambiguity types; tests show that pretrained LMs (including GPT-4) rarely recognise or disentangle the possible readings.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Its entailment-based definition of ambiguity (a premise entails different hypotheses under different readings) is close to SpecDx's entailment set A_c; SpecDx operationalises this at the level of executable cells and detects it from executor behaviour rather than asking a model whether a sentence is ambiguous, and the paper's finding that single LMs miss ambiguity motivates a population-based detector.
- **[park2021jest]** Jihyeok Park, Seungmin An, Dongjun Youn et al. (2021). *JEST: N+1-Version Differential Testing of Both JavaScript Engines and Specification*. ICSE (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.1109/icse43902.2021.00015) · [code](https://github.com/kaist-plrg/jest) · **baseline candidate**
  - Relevance **high**; role: direct competitor; components: defect taxonomy, family effects/correlated errors, ddmin, baseline B2
  - Proposes N+1-version differential testing: synthesise programs from the ECMAScript specification, inject assertions derived from the specification's semantics into conformance tests, run them on several JavaScript engines, treat a failure shared by most engines as a specification bug and a minority failure as an engine bug, and localise specification bugs statistically; JEST found 44 engine bugs and 27 bugs in ES11.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* The closest precedent for SpecDx's core move: the specification is one more fallible 'version', and a deviation shared by most independent implementations indicts the specification rather than the implementations, with statistical localisation in the spec text. SpecDx differs in using stochastic LLM interpreters of a business specification (so it needs an explicit reliability/difficulty model rather than majority voting over near-correct engines), in typing defects (contradiction, hidden requirement, ambiguity, weak check) with MUS/MCS refutation and witnesses, and in handling set-valued truth.
- **[chen2016coverage]** Yuting Chen, Ting Su, Chengnian Sun et al. (2016). *Coverage-directed differential testing of JVM implementations*. PLDI (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.1145/2908080.2908095)
  - Relevance **high**; role: component prior art; components: active synthesis, mutation evaluation
  - Proposes classfuzz, which mutates seed classfiles with predefined mutators chosen by MCMC sampling, keeps mutants with unique coverage on a reference JVM, and runs them on several JVMs to expose discrepancies; it raises the discrepancy-triggering ratio from 1.7% to 11.9%, and several of the 62 reported discrepancies matched clarifications later made to the JVM specification.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Shows that discrepancies among implementations can reveal gaps in the specification itself, and guides input generation adaptively, both ideas central to SpecDx; it relies on a human to decide whether a discrepancy is a JVM bug or a specification issue, which SpecDx automates through its latent model and refutation.
- **[fang2026clarifycodebench]** Zheng Fang, Dongming Jin, Yihong dong et al. (2026). *ClarifyCodeBench: Evaluating LLMs on Clarifying Ambiguous Requirements for Code Generation*. arXiv (preprint). [link](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.00711)
  - Relevance **medium**; role: evaluation/benchmark; components: defect taxonomy
  - ClarifyCodeBench is an interactive benchmark that evaluates whether LLMs resolve ambiguous, incomplete or underspecified code-generation requirements by asking clarifying questions instead of coding one-shot.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* A 2026 benchmark of requirement defects in code generation; it evaluates a single model's clarification behaviour, whereas SpecDx detects such defects from population behaviour without interaction.
- **[liang2026code]** Yunhao Liang, Chengguang Gan, Ruixuan Ying et al. (2026). *Do Code Language Models Follow Tests? Paired Interventions on Program Behavior*. arXiv (preprint). [link](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.26244)
  - Relevance **medium**; role: context; components: anchored cell model, set-valued truth
  - Measures whether code LMs follow the rule expressed by visible tests by holding an underspecified description fixed and changing example outputs to express one of two valid rules; switching rates across five models range from 11.1% to 65.8%.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Directly probes how executors weigh NL text versus executable examples when the text admits two readings, the situation SpecDx models with set-valued entailment sets anchored by d_c; it suggests executor accuracy toward anchors varies widely by model.
- **[ron2026galapagos]** Javier Ron, Diogo Gaspar, Javier Cabrera-Arteaga et al. (2026). *Galápagos: Automated N-Version Programming with LLMs*. TOSEM (peer-reviewed, CORE2020 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.1145/3785363)
  - Relevance **medium**; role: component prior art; components: family effects/correlated errors
  - Galápagos uses LLMs to generate diverse variants of real-world C functions, validates their correctness and equivalence, and assembles N-version binaries, showing that the variants are provably equivalent yet diverse enough to tolerate some faults.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Revives N-version programming with LLMs as the source of diversity, the same population idea SpecDx uses; Galápagos targets runtime fault tolerance of programs, whereas SpecDx uses LLM 'versions' of a specification's interpretation to diagnose the specification.
- **[vashurin2025benchmarking]** Roman Vashurin, Ekaterina Fadeeva, Artem Vazhentsev et al. (2025). *Benchmarking Uncertainty Quantification Methods for Large Language Models with LM-Polygraph*. TACL (peer-reviewed). [link](https://doi.org/10.1162/tacl_a_00737) · [code](https://github.com/IINemo/lm-polygraph) ★524
  - Relevance **medium**; role: survey; components: baseline B4
  - Introduces a benchmark built on LM-Polygraph that implements state-of-the-art UQ baselines and evaluates them consistently across text generation tasks, including confidence normalisation, in a large empirical study.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* The most systematic comparison of the disagreement and uncertainty measures that SpecDx's B4 baseline family draws on; it helps choose which B4 variants to report.
- **[wang2025learning]** Wenxuan Wang, Shi Juluan, Zixuan Ling et al. (2025). *Learning to Ask: When LLM Agents Meet Unclear Instruction*. EMNLP (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.18653/v1/2025.emnlp-main.1104)
  - Relevance **medium**; role: context; components: defect taxonomy
  - Analyses real user instructions to tool-using LLM agents, builds Noisy ToolBench with imperfect (e.g. missing-argument) instructions, finds agents tend to hallucinate missing arguments, and proposes a framework that prompts agents to ask questions when instructions are unclear.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Shows that tool-using agents fill specification gaps by guessing, which is the behaviour that makes SpecDx's populations informative: guesses scatter across agents for underspecified inputs, producing ambiguity signatures.
- **[huang2025look]** Yuheng Huang, Jiayang Song, Zhijie Wang et al. (2025). *Look Before You Leap: An Exploratory Study of Uncertainty Analysis for Large Language Models*. TSE (peer-reviewed, CORE2020 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.1109/tse.2024.3519464)
  - Relevance **medium**; role: context; components: baseline B4
  - An exploratory SE study applying twelve uncertainty estimation methods to LLMs across NLP and code generation tasks, assessing how well uncertainty signals reveal erroneous predictions.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Shows that sampling- and model-based uncertainty only partially predicts LLM errors in SE tasks; SpecDx uses disagreement not to predict model errors but to diagnose specifications.
- **[yang2025prompts]** Chenyang Yang, Yike Shi, Qianou Ma et al. (2025). *What Prompts Don't Say: Understanding and Managing Underspecification in LLM Prompts*. arXiv (preprint). [link](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.13360)
  - Relevance **medium**; role: context; components: defect taxonomy
  - Analyses underspecified prompts: LLMs infer unspecified requirements 41.1% of the time, but such behaviour is fragile (twice as likely to regress across model or prompt changes), and specifying everything does not reliably help because requirements can conflict; requirements-aware prompt optimisation improves performance by 4.8%.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Documents that unspecified requirements are filled inconsistently across models and prompts, which is the variation SpecDx exploits with model families and Tell variants to expose hidden requirements.
- **[chen2024optimal]** Mouxiang Chen, Zhongxin Liu, He Tao et al. (2024). *B4: Towards Optimal Assessment of Plausible Code Solutions with Plausible Tests*. ASE (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.1145/3691620.3695536)
  - Relevance **medium**; role: component prior art; components: anchored cell model, item difficulty/IRT
  - Shows that, in a Bayesian framework, the optimal strategy for selecting among plausible generated code solutions using plausible generated tests is defined by the posterior of passing states, and derives an efficient approximation (B4) that outperforms heuristic code-test agreement strategies.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* A principled latent-variable treatment of mutually unreliable code and tests, close in spirit to SpecDx's treatment of the specification checks as a fallible rater; it aims to pick a correct program, not to diagnose the tests or the specification.
- **[xiong2024llms]** Miao Xiong, Zhiyuan Hu, Xinyang Lu et al. (2024). *Can LLMs Express Their Uncertainty? An Empirical Evaluation of Confidence Elicitation in LLMs*. ICLR (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://iclr.cc/virtual/2024/poster/18135) · [code](https://github.com/MiaoXiong2320/llm-uncertainty) ★150
  - Relevance **medium**; role: context; components: baseline B1
  - Benchmarks black-box confidence elicitation along prompting (verbalised confidence), sampling and aggregation; LLMs verbalising confidence are overconfident, calibration improves with scale but stays far from ideal, and consistency among multiple samples helps mitigate overconfidence.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Shows that self-reported confidence is unreliable and that sample consistency is a better signal, which supports SpecDx's choice of behavioural readings over asking interpreters whether T is ambiguous; it concerns per-answer confidence, not specification defects.
- **[zhang2024clamber]** Tong Zhang, Peixin Qin, Yang Deng et al. (2024). *CLAMBER: A Benchmark of Identifying and Clarifying Ambiguous Information Needs in Large Language Models*. ACL (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.18653/v1/2024.acl-long.578) · [code](https://github.com/zt991211/CLAMBER) ★23
  - Relevance **medium**; role: evaluation/benchmark; components: defect taxonomy
  - Presents CLAMBER, a taxonomy-driven benchmark of about 12K queries for evaluating whether LLMs identify and clarify ambiguous information needs; off-the-shelf LLMs show limited ability, and CoT or few-shot prompting can make them overconfident.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Provides a taxonomy of ambiguity (epistemic misalignment, ambiguity, aleatoric output) and evidence that single LLMs are poor ambiguity judges, supporting SpecDx's choice of a behavioural population oracle over self-reported ambiguity; it targets user queries, not executable specifications.
- **[rao2024diffspec]** Nikitha Rao, Elizabeth Gilbert, Harrison Green et al. (2024). *DiffSpec: Differential Testing with LLMs using Natural Language Specifications and Code Artifacts*. arXiv (preprint). [link](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.04249)
  - Relevance **medium**; role: component prior art; components: active synthesis
  - DiffSpec uses prompt chaining over natural-language specifications (e.g. ISA documents, RFCs), bug reports and code to make LLMs generate targeted differential tests; it produced 1,901 differentiating tests for eBPF runtimes and 299 for Wasm validators, revealing confirmed bugs.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* LLMs read a natural-language specification to synthesise inputs that split implementations, close to SpecDx's clause-guided scenario synthesis; DiffSpec blames implementations, whereas SpecDx's executors are LLMs and the target is the specification.
- **[du2024improving]** Yilun Du, Shuang Li, Antonio Torralba et al. (2024). *Improving Factuality and Reasoning in Language Models through Multiagent Debate*. ICML (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://proceedings.mlr.press/v235/du24e.html) · [code](https://github.com/composable-models/llm_multiagent_debate) ★554
  - Relevance **medium**; role: context; components: family effects/correlated errors
  - Has several LLM instances propose answers and debate them over multiple rounds until they converge on a common answer, improving mathematical and strategic reasoning and reducing hallucinated facts.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Debate deliberately makes agents' answers dependent, which is the opposite of SpecDx's requirement of independent readings across families; SpecDx keeps interpreters isolated so that agreement is evidence about the specification rather than a product of persuasion.
- **[nikitin2024kernel]** Alexander Nikitin, Jannik Kossen, Yarin Gal et al. (2024). *Kernel Language Entropy: Fine-grained Uncertainty Quantification for LLMs from Semantic Similarities*. NeurIPS (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.52202/079017-0283) · [code](https://github.com/AlexanderVNikitin/kernel-language-entropy) ★37 · **baseline candidate**
  - Relevance **medium**; role: component prior art; components: baseline B4
  - Kernel Language Entropy generalises semantic entropy by computing the von Neumann entropy of a positive semidefinite kernel over pairwise semantic similarities of sampled answers, capturing graded rather than all-or-nothing equivalence.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* A finer-grained version of the entropy statistic behind B4, useful where readings are partially similar (e.g. near-equal numeric values or overlapping text); like semantic entropy it lacks an anchor and executor model.
- **[chen2023codet]** Bei Chen, Fengji Zhang, Anh Nguyen et al. (2023). *CodeT: Code Generation with Generated Tests*. ICLR (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://iclr.cc/virtual/2023/poster/12011) · [code](https://github.com/microsoft/CodeT)
  - Relevance **medium**; role: component prior art; components: anchored cell model
  - CodeT has the same LLM generate both code samples and test cases, then ranks samples by dual execution agreement, i.e. consistency of a solution's outputs with generated tests and with other solutions; it improves HumanEval pass@1 to 65.8%.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Jointly scores two fallible artefacts (code and tests) by their mutual agreement, analogous to SpecDx scoring interpreters and the specification's checks against each other; CodeT selects code, while SpecDx targets defects in the checks and requirements.
- **[fadeeva2023polygraph]** Ekaterina Fadeeva, Roman Vashurin, Akim Tsvigun et al. (2023). *LM-Polygraph: Uncertainty Estimation for Language Models*. EMNLP Demo (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.18653/v1/2023.emnlp-demo.41) · [code](https://github.com/IINemo/lm-polygraph) ★524 · **baseline candidate**
  - Relevance **medium**; role: component prior art; components: baseline B4
  - LM-Polygraph is a Python framework implementing a battery of uncertainty estimation methods for LLM text generation behind unified interfaces, with a benchmark and a demo application.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* A ready, offline toolkit for computing many black-box disagreement-based uncertainty scores (lexical similarity, NumSemSets, EigV, semantic entropy) that can serve as B4 variants on SpecDx's run populations.
- **[cohen2023detecting]** Roi Cohen, May Hamri, Mor Geva et al. (2023). *LM vs LM: Detecting Factual Errors via Cross Examination*. EMNLP (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.18653/v1/2023.emnlp-main.778)
  - Relevance **medium**; role: context; components: refutation
  - Detects factual errors by cross-examination: an examiner LM questions the LM that made a claim over multiple turns to surface inconsistencies, outperforming existing factuality methods on four benchmarks.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Inconsistency elicited by a second model as evidence of error resembles SpecDx's directed LLM probes for refutation; SpecDx targets specification claims and requires witness states rather than conversational inconsistency.
- **[wang2023self]** Xuezhi Wang, Jason Wei, Dale Schuurmans et al. (2023). *Self-Consistency Improves Chain of Thought Reasoning in Language Models*. ICLR (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://iclr.cc/virtual/2023/poster/11718)
  - Relevance **medium**; role: foundation; components: baseline B1
  - Replaces greedy chain-of-thought decoding with self-consistency: sample many reasoning paths and return the most frequent final answer, marginalising over paths; this gives large accuracy gains on arithmetic and commonsense benchmarks (e.g. GSM8K +17.9%).
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Self-consistency (majority vote over samples) is the aggregation inside SpecDx's B1 static-review baseline and the majority-vote baseline of its simulation study; it assumes one correct answer per input, whereas SpecDx models set-valued truth and treats systematic agreement against the anchor as evidence of a specification defect rather than of the answer.
- **[shen2022hdiff]** Kaiwen Shen, Jianyu Lu, Yaru Yang et al. (2022). *HDiff: A Semi-automatic Framework for Discovering Semantic Gap Attack in HTTP Implementations*. DSN (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A). [link](https://doi.org/10.1109/dsn53405.2022.00014)
  - Relevance **medium**; role: component prior art; components: active synthesis
  - HDiff extracts rules from HTTP specifications with NLP and uses differential testing across HTTP implementations to discover semantic gap attacks, i.e. inconsistent interpretations of the same request along a processing chain.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Combines specification text mining with differential execution to find divergent interpretations of one specification; SpecDx applies the idea to LLM interpreters and attributes divergence to specification ambiguity versus executor error.
- **[shi2022natural]** Freda Shi, Daniel Fried, Marjan Ghazvininejad et al. (2022). *Natural Language to Code Translation with Execution*. EMNLP (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.18653/v1/2022.emnlp-main.231)
  - Relevance **medium**; role: component prior art; components: baseline B4, set-valued truth
  - Introduces MBR-EXEC, which samples candidate programs, executes them on test inputs, and selects the program whose execution results agree most with the others, marginalising over semantically equivalent implementations.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Execution-result clustering of sampled programs is the mechanism later reused by ClarifyGPT for ambiguity detection and is analogous to SpecDx clustering runs by cell values; MBR-EXEC picks the consensus, whereas SpecDx asks whether the consensus contradicts the specification.
- **[min2020ambigqa]** Sewon Min, Julian Michael, Hannaneh Hajishirzi et al. (2020). *AmbigQA: Answering Ambiguous Open-domain Questions*. EMNLP (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.18653/v1/2020.emnlp-main.466) · [code](https://github.com/shmsw25/AmbigQA) ★123
  - Relevance **medium**; role: foundation; components: set-valued truth, defect taxonomy
  - Introduces AmbigQA, an open-domain QA task in which a system must find every plausible answer to a question and rewrite the question once per answer, together with the AmbigNQ dataset (14,042 NQ-open questions); over half of NQ-open questions turn out to be ambiguous.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Establishes that a natural-language input can admit a set of valid answers, which matches SpecDx's set-valued entailment set A_c = {x,y} for an ambiguous cell; AmbigQA relies on human annotation of the answer set, whereas SpecDx infers it automatically from a population of executors.
- **[chen2018rfc]** Chu Chen, Cong Tian, Zhenhua Duan et al. (2018). *RFC-directed differential testing of certificate validation in SSL/TLS implementations*. ICSE (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.1145/3180155.3180226)
  - Relevance **medium**; role: component prior art; components: active synthesis, refutation
  - Generates test certificates directed by rules extracted from RFC 5280 (expressed as a symbolic program and explored by dynamic symbolic execution) and uses them for differential testing of certificate validation across SSL/TLS implementations, targeting conformance to the RFC rather than random mutation.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Derives test inputs from the clauses of a natural-language standard and solves for inputs that exercise each rule, similar to SpecDx taking boundary values from the clauses and using symbolic search for witness states; the RFC is assumed correct, whereas SpecDx questions the specification.
- **[petsios2017nezha]** Theofilos Petsios, Adrian Tang, Salvatore Stolfo et al. (2017). *NEZHA: Efficient Domain-Independent Differential Testing*. S&P (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.1109/sp.2017.27) · [code](https://github.com/nezha-dt/nezha)
  - Relevance **medium**; role: component prior art; components: active synthesis
  - Presents NEZHA, a domain-independent differential testing framework that guides input generation (gray-box and black-box) by delta-diversity, a metric of behavioural asymmetries among the programs under test; it finds differences 52 and 27 times faster than Frankencerts and Mucerts on certificate validation.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Delta-diversity, i.e. steering inputs toward those that split implementations, is a heuristic form of SpecDx's information-gain scenario selection; SpecDx selects scenarios to discriminate between specification-defect and agent-error hypotheses under a probabilistic model rather than to maximise raw discrepancy.
- **[brubaker2014using]** Chad Brubaker, Suman Jana, Baishakhi Ray et al. (2014). *Using Frankencerts for Automated Adversarial Testing of Certificate Validation in SSL/TLS Implementations*. S&P (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.1109/sp.2014.15)
  - Relevance **medium**; role: foundation; components: active synthesis, family effects/correlated errors
  - Introduces frankencerts, synthetic X.509 certificates assembled from mutated parts of real certificates, and uses differential testing across SSL/TLS libraries (a discrepancy between accepting and rejecting implementations is the oracle); it uncovered 208 discrepancies, many of them serious vulnerabilities.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Uses disagreement among independent implementations of one specification as an oracle and synthesises boundary inputs to provoke it, as SpecDx's active scenario synthesis does; frankencerts attributes discrepancies to implementation flaws, while SpecDx explicitly models the alternative that the specification itself is defective.
- **[yang2011finding]** Xuejun Yang, Yang Chen, Eric Eide et al. (2011). *Finding and understanding bugs in C compilers*. PLDI (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.1145/1993498.1993532) · [code](https://github.com/csmith-project/csmith) ★1255
  - Relevance **medium**; role: foundation; components: refutation
  - Presents Csmith, a randomised generator of valid C programs free of undefined behaviour, used for three years of differential testing of C compilers; it found more than 325 previously unknown bugs, and every compiler tested both crashed and silently miscompiled valid input.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* The canonical case of using several implementations as cross-referencing oracles with majority voting on outputs; SpecDx inherits the voting idea but applies it to LLM interpreters of a natural-language specification and adds a latent model of interpreter reliability, since its 'implementations' are far less reliable than compilers.
- **[littlewood2001modeling]** Bev Littlewood, Peter Popov, Lorenzo Strigini (2001). *Modeling software design diversity: a review*. CSUR (peer-reviewed, CORE2020 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.1145/384192.384195)
  - Relevance **medium**; role: survey; components: family effects/correlated errors
  - Tutorial survey of probabilistic models of software design diversity, explaining why the independence of version failures is untenable and how dependence must be assessed to evaluate the reliability of diverse fault-tolerant systems.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* A reference survey for the correlated-error assumption underlying SpecDx's identifiability theory and for how dependence between versions (here, model families) can be modelled.
- **[mckeeman1998differential]** William M. McKeeman (1998). *Differential Testing for Software*. DTJ (other). [link](http://citeseerx.ist.psu.edu/viewdoc/summary?doi=10.1.1.83.445)
  - Relevance **medium**; role: foundation; components: refutation
  - Introduces differential testing as a technique: run the same test input through several comparable implementations (C compilers at DEC) and treat any disagreement as a candidate bug to be investigated.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* The origin of the cross-implementation oracle that SpecDx generalises to LLM interpreters of a business specification; McKeeman assumes a correct specification and blames implementations.
- **[brilliant1990analysis]** S.S. Brilliant, J.C. Knight, N.G. Leveson (1990). *Analysis of faults in an N-version software experiment*. TSE (peer-reviewed, CORE2020 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.1109/32.44387)
  - Relevance **medium**; role: foundation; components: family effects/correlated errors, defect taxonomy
  - Analyses the faults found in the 27 versions of the Knight-Leveson N-version experiment, examining which faults caused coincident failures across versions.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Fault-level evidence about why independently developed versions of one specification fail together; relevant to whether SpecDx's consensus deviations come from the specification or from shared difficulty.
- **[avizienis1985version]** A. Avizienis (1985). *The N-Version Approach to Fault-Tolerant Software*. TSE (peer-reviewed, CORE2020 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.1109/tse.1985.231893)
  - Relevance **medium**; role: foundation; components: family effects/correlated errors
  - Defines N-version programming as the independent generation of N>=2 functionally equivalent programs from the same initial specification, executed in parallel with a decision algorithm that takes a consensus or majority output.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* SpecDx's population of LLM interpreters is an N-version system in which every 'version' is generated from the same specification T; SpecDx inverts the use of the vote, treating a consensus that contradicts the specification's checks as evidence of a specification defect.
- **[ji2026coding]** Zimo Ji, Zekai Zhang, Congying Xu et al. (2026). *Coding Agents Are Guessing: Measuring Action-Boundary Violations in Underspecified DevOps Instructions*. arXiv (preprint). [link](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.02294)
  - Relevance **low**; role: evaluation/benchmark; components: defect taxonomy
  - UnderSpecBench measures action-boundary violations by coding agents (Claude Code, Codex, OpenCode) on DevOps tasks with benign but underspecified instructions, across 69 task families grounded in documented incidents.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Shows that agents guess under underspecification in operational settings; SpecDx turns such guessing, observed across a population, into evidence about where the specification is underspecified.
- **[paracha2026hallucinating]** Muhammad Talha Paracha, Kyle Posluns, Kevin Borgolte et al. (2026). *Hallucinating Certificates: Differential Testing of TLS Certificate Validation Using Generative Language Models*. ICSE (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.1145/3744916.3773203)
  - Relevance **low**; role: context; components: active synthesis
  - Introduces MLCerts, which uses generative language models to synthesise X.509 certificates for differential testing of TLS certificate validation across libraries, exploiting models' tendency to produce syntactically valid but semantically unusual inputs.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* A recent example of LLMs used as input generators for differential testing; SpecDx instead uses LLMs as the implementations (interpreters) being compared, with the specification as an anchored, possibly defective, rater.
- **[matsnev2026uncertainty]** Gregory Matsnev (2026). *Uncertainty Decomposition for Clarification Seeking in LLM Agents*. arXiv (preprint). [link](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.19559)
  - Relevance **low**; role: context
  - Proposes a prompt-based method that decomposes an LLM agent's uncertainty (including underspecification) to decide when to seek clarification, under black-box and latency constraints that rule out multi-sampling.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Agent-side, single-run self-assessment of underspecification; SpecDx relies on multi-run, multi-family behaviour rather than self-reported uncertainty.
- **[etemadi2025mokav]** Khashayar Etemadi, Bardia Mohammadi, Zhendong Su et al. (2025). *Mokav: Execution-driven differential testing with LLMs*. JSS (peer-reviewed, CORE2020 A). [link](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.jss.2025.112571)
  - Relevance **low**; role: context; components: refutation
  - Mokav iteratively prompts an LLM with execution feedback to generate a difference-exposing test, an input on which two program versions produce provably different outputs.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* An LLM-driven search for discriminating inputs, comparable to SpecDx's directed LLM probe for witness states; SpecDx's witnesses refute specification-defect candidates rather than distinguish two programs.
- **[kim2024aligning]** Hyuhng Joon Kim, Youna Kim, Cheonbok Park et al. (2024). *Aligning Language Models to Explicitly Handle Ambiguity*. EMNLP (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.18653/v1/2024.emnlp-main.119)
  - Relevance **low**; role: context
  - Proposes Alignment with Perceived Ambiguity (APA), which trains LLMs to recognise and handle ambiguous queries using the model's own perceived ambiguity (measured through its knowledge) as the training signal.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Treats ambiguity as model-relative, in line with SpecDx's view that ambiguity is defined by the readings of competent interpreters; it changes model behaviour rather than diagnosing the input text.
- **[qian2024tell]** Cheng Qian, Bingxiang He, Zhong Zhuang et al. (2024). *Tell Me More! Towards Implicit User Intention Understanding of Language Model Driven Agents*. ACL (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.18653/v1/2024.acl-long.61)
  - Relevance **low**; role: context
  - Introduces the Intention-in-Interaction (IN3) benchmark for implicit user intentions in agent tasks and trains Mistral-Interact to assess task vagueness, ask about missing details and refine them into actionable goals before execution.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Agent-side handling of vague task specifications; SpecDx instead diagnoses the specification offline from many agents' behaviour.

<a id="t7_active_testing_design"></a>
## Active testing, experimental design, bandits and scenario generation

- **[keren2026matter]** Tomer Keren, Nitay Calderon, Asaf Yehudai et al. (2026). *A Matter of TASTE: Improving Coverage and Difficulty of Agent Benchmarks*. arXiv (preprint). [link](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.28556)
