  - Relevance **high**; role: component prior art; components: active synthesis, real-defect benchmark
  - TASTE reverses benchmark task construction: an adaptive contrastive n-gram model trained on LLM-judged validity samples valid tool sequences, representative sequences are chosen by clustering, instantiated into tasks and made harder by iterative difficulty evolution, giving tau^c-Bench, an extension of tau2-Bench's three domains (built on the tau-Bench-Verified task sets) on which near-saturated agents drop sharply and tool-combination coverage more than doubles.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Automatic scenario synthesis in exactly SpecDx's benchmark family (tau2-Bench airline/retail/telecom). TASTE aims at coverage and difficulty for agent evaluation and relies on LLM validity judges; SpecDx aims at information about specification defects and could reuse TASTE's coverage-oriented generator as a scenario pool.
- **[hofmann2025fluid]** Valentin Hofmann, David Heineman, Ian Magnusson et al. (2025). *Fluid Language Model Benchmarking*. COLM (peer-reviewed). [link](https://openreview.net/forum?id=mxcCg9YRqj) · [code](https://github.com/allenai/fluid-benchmarking) · **baseline candidate**
  - Relevance **high**; role: component prior art; components: active synthesis, item difficulty/IRT
  - Fluid Benchmarking fits an item response model on existing LM evaluation results and selects items dynamically per model, as in computerised adaptive testing; it beats random sampling and other IRT baselines in efficiency, validity, variance and saturation (e.g., higher validity and lower variance on MMLU with fifty times fewer items).
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* The most recent LLM-side CAT method and a natural adaptive-selection baseline: it chooses items to estimate model ability, whereas SpecDx chooses scenarios to estimate item (specification-cell) defect status. Its released IRT tooling could be repurposed with roles swapped.
- **[zhuang2025position]** Yan Zhuang, Qi Liu, Zachary Pardos et al. (2025). *Position: AI Evaluation Should Learn from How We Test Humans*. ICML (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://proceedings.mlr.press/v267/zhuang25e.html)
  - Relevance **high**; role: context; components: active synthesis, item difficulty/IRT
  - Position paper (arXiv 2306.10512, earlier titled 'Efficiently Measuring the Cognitive Ability of LLMs: An Adaptive Testing Perspective') arguing that AI evaluation should move from static benchmarks to psychometric adaptive testing: estimate each item's characteristics and tailor the item sequence to each model, which also exposes low-quality or erroneous items.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Shares the CAT view (item parameters plus adaptive selection) and the concern with erroneous benchmark items. SpecDx inverts the target: it measures the items (specification cells) using a population of models, and synthesises new scenarios rather than selecting from a fixed bank.
- **[truong2025reliable]** Sang T. Truong, Yuheng Tu, Percy Liang et al. (2025). *Reliable and Efficient Amortized Model-based Evaluation*. ICML (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://proceedings.mlr.press/v267/truong25c.html)
  - Relevance **high**; role: component prior art; components: item difficulty/IRT, active synthesis
  - Uses IRT to separate LM ability from question difficulty, trains a model that predicts difficulty from question content (amortised calibration), and trains a difficulty-conditioned question generator so that adaptive testing can generate informative questions on demand; across 22 benchmarks and 172 LMs this is more reliable and efficient than random subsets.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Closest LLM-evaluation analogue of SpecDx's active scenario synthesis: adaptive selection plus generation of new test items guided by an item model. SpecDx generates business scenarios from clause literals and boundary values to diagnose the specification, not to rank models.
- **[jung2025trust]** Jaehun Jung, Faeze Brahman, Yejin Choi (2025). *Trust or Escalate: LLM Judges with Provable Guarantees for Human Agreement*. ICLR (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://proceedings.iclr.cc/paper_files/paper/2025/hash/08dabd5345b37fffcbe335bd578b15a0-Abstract-Conference.html)
  - Relevance **high**; role: component prior art; components: active synthesis
  - Selective evaluation for LLM judges: estimate judge confidence (Simulated Annotators) and trust a judgment only when confident, giving a provable guarantee of human agreement; Cascaded Selective Evaluation starts with cheap judges and escalates to stronger models only when necessary while keeping the guarantee.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Direct precedent for SpecDx's sequential escalation (cheap pilot, then the full committee) with a calibrated stopping rule. SpecDx escalates from one model per family to the whole population and its guarantee target is defect diagnosis, without human labels.
- **[rainforth2024modern]** Tom Rainforth, Adam Foster, Desi R. Ivanova et al. (2024). *Modern Bayesian Experimental Design*. Stat. Sci. (peer-reviewed). [link](https://doi.org/10.1214/23-sts915)
  - Relevance **high**; role: survey; components: active synthesis
  - Review of modern Bayesian experimental design: how recent advances in EIG estimation, optimisation and amortised/adaptive policies overcome its computational challenges, and open directions.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Up-to-date reference for EIG-based sequential design; SpecDx's scenario synthesis is a myopic adaptive BED over a discrete design space (scenario cells, boundary literals). Use to position the realised-information-gain reward against proper EIG estimators.
- **[kossen2022active]** Jannik Kossen, Sebastian Farquhar, Yarin Gal et al. (2022). *Active Surrogate Estimators: An Active Learning Approach to Label-Efficient Model Evaluation*. NeurIPS (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.52202/068431-1783) · [code](https://github.com/jlko/active-surrogate-estimators) ★13 · **baseline candidate**
  - Relevance **high**; role: component prior art; components: active synthesis
  - Proposes Active Surrogate Estimators, which estimate model performance by interpolating the errors of unlabeled points with an actively learned surrogate instead of a Monte Carlo estimator, with an acquisition strategy (XWED) tailored to the final estimation task; more label-efficient than active testing on deep-network evaluation.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* SpecDx's cell model already acts as a surrogate that predicts the reading pattern of unrun scenario cells; ASE's XWED acquisition is a principled alternative to Thompson-EIG when the target is an aggregate (e.g., defect count). SpecDx's target is per-cell typed diagnosis.
- **[kossen2021active]** Jannik Kossen, Sebastian Farquhar, Yarin Gal et al. (2021). *Active Testing: Sample-Efficient Model Evaluation*. ICML (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://proceedings.mlr.press/v139/kossen21a.html) · [code](https://github.com/jlko/active-testing) ★29 · **baseline candidate**
  - Relevance **high**; role: component prior art; components: active synthesis
  - Defines active testing: choosing which test points to label so that model evaluation is sample-efficient, with acquisition strategies tailored to risk estimation (not training) and an unbiased importance-weighted estimator (LURE-style) that also reduces variance.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Closest ML analogue of SpecDx's scenario selection: both spend a label/run budget to estimate a quantity rather than to train. SpecDx's 'label' is an execution by a committee of LLM interpreters and the target is a defect diagnosis per cell, not a risk estimate; active testing's bias-correction matters if SpecDx reports defect prevalence.
- **[riccio2020model]** Vincenzo Riccio, Paolo Tonella (2020). *Model-based exploration of the frontier of behaviours for deep learning system testing*. ESEC/FSE (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.1145/3368089.3409730) · [code](https://github.com/testingautomated-usi/deepjanus) ★14 · **baseline candidate**
  - Relevance **high**; role: component prior art; components: active synthesis
  - Introduces the frontier of behaviours, the inputs at which a DL system starts to misbehave, and DeepJanus, a model-based search tool that generates pairs of similar inputs straddling that frontier; if the frontier lies inside the validity domain, the inputs reveal quality deficiencies (shown on lane keeping and MNIST).
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* The SE counterpart of SpecDx's boundary values: both target the transition point where behaviour flips. DeepJanus searches a continuous model-based input space for one system; SpecDx takes boundary literals directly from executable clauses and asks whether a population of interpreters flips at the same place the specification does.
- **[russo2020simple]** Daniel Russo (2020). *Simple Bayesian Algorithms for Best-Arm Identification*. Oper. Res. (peer-reviewed, CORE2020 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.1287/opre.2019.1911)
  - Relevance **high**; role: foundation; components: active synthesis
  - Studies adaptive allocation of measurement effort to identify the best of a finite set of designs from noisy signals (pure exploration), proposes several simple Bayesian allocation algorithms (top-two sampling rules built on Thompson sampling), and characterises asymptotic limits showing these algorithms are in a formal sense the best possible.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* SpecDx's objective is identification (which cells are defective, and of what type), not regret, so plain Thompson sampling over-exploits; top-two Thompson sampling is the principled pure-exploration fix and a cheap ablation of the selector.
- **[foster2019variational]** Adam Foster, Martin Jankowiak, Elias Bingham et al. (2019). *Variational Bayesian Optimal Experimental Design*. NeurIPS (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://proceedings.neurips.cc/paper/2019/hash/d55cbf210f175f4a37916eafe6c04f0d-Abstract.html) · [code](https://github.com/pyro-ppl/pyro) ★9068 · **baseline candidate**
  - Relevance **high**; role: component prior art; components: active synthesis
  - Introduces several classes of fast estimators of the expected information gain (EIG) of an experiment, built on amortised variational inference, and shows theoretically and empirically that they are faster and more accurate than previous approaches, including in end-to-end adaptive experiments.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Provides principled EIG estimators that could replace SpecDx's realised-information-gain reward; SpecDx's design space is discrete and small, so exact or nested-MC EIG under the EM posterior is feasible, which makes VBOED mainly a positioning reference.
- **[russo2018tutorial]** Daniel J. Russo, Benjamin Van Roy, Abbas Kazerouni et al. (2018). *A Tutorial on Thompson Sampling*. FnTML (peer-reviewed). [link](https://doi.org/10.1561/2200000070)
  - Relevance **high**; role: survey; components: active synthesis
  - Tutorial on Thompson sampling for online decision problems that balance exploitation and information gathering, covering Bernoulli bandits, shortest paths, recommendation, active learning with neural networks and RL, and discussing when Thompson sampling works and when alternatives are needed.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Standard reference for posterior sampling over correlated arms; SpecDx's cells are arms whose outcomes inform beliefs about other cells (shared clause/defect hypotheses), a case the tutorial discusses. SpecDx replaces the regret objective with information gain on defect diagnosis.
- **[mullins2018adaptive]** Galen E. Mullins, Paul G. Stankiewicz, R. Chad Hawthorne et al. (2018). *Adaptive generation of challenging scenarios for testing and evaluation of autonomous vehicles*. JSS (peer-reviewed, CORE2020 A). [link](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.jss.2017.10.031)
  - Relevance **high**; role: component prior art; components: active synthesis
  - Generates test scenarios for black-box autonomous systems by adaptive, simulation-based sampling that concentrates on the boundaries between performance modes, then clusters scenarios by mode and ranks boundary scenarios by how well they diagnose behaviour changes (summary from the publisher/APL description of the abstract).
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Closest SE analogue of SpecDx's boundary-oriented active scenario synthesis: adaptive sampling at mode boundaries with few expensive runs. SpecDx's boundaries come symbolically from clause literals and its 'modes' are reading patterns of an interpreter population.
- **[russo2018learning]** Daniel Russo, Benjamin Van Roy (2018). *Learning to Optimize via Information-Directed Sampling*. Oper. Res. (peer-reviewed, CORE2020 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.1287/opre.2017.1663)
  - Relevance **high**; role: foundation; components: active synthesis
  - Introduces information-directed sampling (IDS), which samples actions to minimise the ratio of squared expected one-step regret to the mutual information between the optimal action and the next observation, with an entropy-scaled regret bound and gains over Thompson sampling and UCB in structured problems. Conference version at NeurIPS 2014.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* IDS is the principled alternative to SpecDx's 'Thompson sampling rewarded by realised information gain'; SpecDx has no regret term (pure exploration for diagnosis), so its objective sits closer to the information-gain denominator of IDS. Worth citing to justify or contrast the heuristic.
- **[naghshvar2013active]** Mohammad Naghshvar, Tara Javidi (2013). *Active sequential hypothesis testing*. Ann. Stat. (peer-reviewed). [link](https://doi.org/10.1214/13-aos1144)
  - Relevance **high**; role: foundation; components: active synthesis
  - Studies a decision maker who adaptively selects the most informative sensing action to identify a hypothesis quickly, penalising wrong declarations; derives dynamic-programming lower bounds on optimal cost and upper bounds for two heuristic action-selection policies that are asymptotically optimal.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Theory for exactly SpecDx's problem shape: choose actions (scenarios) to identify which hypothesis (contradiction, hidden requirement, ambiguity, weak check, agent error) holds. Could supply optimality language for Thompson-EIG; SpecDx's observation model is the learned anchored cell model.
- **[chen2013optimistic]** Xi Chen, Qihang Lin, Dengyong Zhou (2013). *Optimistic Knowledge Gradient Policy for Optimal Budget Allocation in Crowdsourcing*. ICML (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://proceedings.mlr.press/v28/chen13f.html) · **baseline candidate**
  - Relevance **high**; role: component prior art; components: active synthesis, item difficulty/IRT
  - Formulates allocating a fixed labeling budget across instance-worker pairs as a Bayesian MDP and proposes the optimistic knowledge gradient policy, a tractable consistent approximation to the DP optimum, extended to heterogeneous workers and contextual tasks with differing ambiguity. A journal version appeared later (Chen, Lin, Zhou, 'Statistical decision making for optimal budget allocation in crowd labeling').
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Nearest prior art for SpecDx's allocation of LLM runs to cells under item difficulty and rater reliability: both allocate noisy ratings where posterior uncertainty is largest. SpecDx's raters are LLM interpreters with family effects, the specification itself is an anchor rater, and the truth is set-valued.
- **[houlsby2011bayesian]** Neil Houlsby, Ferenc Huszár, Zoubin Ghahramani et al. (2011). *Bayesian Active Learning for Classification and Preference Learning*. arXiv (preprint). [link](https://arxiv.org/abs/1112.5745) · **baseline candidate**
  - Relevance **high**; role: component prior art; components: active synthesis
  - Proposes Bayesian Active Learning by Disagreement (BALD): express information gain about model parameters in terms of predictive entropies (entropy of the marginal prediction minus expected conditional entropy), applied to Gaussian process classification and preference learning.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* BALD's 'disagreement' form of mutual information matches SpecDx's committee setting: a scenario is informative when interpreters disagree yet each is confident. SpecDx targets information about specification defects (A_c, anchors), not model parameters. Seminal 2011 preprint, kept despite the recency rule because the caller named it and it is the canonical BALD source.
- **[chaloner1995bayesian]** Kathryn Chaloner, Isabella Verdinelli (1995). *Bayesian Experimental Design: A Review*. Stat. Sci. (peer-reviewed). [link](https://doi.org/10.1214/ss/1177009939)
  - Relevance **high**; role: survey; components: active synthesis
  - Reviews Bayesian experimental design under a single decision-theoretic framework that covers linear and nonlinear design problems, casts the Bayesian design criteria as choices of utility function, and justifies choosing an optimality criterion on decision-theoretic grounds.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Foundation for treating scenario choice as an experiment whose utility is expected information about the latent entailment sets and defect labels. SpecDx applies this sequentially and with a bandit approximation instead of solving the design problem exactly.
- **[seung1992query]** H. S. Seung, M. Opper, H. Sompolinsky (1992). *Query by committee*. COLT (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.1145/130385.130417) · **baseline candidate**
  - Relevance **high**; role: foundation; components: active synthesis, family effects/correlated errors
  - Proposes query by committee: a committee of learners trained on the same data picks the next query by maximal disagreement; on toy models this yields asymptotically finite information gain per query and exponentially decreasing generalisation error, unlike random queries.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* SpecDx's committee of LLM interpreters and disagreement-driven scenario choice descend from QBC. The twist: SpecDx treats consensus deviation from the specification (not disagreement alone) as defect evidence, and disagreement among families as evidence of ambiguity.
- **[white1980domain]** L.J. White, E.I. Cohen (1980). *A Domain Strategy for Computer Program Testing*. TSE (peer-reviewed, CORE2020 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.1109/tse.1980.234486)
  - Relevance **high**; role: foundation; components: active synthesis, mutation evaluation
  - Domain testing: program predicates partition the input space into path domains, and test points placed on and just off each domain boundary detect domain errors, i.e., a shifted boundary or a changed relational operator, with conditions under which the strategy is reliable.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* The theoretical root of SpecDx's boundary values from clause literals: an on/off pair around a clause threshold separates a mis-keyed check (shifted constant, > vs >=) from the NL requirement. SpecDx applies it to specification clauses and judges the boundary by an interpreter population rather than a known-correct program.
- **[thompson1933likelihood]** William R. Thompson (1933). *On the Likelihood that One Unknown Probability Exceeds Another in View of the Evidence of Two Samples*. Biometrika (peer-reviewed). [link](https://doi.org/10.2307/2332286)
  - Relevance **high**; role: foundation; components: active synthesis
  - The original paper behind Thompson sampling: it asks how likely it is that one unknown success probability exceeds another given two samples, and proposes allocating trials according to that posterior probability (as later summarised by Agrawal & Goyal and the Russo et al. tutorial).
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* SpecDx's scenario-cell selector is a Thompson sampler; this is the citation for the rule. SpecDx differs in that the reward is realised information gain about defect hypotheses, not a success probability.
- **[zhuang2026survey]** Yan Zhuang, Qi Liu, Haoyang Bi et al. (2026). *Survey of Computerized Adaptive Testing: A Machine Learning Perspective*. TPAMI (peer-reviewed, CORE2020 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.1109/tpami.2026.3672850)
  - Relevance **medium**; role: survey; components: active synthesis, item difficulty/IRT
  - Machine-learning-focused survey of computerised adaptive testing covering measurement models, question-selection algorithms, item-bank construction and test control, including CAT for evaluating AI models.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Survey to cite for selection rules (Fisher, KL, MI, learned policies) that SpecDx's Thompson-EIG competes with; SpecDx's 'bank' is synthesised from the specification rather than pre-calibrated.
- **[li2024active]** Yang Li, Jie Ma, Miguel Ballesteros et al. (2024). *Active Evaluation Acquisition for Efficient LLM Benchmarking*. arXiv (preprint). [link](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.05952)
  - Relevance **medium**; role: context; components: active synthesis
  - Selects a subset of prompts from each benchmark with a learned acquisition policy that models dependencies between test examples, so the outcomes of unselected examples can be predicted from the selected ones; an RL-based policy reduces the number of evaluation prompts while keeping performance estimates accurate.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Active acquisition of evaluation outcomes with a predictive model of unrun items, parallel to SpecDx's cell model predicting unrun scenario cells; SpecDx's acquisition targets defect diagnosis.
- **[zrnic2024active]** Tijana Zrnic, Emmanuel Candes (2024). *Active Statistical Inference*. ICML (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://proceedings.mlr.press/v235/zrnic24a.html)
  - Relevance **medium**; role: component prior art; components: active synthesis
  - Active inference uses a black-box ML model to decide which points to label under a budget (label where the model is uncertain, rely on predictions where confident) and still produces valid confidence intervals and tests, with fewer labels than non-adaptive baselines.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Template for making SpecDx's adaptively collected evidence support valid statistical statements (e.g., defect-rate intervals) while spending runs where the cell model is uncertain.
- **[dobslaw2023automated]** Felix Dobslaw, Robert Feldt, Francisco Gomes de Oliveira Neto (2023). *Automated black-box boundary value detection*. PeerJ CS (peer-reviewed). [link](https://doi.org/10.7717/peerj-cs.1625)
  - Relevance **medium**; role: component prior art; components: active synthesis
  - Proposes an automated black-box boundary value detection method that searches for pairs of nearby inputs with highly different outputs (using a program-derivative measure), supporting systematic boundary value analysis even without a specification or model.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Black-box alternative for SpecDx's boundary values: it would find where executor behaviour changes without reading clause literals. SpecDx compares the executors' boundary with the boundary the clause declares, which this method cannot do alone.
- **[ribeiro2022adaptive]** Marco Tulio Ribeiro, Scott Lundberg (2022). *Adaptive Testing and Debugging of NLP Models*. ACL (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.18653/v1/2022.acl-long.230)
  - Relevance **medium**; role: component prior art; components: active synthesis
  - AdaTest pairs a large language model with human feedback to write unit tests that reveal bugs in a target NLP model, adapting test generation to what has been found and iterating a test-fix-retest loop; users are 5-10x more effective at finding bugs.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Adaptive LLM-driven test generation; SpecDx is fully automatic (no human in the loop) and its tests are scenarios that probe the specification through a population of executors.
- **[zohdinasab2021deephyperion]** Tahereh Zohdinasab, Vincenzo Riccio, Alessio Gambi et al. (2021). *DeepHyperion: exploring the feature space of deep learning-based systems through illumination search*. ISSTA (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A). [link](https://doi.org/10.1145/3460319.3464811)
  - Relevance **medium**; role: component prior art; components: active synthesis

  - *Relation to SpecDx:* SpecDx's 'scenario cells' are a grid over specification features much like a DeepHyperion feature map; SpecDx fills it by Thompson sampling with an information-gain reward instead of a quality-diversity archive with a failure-closeness fitness.
- **[guerriero2021operation]** Antonio Guerriero, Roberto Pietrantuono, Stefano Russo (2021). *Operation is the Hardest Teacher: Estimating DNN Accuracy Looking for Mispredictions*. ICSE (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.1109/icse43902.2021.00042)

  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Shares SpecDx's dual aim (estimate and find defects) under a labeling budget; SpecDx's target failures are specification defects and its 'labels' are committee executions.
- **[howard2021time]** Steven R. Howard, Aaditya Ramdas, Jon McAuliffe et al. (2021). *Time-uniform, nonparametric, nonasymptotic confidence sequences*. Ann. Stat. (peer-reviewed). [link](https://doi.org/10.1214/20-aos1991)

  - *Relation to SpecDx:* SpecDx's Hoeffding finite-sample bounds are fixed-sample; because scenarios are chosen adaptively and stopping is data-dependent, time-uniform confidence sequences are the correct tool for valid stopping guarantees.
- **[bohme2020boosting]** Marcel Böhme, Valentin J. M. Manès, Sang Kil Cha (2020). *Boosting fuzzer efficiency: an information theoretic perspective*. ESEC/FSE (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.1145/3368089.3409748)

  - Treats fuzzing as a learning process in which each execution reveals some information about the program's behaviours; within a probabilistic model of fuzzing, Shannon entropy over observed behaviours measures how much each input teaches and the rate at which the fuzzer discovers new behaviours, so efficient fuzzers maximise information (the basis of the authors' entropy-based seed schedule, Entropic).
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* SE precedent for rewarding test selection by realised information gain, as SpecDx's Thompson sampler does over scenario cells; Entropic's information is about program behaviours, SpecDx's is about specification-defect hypotheses.
- **[dobslaw2020boundary]** Felix Dobslaw, Francisco Gomes de Oliveira Neto, Robert Feldt (2020). *Boundary Value Exploration for Software Analysis*. ICSTW (peer-reviewed). [link](https://doi.org/10.1109/icstw50294.2020.00062)
  - Relevance **medium**; role: component prior art; components: active synthesis
  - Clarifies boundary value analysis and testing, which are poorly defined and hard to automate because subdomains must be identified from a specification, and introduces Boundary Value Exploration: techniques that search for input pairs whose outputs differ sharply, as a basis for automation.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* SpecDx sidesteps the subdomain-identification problem BVE raises because executable clauses expose their literals; BVE-style search is the black-box fallback when T states a threshold that C does not.
- **[gambi2019automatically]** Alessio Gambi, Marc Mueller, Gordon Fraser (2019). *Automatically testing self-driving cars with search-based procedural content generation*. ISSTA (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A). [link](https://doi.org/10.1145/3293882.3330566)

  - AsFault combines procedural content generation with search-based testing to create virtual road networks that make lane-keeping software of self-driving cars fail in simulation.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Automatic generation of structured scenarios for a black-box system under test; SpecDx's scenarios are business states and requests constructed from clause literals, evaluated by an LLM population.
- **[kirsch2019batchbald]** Andreas Kirsch, Joost van Amersfoort, Yarin Gal (2019). *BatchBALD: Efficient and Diverse Batch Acquisition for Deep Bayesian Active Learning*. NeurIPS (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://proceedings.neurips.cc/paper/2019/hash/95323660ed2124450caaac2c46b5ed90-Abstract.html) · [code](https://github.com/BlackHC/BatchBALD) ★249
  - Relevance **medium**; role: component prior art; components: active synthesis

  - *Relation to SpecDx:* SpecDx runs scenarios in batches on a shared GPU host; top-k Thompson draws can pick redundant cells that share one clause. BatchBALD gives a principled joint batch criterion.
- **[li2019boosting]** Zenan Li, Xiaoxing Ma, Chang Xu et al. (2019). *Boosting operational DNN testing efficiency through conditioning*. ESEC/FSE (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.1145/3338906.3338930)
  - Relevance **medium**; role: component prior art; components: active synthesis
  - Views operational testing as reliability estimation by statistical sampling, reinterprets structural coverage as conditioning for variance reduction, and conditions on the distribution of last-hidden-layer neuron outputs (sampled via cross-entropy minimisation), needing about half the labels of simple random sampling for the same precision.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* SE counterpart of active testing: choosing which field inputs to label to estimate accuracy. SpecDx selects scenarios to diagnose the specification, but variance-reduction sampling matters when it reports defect prevalence.
- **[abdessalem2018testing]** Raja Ben Abdessalem, Annibale Panichella, Shiva Nejati et al. (2018). *Testing autonomous cars for feature interaction failures using many-objective search*. ASE (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.1145/3238147.3238192)
  - Relevance **medium**; role: component prior art; components: active synthesis
  - Casts detection of feature-interaction failures in autonomous cars as search-based test generation, defining hybrid test objectives that combine coverage heuristics with interaction-failure distances and a many-objective algorithm (FITEST) that generates simulation scenarios violating system requirements.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Requirement-violation-guided scenario search; SpecDx also searches a scenario space for requirement-relevant behaviour, but its fitness is information about specification defects across a population of executors rather than failure distance of one system.
- **[abdessalem2018testinga]** Raja Ben Abdessalem, Shiva Nejati, Lionel C. Briand et al. (2018). *Testing vision-based control systems using learnable evolutionary algorithms*. ICSE (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.1145/3180155.3180160)
  - Relevance **medium**; role: component prior art; components: active synthesis
  - Tests vision-based control systems with learnable evolutionary algorithms: multi-objective search combined with decision-tree classifiers that learn which regions of the scenario space are critical, steering generation towards failure-inducing scenarios and characterising those regions.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Model-guided scenario generation that learns where critical regions are, akin to SpecDx using its cell model to steer Thompson sampling; SpecDx's regions of interest are clause boundaries and disagreement, not failures of one system.
- **[pei2017deepxplore]** Kexin Pei, Yinzhi Cao, Junfeng Yang et al. (2017). *DeepXplore: Automated Whitebox Testing of Deep Learning Systems*. SOSP (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.1145/3132747.3132785)
  - Relevance **medium**; role: component prior art; components: active synthesis, family effects/correlated errors
  - DeepXplore is a whitebox framework that introduces neuron coverage and uses several DL systems with similar functionality as cross-referencing oracles, generating inputs by gradient-based joint optimisation of differential behaviour and coverage; it finds thousands of erroneous corner cases.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Uses a population of similar models as each other's oracle and generates inputs that maximise their disagreement, close to SpecDx's committee idea. - **[deshmukh2017testing]** Jyotirmoy Deshmukh, Marko Horvat, Xiaoqing Jin et al. (2017). *Testing Cyber-Physical Systems through Bayesian Optimization*. TECS (peer-reviewed, CORE2020 B). [link](https://doi.org/10.1145/3126521)
  - Relevance **medium**; role: component prior art; components: active synthesis
  - Treats CPS testing as black-box optimisation of a cost over output traces (e.g., robustness of a temporal-logic requirement) and provides a tool that applies Bayesian optimisation, with automated dimensionality reduction, to find falsifying inputs with few simulations.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Surrogate-guided, sample-efficient search for requirement violations; SpecDx uses a Bayesian cell model and Thompson sampling instead of a GP over continuous inputs, and its objective is information about the specification, not falsification of the system.
- **[settles2012active]** Burr Settles (2012). *Active Learning*. Synth. Lect. AI ML (book). [link](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-031-01560-1)
  - Relevance **medium**; role: survey; components: active synthesis
  - Book-length treatment of active learning, in which the learner chooses which unlabeled instances an oracle should label; it is the later, extended form of the author's 2009 technical-report survey (the abstract was not available to us; summary from the record metadata).
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Standard survey to cite for query-selection vocabulary (uncertainty sampling, query-by-committee). SpecDx queries a committee of LLM executors on synthesised scenarios, and no single oracle is assumed correct.
- **[agrawal2012analysis]** Shipra Agrawal, Navin Goyal (2012). *Analysis of Thompson Sampling for the Multi-armed Bandit Problem*. COLT (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://proceedings.mlr.press/v23/agrawal12.html)
  - Relevance **medium**; role: foundation; components: active synthesis
  - First proof that Thompson sampling achieves logarithmic expected regret for the stochastic multi-armed bandit problem, giving regret bounds for two-armed and N-armed bandits.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Theoretical backing for using Thompson sampling as SpecDx's scenario-cell selector; SpecDx's reward (realised information gain) is non-stationary and depends on the posterior, so these guarantees do not transfer directly.
- **[nie2011survey]** Changhai Nie, Hareton Leung (2011). *A survey of combinatorial testing*. CSUR (peer-reviewed, CORE2020 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.1145/1883612.1883618)
  - Relevance **medium**; role: survey; components: active synthesis
  - Surveys two decades of combinatorial testing, which detects failures triggered by parameter interactions using covering-array test suites, classifying work into modelling, generation, constraints, failure diagnosis, prioritisation, metrics and applications.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Covering arrays over scenario parameters are a non-adaptive alternative to SpecDx's Thompson-sampled cells; CT's failure-diagnosis line (locating failure-inducing combinations) is also related to localising the clause behind a deviation.
- **[chapelle2011empirical]** Olivier Chapelle, Lihong Li (2011). *An Empirical Evaluation of Thompson Sampling*. NeurIPS (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://proceedings.neurips.cc/paper/2011/hash/e53a0a2978c28872a4505bdb51db06dc-Abstract.html)
  - Relevance **medium**; role: foundation; components: active synthesis
  - Shows on simulated and real (display advertising, news recommendation) data that Thompson sampling is highly competitive with standard bandit heuristics and argues it should be a standard baseline because it is easy to implement.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Empirical justification for choosing Thompson sampling as SpecDx's default selector; SpecDx's ablation A4 (random selection) and an IDS/UCB variant would mirror this kind of comparison.
- **[chang1996global]** Hua-Hua Chang, Zhiliang Ying (1996). *A Global Information Approach to Computerized Adaptive Testing*. APM (peer-reviewed). [link](https://doi.org/10.1177/014662169602000303)
  - Relevance **medium**; role: foundation; components: active synthesis, item difficulty/IRT
  - Argues that maximum-Fisher-information item selection is inefficient early in a CAT, when the ability estimate is poor, and proposes item selection based on average global (Kullback-Leibler) information, supported by simulations.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Directly informs SpecDx's early rounds: with few runs the cell-model posterior is wide, so a global/expected-information criterion (or Thompson's posterior sampling) is preferable to plug-in local information.
- **[chernoff1959sequential]** Herman Chernoff (1959). *Sequential Design of Experiments*. Ann. Math. Stat. (peer-reviewed). [link](https://doi.org/10.1214/aoms/1177706205)
  - Relevance **medium**; role: foundation; components: active synthesis
  - Foundational paper on the sequential design of experiments, in which the experimenter adaptively chooses which experiment to perform next as well as when to stop and which hypothesis to accept.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* SpecDx's loop (choose scenario, observe committee, update, stop) is a Chernoff-style sequential design for identifying the defect hypothesis per cell.
- **[lindley1956measure]** D. V. Lindley (1956). *On a Measure of the Information Provided by an Experiment*. Ann. Math. Stat. (peer-reviewed). [link](https://doi.org/10.1214/aoms/1177728069)
  - Relevance **medium**; role: foundation; components: active synthesis
  - Introduces a Shannon-based measure of the information an experiment provides relative to a prior over the parameter, uses it to compare experiments, and applies it to design problems whose aim is to gain knowledge rather than to reach decisions.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Defines the information-gain quantity that SpecDx uses as the reward for a scenario; SpecDx computes a realised (post-hoc) version from the EM posterior.
- **[wald1945sequential]** A. Wald (1945). *Sequential Tests of Statistical Hypotheses*. Ann. Math. Stat. (peer-reviewed). [link](https://doi.org/10.1214/aoms/1177731118)
  - Relevance **medium**; role: foundation; components: active synthesis
  - Introduces the sequential probability ratio test, which keeps sampling until the likelihood ratio crosses one of two thresholds, reaching given error rates with fewer expected observations than a fixed-sample test.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* The principle behind SpecDx's stop/escalate decision per cell (stop after the pilot when evidence is decisive, otherwise run the full committee). SpecDx uses a posterior threshold from the cell model rather than a two-hypothesis likelihood ratio.
- **[dobslaw2024automated]** Felix Dobslaw, Robert Feldt (2024). *Automated Boundary Identification for Machine Learning Classifiers*. SBFT (peer-reviewed). [link](https://doi.org/10.1145/3643659.3643927)
  - Relevance **low**; role: context; components: active synthesis
  - Extends automated boundary-value identification (pairs of nearby inputs with highly differing outputs) from traditional software to machine-learning classifiers, so developers can judge whether a model's boundaries are where they should be.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Boundary candidates for learned components; SpecDx's executors are LLM agents whose decision boundary is compared with the specification's declared one.
- **[perlitz2024efficient]** Yotam Perlitz, Elron Bandel, Ariel Gera et al. (2024). *Efficient Benchmarking (of Language Models)*. NAACL (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A). [link](https://doi.org/10.18653/v1/2024.naacl-long.139)
  - Relevance **low**; role: context; components: active synthesis
  - Defines efficient benchmarking (cutting LM evaluation cost without losing reliability), introduces the Decision Impact on Reliability (DIoR) measure, and shows on HELM that design choices such as removing low-ranked models can flip leaders, while a proposed algorithm cuts computation by 100x or more.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Budget-aware evaluation context; SpecDx's run budget on the 48 GB host motivates the same concern but the object being assessed is the specification.
- **[tang2023survey]** Shuncheng Tang, Zhenya Zhang, Yi Zhang et al. (2023). *A Survey on Automated Driving System Testing: Landscapes and Trends*. TOSEM (peer-reviewed, CORE2020 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.1145/3579642)
  - Relevance **low**; role: survey; components: active synthesis
  - Surveys automated driving system testing at both the module level (sensing, perception, planning, control) and the system level, covering empirical studies of field issues, inter-module problems and the simulator-to-reality gap, and outlines trends and open problems.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Survey pointer for the search-based scenario-generation literature that SpecDx's active synthesis borrows from.
- **[ramdas2023game]** Aaditya Ramdas, Peter Grünwald, Vladimir Vovk et al. (2023). *Game-Theoretic Statistics and Safe Anytime-Valid Inference*. Stat. Sci. (peer-reviewed). [link](https://doi.org/10.1214/23-sts894)
  - Relevance **low**; role: survey; components: active synthesis
  - Surveys safe anytime-valid inference: e-processes and confidence sequences built on test martingales that remain valid under continuous monitoring and optional stopping, with game-theoretic foundations.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Reference for making SpecDx's escalation and stopping decisions valid under adaptive scenario choice.
- **[perez2022red]** Ethan Perez, Saffron Huang, Francis Song et al. (2022). *Red Teaming Language Models with Language Models*. EMNLP (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.18653/v1/2022.emnlp-main.225)
  - Relevance **low**; role: context; components: active synthesis
  - Uses one LM to generate test cases that elicit harmful behaviour from a target LM, judged by a classifier, comparing zero-shot, few-shot, supervised and RL generation for diversity and difficulty.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Automatic LM-based test generation with an automatic judge; SpecDx's generator is grounded in clause literals and its judge is the executable check C itself, whose correctness is under test.
- **[chen2020practical]** Junjie Chen, Zhuo Wu, Zan Wang et al. (2020). *Practical Accuracy Estimation for Efficient Deep Neural Network Testing*. TOSEM (peer-reviewed, CORE2020 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.1145/3394112)
  - Relevance **low**; role: context; components: active synthesis
  - PACE clusters test inputs by the functionality they exercise, then selects prototypes from each group with the MMD-critic algorithm (proportional to group size), so that labeling a small selected set precisely estimates DNN accuracy on the whole test set.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Representative-subset selection for evaluation; a non-adaptive alternative to SpecDx's Thompson-EIG for choosing which scenario cells to execute.
- **[gal2017deep]** Yarin Gal, Riashat Islam, Zoubin Ghahramani (2017). *Deep Bayesian Active Learning with Image Data*. ICML (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://proceedings.mlr.press/v70/gal17a.html)
  - Relevance **low**; role: context; components: active synthesis
  - Combines Bayesian deep learning (MC-dropout Bayesian CNNs) with information-theoretic acquisition functions such as BALD and variation ratios, making active learning practical for high-dimensional image data.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Shows BALD-style acquisition with approximate posteriors; SpecDx's committee of distinct LLM families plays the role of posterior samples, but the queried object is a business scenario, not an image.
- **[frazier2008knowledge]** Peter I. Frazier, Warren B. Powell, Savas Dayanik (2008). *A Knowledge-Gradient Policy for Sequential Information Collection*. SIAM J. Control Optim. (peer-reviewed). [link](https://doi.org/10.1137/070693424)
  - Relevance **low**; role: foundation; components: active synthesis
  - Analyses the knowledge-gradient policy for Bayesian ranking and selection, which myopically maximises the expected increment in value of information, proving optimality for one-step and infinite horizons and bounding its suboptimality otherwise.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Knowledge gradient is the value-of-information alternative to Thompson-EIG for choosing SpecDx's next scenario; it underlies the optimistic-KG crowdsourcing policy.
- **[kuhn2004software]** D.R. Kuhn, D.R. Wallace, A.M. Gallo (2004). *Software fault interactions and implications for software testing*. TSE (peer-reviewed, CORE2020 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.1109/tse.2004.24)
  - Relevance **low**; role: foundation; components: active synthesis
  - Empirical study across domains showing that software failures are triggered by combinations of few conditions, so testing all n-way parameter combinations for small n can be effectively exhaustive.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Supports constructing SpecDx's scenario space from small combinations of clause literals (pairwise coverage of conditions) rather than full cross-products.
- **[claessen2000quickcheck]** Koen Claessen, John Hughes (2000). *QuickCheck: a lightweight tool for random testing of Haskell programs*. ICFP (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A). [link](https://doi.org/10.1145/351240.351266)
  - Relevance **low**; role: foundation; components: active synthesis
  - QuickCheck lets Haskell programmers state program properties as functions and test them automatically on random inputs drawn from customisable generators.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* SpecDx's executable clauses play the role of properties and the scenario synthesiser the role of generators; SpecDx adds an LLM population as executor and asks whether the property itself is wrong.
- **[cohen1997aetg]** D.M. Cohen, S.R. Dalal, M.L. Fredman et al. (1997). *The AETG system: an approach to testing based on combinatorial design*. TSE (peer-reviewed, CORE2020 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.1109/32.605761)
  - Relevance **low**; role: foundation; components: active synthesis
  - Presents AETG, which uses combinatorial designs to generate test sets covering all valid pairwise or n-way combinations of the parameters that define test scenarios, with test-set size growing logarithmically in the number of parameters.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Classic non-adaptive scenario-space covering; a natural 'pairwise' alternative to random scenario selection (A4) for SpecDx's pilot round.
- **[weiss1984application]** DAVID J. WEISS, G. GAGE KINGSBURY (1984). *APPLICATION OF COMPUTERIZED ADAPTIVE TESTING TO EDUCATIONAL PROBLEMS*. J. Educ. Meas. (peer-reviewed). [link](https://doi.org/10.1111/j.1745-3984.1984.tb01040.x)
  - Relevance **low**; role: foundation; components: active synthesis, item difficulty/IRT
  - Describes three IRT-based CAT applications in education: adaptive mastery testing, adaptive grading, and adaptive self-referenced testing for measuring change, with evidence that adaptive mastery testing reaches decisions with fewer items.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Adaptive mastery testing (stop as soon as a classification is confident) is the psychometric twin of SpecDx's per-cell escalation-and-stop rule.
- **[brien1979multiple]** Peter C. O'Brien, Thomas R. Fleming (1979). *A Multiple Testing Procedure for Clinical Trials*. Biometrics (peer-reviewed). [link](https://doi.org/10.2307/2530245)
  - Relevance **low**; role: foundation; components: active synthesis
  - Proposes a multiple-testing procedure for comparing two treatments with repeated chi-square tests at a fixed maximum number of looks, keeping overall size and power close to a single fixed-sample test while allowing early termination (the O'Brien-Fleming boundary).
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Alternative stopping boundary for SpecDx's stage-wise escalation; conservative early boundaries would make a pilot-only defect decision rare.
- **[pocock1977group]** S. J. POCOCK (1977). *Group sequential methods in the design and analysis of clinical trials*. Biometrika (peer-reviewed). [link](https://doi.org/10.1093/biomet/64.2.191)
  - Relevance **low**; role: foundation; components: active synthesis
  - Proposes group sequential designs that split entry into equal-sized groups and decide after each group whether to stop, using repeated significance tests, with exact results for normal responses and guidance on the number and size of groups.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* SpecDx's pilot (one model per family) then full committee is a two-stage group-sequential design; Pocock-style boundaries are a way to control the false-escalation rate.

<a id="t8_bpm_agents_policies"></a>
## Business-process agents and policies, conformance checking, rule verification

- **[calvanese2026agentic]** Diego Calvanese, Angelo Casciani, Giuseppe De Giacomo et al. (2026). *Agentic Business Process Management: A research manifesto*. IS (peer-reviewed, CORE2020 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.is.2026.102738)
  - Relevance **high**; role: survey
  - A research manifesto for Agentic Business Process Management (APM), in which software and human agents execute processes within explicit process frames; it introduces core abstractions and architecture and four capabilities APM agents need: framed autonomy, explainability, conversational actionability and self-modification.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Positions LLM agents as executors of business process specifications, SpecDx's setting; the manifesto assumes the process frame is the reference for alignment, while SpecDx shows how the frame itself can be diagnosed from agents' deviations.
- **[matei2026ambiguity]** Ion Matei, Praveen Kumar Menaka Sekar, Maksym Zhenirovskyy et al. (2026). *Ambiguity Detection and Elimination in Automated Executable Process Modeling*. arXiv (preprint). [link](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.10884) · **baseline candidate**
  - Relevance **high**; role: direct competitor; components: ddmin, defect taxonomy, set-valued truth, baseline B4
  - Detects when a natural-language process specification fails to support a stable executable interpretation: BPMN models are generated and simulated repeatedly, behavioural inconsistency is detected from the empirical KPI distribution, localised to gateway logic by model-based diagnosis, mapped back to verbatim narrative segments, and the text is repaired; demonstrated on health-guidance policies.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* The closest threat in this theme: it uses repeated LLM interpretations without ground truth to detect and localise ambiguity in T, as SpecDx's components 1 and 5 do. It differs by covering only ambiguity (not contradiction, hidden requirement or weak check), using one generator rather than a multi-family population with an error model, having no executable checks C or anchors, and giving no refutation witnesses.
- **[anand2026mantra]** Ashwani Anand, Ivi Chatzi, Ritam Raha et al. (2026). *MANTRA: Synthesizing SMT-Validated Compliance Benchmarks for Tool-Using LLM Agents*. arXiv (preprint). [link](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.06334)
  - Relevance **high**; role: direct competitor; components: MUS/MCS, refutation, active synthesis, defect taxonomy
  - MANTRA synthesises machine-checkable compliance benchmarks from natural-language procedural manuals and tool schemas: it separately generates a symbolic world model and trace-level compliance checks, validates their mutual consistency with SMT solving, repairs inconsistencies in a loop, and derives tasks of tunable complexity.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Threatens part of SpecDx's novelty: it automatically detects inconsistencies between NL-derived checks and a model of the manual using a solver. It differs in that it validates checks it generated itself, uses no population of executors, and gives no typed diagnosis of ambiguity or weak checks in a pre-existing specification.
- **[winston2026solver]** Cailin Winston, Claris Winston, René Just (2026). *Solver-Aided Verification of Policy Compliance in Tool-Augmented LLM Agents*. arXiv (preprint). [link](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.20449) · **baseline candidate**
  - Relevance **high**; role: component prior art; components: baseline B5, refutation
  - Translates natural-language tool-use policies into SMT-LIB constraints with an LLM-assisted, human-guided process; at runtime each planned tool call is checked with Z3 as a precondition and blocked if it violates the policy; evaluated on τ-bench with fewer violations at unchanged task accuracy.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Shares SpecDx's use of solvers over τ-bench policy clauses, but for enforcement on agents; it does not use the solver to test the policy/check pair for contradictions or looseness.
- **[levy2026webagentbench]** Ido Levy, Ben Wiesel, Sami Marreed et al. (2026). *ST-WebAgentBench: A Benchmark for Evaluating Safety and Trustworthiness in Web Agents*. ICLR (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://proceedings.iclr.cc/paper_files/paper/2026/hash/c6c29e590e3c62e37e6b39cdd6baf2e8-Abstract-Conference.html)
  - Relevance **high**; role: evaluation/benchmark
  - ST-WebAgentBench pairs enterprise web tasks with explicit safety and trustworthiness policies scored on six dimensions, and introduces Completion-under-Policy, which credits only completions that respect all policies, plus a Risk Ratio of breaches.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Policies are explicit clauses checked over agent behaviour, as in SpecDx's C; the benchmark measures agents against them, while SpecDx asks whether the policy clauses themselves are contradictory, hidden or weak.
- **[levi2025intellagent]** Elad Levi, Ilan Kadar (2025). *IntellAgent: A Multi-Agent Framework for Evaluating Conversational AI Systems*. arXiv (preprint). [link](https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.11067) · [code](https://github.com/plurai-ai/intellagent) ★1260 · **baseline candidate**
  - Relevance **high**; role: component prior art; also in t7_active_testing_design; components: active synthesis, baseline B1
  - IntellAgent is an open-source multi-agent framework that builds a graph of policies (nodes weighted by complexity, edges by co-occurrence), samples multi-policy events and user goals from it, simulates user-agent dialogues and critiques the agent's policy adherence for fine-grained diagnostics.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Shares SpecDx's policy-driven scenario generation and fine-grained per-policy diagnosis, but its goal is to diagnose the agent and it treats the policy as correct; SpecDx diagnoses the policy/check pair from a population of agents. | Closest prior work on synthesising business scenarios from policies for tau-bench-style agents. IntellAgent samples policy combinations to evaluate the agent and trusts the policies; SpecDx chooses scenarios adaptively (Thompson-EIG, clause boundaries) to diagnose defects in the policies and checks themselves.
- **[li2025sopbench]** Zekun Li, Shinda Huang, Jiangtian Wang et al. (2025). *SOPBench: Evaluating Language Agents at Following Standard Operating Procedures and Constraints*. arXiv (preprint). [link](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.08669) · [code](https://github.com/Leezekun/SOPBench) ★24
  - Relevance **high**; role: evaluation/benchmark; components: active synthesis, baseline B2
  - SOPBench turns service-specific SOP code programs into directed graphs of executable functions across seven customer-service domains; agents must follow the natural-language SOP, the original code acts as a rule-based oracle verifier, and an automated generator produces 900+ verified test cases.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Closest benchmark design to SpecDx's setting: NL SOP (T) paired with executable verifier code (C) and generated scenarios (Π). SOPBench assumes the code oracle matches the text; SpecDx tests that assumption and localises mismatches.
- **[zwerdling2025enforcing]** Naama Zwerdling, David Boaz, Ella Rabinovich et al. (2025). *Towards Enforcing Company Policy Adherence in Agentic Workflows*. EMNLP Industry (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A* (Industry track)). [link](https://doi.org/10.18653/v1/2025.emnlp-industry.41) · [code](https://github.com/AgentToolkit/toolguard) ★7 · **baseline candidate**
  - Relevance **high**; role: component prior art; components: baseline B5, baseline B1
  - Compiles company policy documents offline into verifiable guard code attached to each tool, and runs the guards before each agent action at runtime, giving deterministic and transparent policy enforcement; demonstrated on the τ-bench airline domain.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Works on exactly SpecDx's τ-bench airline subject and makes T executable as guards, but trusts the policy and the compilation; SpecDx's comparison of T, C and agent behaviour would expose where ToolGuard's guards and the benchmark checks disagree.
- **[corea2022measuring]** Carl Corea, John Grant, Matthias Thimm (2022). *Measuring Inconsistency in Declarative Process Specifications*. BPM (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A). [link](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-031-16103-2_20) · **baseline candidate**
  - Relevance **high**; role: component prior art; components: MUS/MCS, baseline B5
  - Shows that classical inconsistency measures cannot assess inconsistency in LTL with temporal operators, proposes a paraconsistent semantics for LTL on fixed traces, and defines two inconsistency measures with desirable properties for declarative process specifications, with a complexity analysis.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Quantifies how inconsistent a declarative specification is, with MUS-based measures among the baselines; SpecDx localises and types contradictions from behaviour and refutes candidates with witnesses, which these measures do not.
- **[aa2018checking]** Han van der Aa, Henrik Leopold, Hajo A. Reijers (2018). *Checking process compliance against natural language specifications using behavioral spaces*. IS (peer-reviewed, CORE2020 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.is.2018.01.007)
  - Relevance **high**; role: component prior art; components: set-valued truth
  - Journal version of the behavioural-space approach: a behavioural space systematically captures all interpretations of an ambiguous textual process description, so that compliance checking against the text avoids committing to one possibly wrong reading; evaluated on 47 textual descriptions.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Compliance against all readings of T parallels SpecDx's satisfaction-based defect definitions over set-valued truth; SpecDx adds executable clauses and automatic detection of which statements are ambiguous.
- **[carmona2018conformance]** Josep Carmona, Boudewijn van Dongen, Andreas Solti et al. (2018). *Conformance Checking: Relating Processes and Models*. Springer book (book). [link](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-319-99414-7)
  - Relevance **high**; role: foundation
  - Book by Carmona, van Dongen, Solti and Weidlich that formalises the relation between modelled and recorded behaviour and surveys conformance-checking techniques (alignments, fitness, precision, generalisation) at an intuitive and formal level.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Gives the vocabulary for SpecDx's analogy: a fitness failure shared by many traces at the same place can mean the model (specification) is wrong; precision failures correspond to SpecDx's weak checks.
- **[calvanese2018semantics]** Diego Calvanese, Marlon Dumas, Ülari Laurson et al. (2018). *Semantics, Analysis and Simplification of DMN Decision Tables*. IS (peer-reviewed, CORE2020 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.is.2018.01.010) · **baseline candidate**
  - Relevance **high**; role: component prior art; components: baseline B5
  - Journal version: a formal semantics of DMN decision tables, a general geometric approach to analyse and refactor them, efficient algorithms for detecting overlapping and missing rules, and a table simplification by rule merging, evaluated on real and synthetic tables.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* The strongest symbolic baseline for rule-shaped clauses: it finds conflicts and gaps in C by geometry alone. SpecDx differs by relating C to the NL text T through executor readings, and by typing hidden requirements and weak checks that are invisible inside C alone.
- **[aa2017comparing]** Han van der Aa, Henrik Leopold, Hajo A. Reijers (2017). *Comparing textual descriptions to process models – The automatic detection of inconsistencies*. IS (peer-reviewed, CORE2020 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.is.2016.07.010) · **baseline candidate**
  - Relevance **high**; role: component prior art; also in t3_requirements_nlp; components: baseline B1
  - Automatically detects inconsistencies between a process model and its textual description by aligning sentences to model activities with NLP and flagging activities described in different orders and model activities missing from the text; evaluated on 53 real model-text pairs.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Directly detects T-versus-C-style inconsistencies between two representations of a process, which is SpecDx's goal for T and C. It uses linguistic alignment of one model and one text, with no executors, no ambiguity type and no executable checks. | A business-specification analogue of the T-versus-C gap: text versus a formal artefact. SpecDx compares T and C through executions rather than by NLP alignment, and also types defects (Hidden requirement, Weak check).
- **[ciccio2017resolving]** Claudio Di Ciccio, Fabrizio Maria Maggi, Marco Montali et al. (2017). *Resolving inconsistencies and redundancies in declarative process models*. IS (peer-reviewed, CORE2020 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.is.2016.09.005) · **baseline candidate**
  - Relevance **high**; role: component prior art; components: MUS/MCS, baseline B5
  - Discovered Declare models can contain mutually contradicting constraints that accept no trace; the paper resolves such inconsistencies and removes redundancies automatically using automata products (an automata-product monoid), keeping the most interesting constraints while guaranteeing a consistent model.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Directly addresses contradiction in a declarative clause set, i.e., SpecDx's contradiction type, with automata rather than sampled correction sets; it decides which constraints to drop by interest heuristics, not by evidence from executors or the NL text.
- **[aa2016dealing]** Han van der Aa, Henrik Leopold, Hajo A. Reijers (2016). *Dealing with Behavioral Ambiguity in Textual Process Descriptions*. BPM (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A). [link](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-319-45348-4_16)
  - Relevance **high**; role: component prior art; components: set-valued truth, defect taxonomy
  - Introduces the behavioural space, which systematically captures all interpretations of ambiguous statements in a textual process description, and uses it for compliance checking on 47 texts, balancing between ignoring ambiguous statements and fixing one interpretation.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Represents an ambiguous text by its set of readings, the same idea as SpecDx's set-valued entailment set A_c={x,y}. SpecDx estimates the readings empirically from LLM executors rather than enumerating them linguistically.
- **[polyvyanyy2016impact]** Artem Polyvyanyy, Wil M. P. Van Der Aalst, Arthur H. M. Ter Hofstede et al. (2016). *Impact-Driven Process Model Repair*. TOSEM (peer-reviewed, CORE2020 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.1145/2980764)
  - Relevance **high**; role: component prior art
  - Impact-driven model repair assigns costs to repair actions (inserting or skipping activities) and, under a bound on the degree of change, searches for the repaired model that best improves alignment with the log.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Treats specification repair as an optimisation over minimal changes, related to SpecDx's minimal correction sets; it trusts the log fully, whereas SpecDx models executor fallibility.
- **[solti2016log]** Andreas Rogge-Solti, Arik Senderovich, Matthias Weidlich et al. (2016). *In Log and Model We Trust? A Generalized Conformance Checking Framework*. BPM (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A). [link](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-319-45348-4_11)
  - Relevance **high**; role: direct competitor; components: anchored cell model, set-valued truth
  - Notes that conformance checking assumes either the log or the model can be fully trusted, and proposes a generalized conformance-checking framework for the common case where neither is fully trusted, balancing repairs of log and model; it generalises standard techniques.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Closest BPM idea to SpecDx's anchored model: both the specification (anchor) and the observations are fallible and the method decides which to correct. It differs by working on one log of process traces rather than a population of LLM executors, without executor accuracy/item-difficulty parameters, typed defects or witnesses.
- **[calvanese2016semantics]** Diego Calvanese, Marlon Dumas, Ülari Laurson et al. (2016). *Semantics and Analysis of DMN Decision Tables*. BPM (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A). [link](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-319-45348-4_13)
  - Relevance **high**; role: component prior art; components: baseline B5, defect taxonomy

- **[fahland2015model]** Dirk Fahland, Wil M.P. van der Aalst (2015). *Model repair — aligning process models to reality*. IS (peer-reviewed, CORE2020 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.is.2013.12.007)

  - Repairs a process model with respect to an event log so that the repaired model replays the log while staying as similar as possible to the original, by aligning log and model, grouping deviations into sublogs and adding loops or subprocesses at the right location.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* The core analogy for SpecDx: systematic deviations at the same location are evidence that the model should change. Model repair assumes the log is correct and repairs everything; SpecDx must separate specification defects from scattered executor errors before blaming the specification.
- **[elkoussy2026agentltl]** Laïla Elkoussy, Julien Perez (2026). *AgentLTL: A Trace-Verification Framework for Measuring, Enforcing, and Training Procedural Compliance in Tool-Using LLM Agents*. arXiv (preprint). [link](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.02599)
  - Relevance **medium**; role: component prior art; components: baseline B5

- **[wang2026agentspec]** Haoyu Wang, Christopher M. Poskitt, Jun Sun (2026). *AgentSpec: Customizable Runtime Enforcement for Safe and Reliable LLM Agents*. ICSE (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.1145/3744916.3764546) · [code](https://github.com/haoyuwang99/AgentSpec) ★62

- **[mondl2026autoformalization]** Adam Mondl, Matthew Maisel, John H. Brock (2026). *Autoformalization of Agent Instructions into Policy-as-Code*. ICML-W (preprint). [link](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.26649)

- **[balaji2026beyond]** Sumanth Balaji, Piyush Mishra, Aashraya Sachdeva et al. (2026). *Beyond IVR: Benchmarking Customer Support LLM Agents for Business-Adherence*. arXiv (preprint). [link](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.00596)

- **[yang2026complibench]** Jingbo Yang, Guanyu Yao, Bairu Hou et al. (2026). *CompliBench: Benchmarking LLM Judges for Compliance Violation Detection in Dialogue Systems*. arXiv (preprint). [link](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.12312)

  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Its controllable flaw injection with exact ground truth parallels SpecDx's mutation operators, but flaws are injected into agent behaviour, not into the specification; LLM-judge verdicts are a possible single-judge baseline.
- **[kang2026policyguard]** Seongjae Kang, Taehyung Yu, Sung Ju Hwang (2026). *PolicyGuard: A Dialogue-Grounded Sub-Agent Verifier for Policy Adherence in LLM Agents*. arXiv (preprint). [link](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.29225)

  - PolicyGuard is a sub-agent verifier that shares the agent's dialogue view, intercepts mutating tool calls, reasons over the raw policy text in context and returns remediation feedback; on τ²-bench airline it raises pass^4 by 6-12 points across three vendors and transfers to retail and telecom.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Evaluated on SpecDx's τ²-bench subject and reasons directly over the policy text T; it treats T as authoritative and fixes agents, so residual disagreements between PolicyGuard and the benchmark checks are candidate specification defects SpecDx would type.
- **[wang2026probguard]** Haoyu Wang, Christopher M. Poskitt, Jiali Wei et al. (2026). *ProbGuard: Proactive Runtime Monitoring for LLM Agent Safety via Probabilistic Prediction*. ASE (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.1145/3832783.3837413)

- **[sun2025compliance]** Jingyun Sun, Zhongze Luo, Yang Li (2025). *A Compliance Checking Framework Based on Retrieval Augmented Generation*. COLING (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 B). [link](https://aclanthology.org/2025.coling-main.178/)

- **[huang2025crmarena]** Kung-Hsiang Huang, Akshara Prabhakar, Onkar Thorat et al. (2025). *CRMArena-Pro: Holistic Assessment of LLM Agents Across Diverse Business Scenarios and Interactions*. arXiv (preprint). [link](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.18878)

- **[huang2025crmarenaa]** Kung-Hsiang Huang, Akshara Prabhakar, Sidharth Dhawan et al. [link](https://doi.org/10.18653/v1/2025.naacl-long.194)

- **[kamath2025enforcing]** Adharsh Kamath, Sishen Zhang, Calvin Xu et al. (2025). *Enforcing Temporal Constraints for LLM Agents*. arXiv (preprint). [link](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.23738)

- **[kourani2025evaluating]** Humam Kourani, Alessandro Berti, Daniel Schuster et al. (2025). *Evaluating large language models on business process modeling: framework, benchmark, and self-improvement analysis*. SoSyM (peer-reviewed, CORE2020 B). [link](https://doi.org/10.1007/s10270-025-01318-w)
