  - First approach to automatically identify inconsistencies between a process model and a corresponding textual description, detecting different activity orders and model activities missing from the text; evaluated on 46 real-life model-text pairs.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* BPM 2015 predecessor of van der Aa et al. (IS 2017); same relation to SpecDx.
- **[ciccio2015discovery]** Claudio Di Ciccio, Massimo Mecella (2015). *On the Discovery of Declarative Control Flows for Artful Processes*. TMIS (peer-reviewed). [link](https://doi.org/10.1145/2629447)
  - Relevance **medium**; role: component prior art
  - MINERful discovers declarative control-flow constraints from logs in two phases: it builds a knowledge base of statistics extracted from the log and then queries it to infer Declare constraints with support and confidence.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Mining constraints with support/confidence from many traces is the inverse of SpecDx (which mines where given constraints are violated by many executors); MINERful is also the implementation host of the Di Ciccio et al. inconsistency resolution.
- **[leopold2014supporting]** Henrik Leopold, Jan Mendling, Artem Polyvyanyy (2014). *Supporting Process Model Validation through Natural Language Generation*. TSE (peer-reviewed, CORE2020 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.1109/tse.2014.2327044)
  - Relevance **medium**; role: foundation
  - Generates natural-language texts from business process models so that domain experts can validate models; generated texts are more complete and structured than manual ones and let readers infer model semantics.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Validation of a formal specification through its NL reading, the reverse direction of SpecDx, which validates T and C by how executors read and act on them.
- **[leoni2013aligning]** Massimiliano de Leoni, Wil M. P. van der Aalst (2013). *Aligning Event Logs and Process Models for Multi-perspective Conformance Checking: An Approach Based on Integer Linear Programming*. BPM (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A). [link](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-642-40176-3_10)
  - Relevance **medium**; role: component prior art
  - Aligns event logs and process models across control flow, data, resources and time using integer linear programming, so that conformance can be quantified and differences diagnosed beyond control flow.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Data-aware alignment is the BPM counterpart of SpecDx's cells, which are (table,row,column) values read at a scenario step; SpecDx's guardrail clauses over PostgreSQL state are multi-perspective constraints.
- **[adriansyah2011conformance]** A. Adriansyah, B.F. van Dongen, W.M.P. van der Aalst (2011). *Conformance Checking Using Cost-Based Fitness Analysis*. EDOC (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 B). [link](https://doi.org/10.1109/edoc.2011.12)
  - Relevance **medium**; role: foundation
  - Presents a robust replay technique that measures conformance of an event log to a process model with configurable costs for deviations and returns diagnostics about where they occur; the basis of cost-based alignments.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Alignments localise deviations per trace; SpecDx's per-cell deviation counts across a population play the same localising role, with statistical aggregation across executors.
- **[rozinat2008conformance]** A. Rozinat, W.M.P. van der Aalst (2008). *Conformance checking of processes based on monitoring real behavior*. IS (peer-reviewed, CORE2020 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.is.2007.07.001)
  - Relevance **medium**; role: foundation
  - Proposes an incremental conformance-checking approach that first measures fitness of a log to a process model and then the model's behavioural and structural appropriateness, implemented as the ProM Conformance Checker.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Classic log-versus-model comparison; SpecDx applies the same comparison to agent behaviours versus clauses, but adds a latent model that decides whether the model or the executor is at fault.
- **[borman2026beyond]** Harris Borman, Herman Wandabwa, Fusun Yu et al. (2026). *Beyond Generalist LLMs: Specialist Agentic Systems for Structured Code Workflow Execution*. arXiv (preprint). [link](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.14456)
  - Relevance **low**; role: context
  - Compares a specialist workflow with generalist coding agents for turning BPMN diagrams into executable agentic workflows where model and inputs uniquely determine the path, finding the specialist more exact, faster and cheaper.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Executes BPMN specifications with LLM agents and measures exactness against the model; it presumes the BPMN is correct.
- **[xiao2026symbolic]** Yifeng Xiao, Pierluigi Nuzzo (2026). *Symbolic Temporal Supervision of LLM Agents Using Contracts*. arXiv (preprint). [link](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.18128)
  - Relevance **low**; role: component prior art
  - ContrAgent formalises an agent's tool-call trace over checkable predicates and specifies required behaviour as assume-guarantee contracts in LTLf compiled to DFAs, used both for online gating and offline trace evaluation.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Contract libraries are another form of executable clause set C; contract consistency checking (assume-guarantee) is a symbolic B5-style analysis without interpreters.
- **[styles2026workbench]** Olly Styles, Sam Miller (2026). *WorkBench Revisited: Workplace Agents Two Years On*. arXiv (preprint). [link](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.13715)
  - Relevance **low**; role: context
  - Revisits WorkBench two years on: frontier agents now complete 98% of tasks and make far fewer harmful actions, and the authors release an updated benchmark with data and code quality improvements.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Illustrates that benchmark specifications need repair over time ('data and code quality improvements'), the maintenance task SpecDx automates.
- **[norouzifar2025bridging]** Ali Norouzifar, Humam Kourani, Marcus Dees et al. (2025). *Bridging Domain Knowledge and Process Discovery Using Large Language Models*. BPM Workshops (LNBIP) (peer-reviewed). [link](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-031-78666-2_4)
  - Relevance **low**; role: context
  - Uses LLM-derived declarative rules from domain knowledge and process documentation to guide process discovery so that the discovered model aligns with both the documentation and the event log, with a case study at the UWV insurance agency.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Reconciles NL documentation with observed behaviour, a BPM analogue of SpecDx's T-versus-behaviour comparison, but trusts the documentation-derived rules.
- **[nakash2025effective]** Itay Nakash, George Kour, Koren Lazar et al. (2025). *Effective Red-Teaming of Policy-Adherent Agents*. arXiv (preprint). [link](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.09600)
  - Relevance **low**; role: context
  - Proposes a threat model of adversarial users exploiting policy-adherent customer-service agents, the CRAFT multi-agent red-teaming system using policy-aware persuasion, and τ-break, a τ-bench extension for robustness to manipulative users.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Uses τ-bench policies as the fixed reference and attacks agents; complementary to SpecDx, which questions the policy and checks rather than the agent's robustness.
- **[berti2025llm]** Alessandro Berti, Humam Kourani, Wil M. P. van der Aalst (2025). *PM-LLM-Benchmark: Evaluating Large Language Models on Process Mining Tasks*. ICPM Workshops (LNBIP) (peer-reviewed). [link](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-031-82225-4_45)
  - Relevance **low**; role: evaluation/benchmark
  - PM-LLM-Benchmark evaluates LLMs on process-mining tasks requiring process-mining and process-specific domain knowledge, finds most models adequate for some tasks but tiny models inadequate, and discusses evaluation biases of LLM judges.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Context: LLM competence on process artefacts and the bias of LLM judges, relevant to SpecDx's use of local models and its baseline B1.
- **[kholkar2025policy]** Gauri Kholkar, Ratinder Ahuja (2025). *Policy-as-Prompt: Turning AI Governance Rules into Guardrails for AI Agents*. NeurIPS-W (preprint). [link](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.23994)
  - Relevance **low**; role: context
  - Converts unstructured design artifacts (PRDs, technical design documents, code) and risk controls into a source-linked policy tree that is compiled into prompt-based classifiers for runtime monitoring of agents, with provenance and human review.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Derives guardrails C from requirement documents T with traceability links, the T-to-C mapping that SpecDx audits; it does not test the derived guardrails for defects.
- **[grohs2024large]** Michael Grohs, Luka Abb, Nourhan Elsayed et al. (2024). *Large Language Models Can Accomplish Business Process Management Tasks*. BPM Workshops (LNBIP) (peer-reviewed). [link](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-031-50974-2_34)
  - Relevance **low**; role: context
  - Empirically shows that general-purpose LLMs can accomplish several BPM tasks from text, such as mining imperative and declarative process models from textual descriptions and assessing task suitability for automation.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Evidence that LLMs can read process texts into models, which SpecDx exploits as readings of T; no defect diagnosis.
- **[kampik2024large]** Timotheus Kampik, Christian Warmuth, Adrian Rebmann et al. (2024). *Large Process Models: A Vision for Business Process Management in the Age of Generative AI*. KI (peer-reviewed). [link](https://doi.org/10.1007/s13218-024-00863-8)
  - Relevance **low**; role: context
  - Proposes Large Process Models that combine the correlation power of LLMs with the precision of knowledge-based systems and automated reasoning, to deliver tailored process models and analyses to organisations.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Advocates the neuro-symbolic combination SpecDx instantiates (statistics over LLM executors plus MUS/MCS reasoning), but as a vision without a diagnosis method.
- **[xu2024theagentcompany]** Frank F. Xu, Yufan Song, Boxuan Li et al. (2024). *TheAgentCompany: Benchmarking LLM Agents on Consequential Real World Tasks*. arXiv (preprint). [link](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.14161)
  - Relevance **low**; role: evaluation/benchmark
  - TheAgentCompany is an extensible benchmark that simulates a small software company with internal websites and simulated colleagues, and measures how far LLM agents can autonomously complete consequential professional tasks, with checkpoint-based partial credit.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* A business-workflow benchmark with checkpoint evaluators (C); context for SpecDx's claim that agent benchmarks treat their checks as ground truth.
- **[vidgof2023large]** Maxim Vidgof, Stefan Bachhofner, Jan Mendling (2023). *Large Language Models for Business Process Management: Opportunities and Challenges*. BPM Forum (LNBIP) (peer-reviewed). [link](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-031-41623-1_7)
  - Relevance **low**; role: survey
  - Position paper (BPM 2023 Forum) discussing opportunities and challenges of applying large language models across the BPM lifecycle.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Context on LLM use in BPM; does not address specification defects.
- **[rebedea2023nemo]** Traian Rebedea, Razvan Dinu, Makesh Narsimhan Sreedhar et al. (2023). *NeMo Guardrails: A Toolkit for Controllable and Safe LLM Applications with Programmable Rails*. EMNLP Demo (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.18653/v1/2023.emnlp-demo.40)
  - Relevance **low**; role: context
  - NeMo Guardrails is an open-source toolkit that adds user-defined, model-independent and interpretable programmable rails (e.g., topic restrictions, predefined dialogue paths) to LLM conversational systems through a runtime inspired by dialogue management.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* An industrial guardrail layer of the kind SpecDx's guardrail triggers model; context only.
- **[bellan2023pet]** Patrizio Bellan, Han van der Aa, Mauro Dragoni et al. (2023). *PET: An Annotated Dataset for Process Extraction from Natural Language Text Tasks*. BPM Workshops (LNBIP) (peer-reviewed). [link](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-031-25383-6_23)
  - Relevance **low**; role: evaluation/benchmark
  - PET is a gold-standard corpus of business process descriptions annotated with process entities and relations for evaluating process extraction from text.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* An annotated NL process corpus; a possible source of T documents but without executable checks.
- **[ye2023proagent]** Yining Ye, Xin Cong, Shizuo Tian et al. (2023). *ProAgent: From Robotic Process Automation to Agentic Process Automation*. arXiv (preprint). [link](https://arxiv.org/abs/2311.10751) · [code](https://github.com/OpenBMB/ProAgent) ★865
  - Relevance **low**; role: context
  - Introduces Agentic Process Automation, where LLM agents both construct workflows from human instructions and make decisions during execution, instantiated as ProAgent.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* LLM agents as process executors; context for SpecDx's executor population.
- **[dunzer2019conformance]** Sebastian Dunzer, Matthias Stierle, Martin Matzner et al. (2019). *Conformance checking: a state-of-the-art literature review*. S-BPM ONE (peer-reviewed). [link](https://doi.org/10.1145/3329007.3329014)
  - Relevance **low**; role: survey
  - Reviews 37 publications on conformance-checking techniques and classifies them along modelling language, algorithm type, quality criteria and tool support.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Survey reference for the conformance-checking analogy in SpecDx's related work.
- **[aa2019extracting]** Han van der Aa, Claudio Di Ciccio, Henrik Leopold et al. (2019). *Extracting Declarative Process Models from Natural Language*. CAiSE (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A). [link](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-030-21290-2_23)
  - Relevance **low**; role: context
  - First automated approach to extract declarative (Declare) process constraints from natural-language constraint descriptions using tailored NLP, producing constraints close to those defined by humans.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Turns T into a clause set C automatically; such generated clauses are exactly where hidden requirements and weak checks arise.
- **[li2017verifas]** Yuliang Li, Alin Deutsch, Victor Vianu (2017). *VERIFAS: a practical verifier for artifact systems*. PVLDB (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A* (as VLDB)). [link](https://doi.org/10.14778/3157794.3157798)
  - Relevance **low**; role: component prior art; components: baseline B5
  - VERIFAS is a practical verifier for artifact systems (data-driven workflows) with unbounded data, checking linear-time temporal properties over real and synthetic workflows within seconds.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Shows that business workflows with databases can be verified symbolically; such verification presumes the properties are the right ones, which SpecDx does not.
- **[burattin2016conformance]** Andrea Burattin, Fabrizio M. Maggi, Alessandro Sperduti (2016). *Conformance checking based on multi-perspective declarative process models*. ESWA (peer-reviewed, CORE2020 B). [link](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.eswa.2016.08.040)
  - Relevance **low**; role: component prior art
  - Defines semantics and abstract syntax for Multi-Perspective Declare (constraints over control flow, data and time) and algorithms for conformance checking of logs against MP-Declare models, implemented in ProM.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* MP-Declare constraints with data conditions resemble SpecDx's clauses over database values at scenario steps; conformance results could be one input to SpecDx's cell model.
- **[ly2015compliance]** Linh Thao Ly, Fabrizio Maria Maggi, Marco Montali et al. (2015). *Compliance monitoring in business processes: Functionalities, application, and tool-support*. IS (peer-reviewed, CORE2020 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.is.2015.02.007)
  - Relevance **low**; role: survey
  - Systematically identifies compliance-monitoring functionalities for business processes (observing violations, fine-grained feedback, prediction) and compares existing approaches and tools against them.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Context on runtime compliance monitoring, the BPM counterpart of agent guardrails.
- **[calvanese2013foundations]** Diego Calvanese, Giuseppe De Giacomo, Marco Montali (2013). *Foundations of data-aware process analysis: a database theory perspective*. PODS (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.1145/2463664.2467796)
  - Relevance **low**; role: survey
  - Surveys database-theory research on data-aware business processes and argues that this community should provide the formal foundations for analysing processes and data together.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Theoretical background for verifying business specifications whose checks are database invariants, as in TonomyX.
- **[friedrich2011process]** Fabian Friedrich, Jan Mendling, Frank Puhlmann (2011). *Process Model Generation from Natural Language Text*. CAiSE (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A). [link](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-642-21640-4_36)
  - Relevance **low**; role: foundation
  - Generates BPMN process models automatically from natural-language process descriptions (summary from title and venue; the abstract was not accessible).
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Seminal text-to-model work; SpecDx uses LLM executors as many implicit text-to-behaviour translators.
- **[pesic2007declare]** Maja Pesic, Helen Schonenberg, Wil M.P. van der Aalst (2007). *DECLARE: Full Support for Loosely-Structured Processes*. EDOC (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 B). [link](https://doi.org/10.1109/edoc.2007.14)
  - Relevance **low**; role: foundation
  - Presents DECLARE, a prototype workflow system for loosely structured processes based on a constraint-based (LTL) declarative modelling language, which keeps features such as model verification and analysis of past executions.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Foundation of Declare clause languages; SpecDx's temporal guardrail clauses can be read as Declare-style constraints.

<a id="t9_agent_failure_attribution"></a>
## Agent failure attribution, evaluation reliability and specification gaming

- **[barres2026bench]** Victor Barres, Honghua Dong, Soham Ray et al. (2026). *$τ^2$-Bench: Evaluating Conversational Agents in a Dual-Control Environment*. ICML (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://proceedings.mlr.press/v306/barres26a.html) · [code](https://github.com/sierra-research/tau2-bench) ★2263
  - Relevance **high**; role: evaluation/benchmark; also in t2_irt_benchmark_audit, t8_bpm_agents_policies; components: real-defect benchmark, active synthesis
  - Introduces tau2-bench with a dual-control telecom domain (a Dec-POMDP where both user and agent act with tools), a compositional generator of verifiable tasks, a tool-constrained user simulator, and ablations that separate reasoning errors from communication and coordination errors.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Target benchmark family for SpecDx; its later repair (tau2-Bench-Verified, amazon-agi/tau2-bench-verified) supplies real specification defects. tau2-bench attributes failures to agent reasoning versus coordination, never to the task specification. | The specification family on which SpecDx's real defects are defined (τ²-Bench-Verified diff, the Policy Loopholes audit, COBA's findings); SpecDx diagnoses defects in its T (policies) and C (goal states and evaluation criteria). | Its airline and retail policies and task checks are SpecDx's second subject. The compositional task generator is a form of scenario synthesis, but it assumes the policy and checks are correct; SpecDx diagnoses defects in them (cf. the later τ²-Bench-Verified repairs).
- **[zhang2026agentracer]** Guibin Zhang, Junhao Wang, Junjie Chen et al. (2026). *AgenTracer: Who Is Inducing Failure in the LLM Agentic Systems?*. ICLR (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://proceedings.iclr.cc/paper_files/paper/2026/hash/134ed7a477770f227f12450ef0cbb8f4-Abstract-Conference.html)
  - Relevance **high**; role: component prior art; components: refutation
  - AgenTracer annotates failed multi-agent trajectories automatically by counterfactual replay (replace an agent action with a corrected one and re-simulate) and programmed fault injection, producing TracerTraj, and trains AgenTracer-8B with multi-granular RL. It beats large proprietary LLMs on Who&When by up to 18.18% and its feedback improves MetaGPT and MaAS by 4.8-14.2%.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Counterfactual replay is an intervention-based test of a blame hypothesis, close in spirit to SpecDx's refutation by witness; but it presumes the task is solvable and the checker correct, so the earliest flipping correction is always an agent step. SpecDx asks whether any behaviour can satisfy C at all.
- **[barke2026agentrx]** Shraddha Barke, Arnav Goyal, Alind Khare et al. (2026). *AgentRx: Diagnosing AI Agent Failures from Execution Trajectories*. arXiv (preprint). [link](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.02475) · [code](https://github.com/microsoft/agentrx) ★158 · **baseline candidate**
  - Relevance **high**; role: component prior art; components: defect taxonomy, baseline B1, baseline B2
  - Releases 170 manually annotated failed trajectories over 11 settings (structured API workflows, incident management, web/file tasks) with a critical step and a grounded-theory failure category, and AgentRx, which synthesises constraints, checks them step by step into an auditable log of violations with evidence, and lets an LLM judge localise the critical step; step localisation improves by 75% on average.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Synthesises checkable constraints from the task and policy and records clause violations per step, much like SpecDx's per-run violated-clause sets; but it uses one trajectory and assumes the constraints are right, whereas SpecDx treats the clause set as fallible and aggregates violations across a population.
- **[suh2026agentsuite]** Hyewon Suh, Binfei Ji, Seojune Lee et al. (2026). *AgentSuite: Toward More Reliable Agent Evaluation with a Component-Based Benchmark Auditing Pipeline*. ICML (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://proceedings.mlr.press/v306/suh26a.html) · [code](https://github.com/Agent-Suite/AgentSuite) ★3 · **baseline candidate**
  - Relevance **high**; role: direct competitor; also in t2_irt_benchmark_audit; components: defect taxonomy, baseline B1, real-defect benchmark, baseline B5
  - COBA decomposes each agent task into User, Environment, Ground Truth and Evaluation components and detects validity issues with hybrid rule-based detectors plus taxonomy-guided LLM evaluation. On six agent benchmarks it reaches F1 0.791-0.874 against experts, finds tau2-bench issues missed by manual verification, and ships in the AgentSuite platform with audited benchmark variants.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* The closest published competitor on SpecDx's own target (tau2-bench-style task specifications): automatic, component-localised flaw detection. It reads the artifacts with rules and an LLM rather than executing a population of interpreters, so it gives no satisfaction-based typing, no statistical separation from agent error, and no executed witnesses. | Peer-reviewed (ICML 2026) automatic auditing of the same τ²-bench defects SpecDx targets, so it is the strongest threat to the 'automatic' part of the novelty claim. It combines rules and LLM judgement over task artefacts; it does not execute the specification with a population of interpreters, does not separate defect from agent error statistically, and gives no MUS-based contradiction certificates or refutation witnesses.
- **[mohl2026automated]** Jeff Mohl, Nelson Gardner-Challis, Magda Dubois et al. (2026). *Automated Transcript Analysis for Detecting Flaws in Agentic Benchmarks*. arXiv (preprint). [link](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.27518) · [code](https://github.com/Generality-Labs/scanner_evaluation) ★2 · **baseline candidate**
  - Relevance **high**; role: direct competitor; components: baseline B1, real-defect benchmark
  - Builds LLM scanners over agent transcripts to detect four validity issues (ground-truth access, tool failure, guessing vulnerability, answer-format ambiguity), with human labelling rubrics, and evaluates them on held-out Inspect Evals benchmarks; the scanners found verified issues in five widely used benchmarks, though performance varied by benchmark, criterion and model.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Uses agent transcripts, as SpecDx does, to surface benchmark-side problems, but scans transcripts one at a time with an LLM for predefined issue types; SpecDx aggregates across a population and localises to specification cells and clauses.
- **[bhat2026benchmarking]** Vishvesh Bhat, Jay Vaghasiya, Muhammad Ahmed Mohsin et al. (2026). *Benchmarking the Benchmarks: A Validity Audit of Tool-Calling Evaluation*. arXiv (preprint). [link](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.02577)
  - Relevance **high**; role: evaluation/benchmark; also in t8_bpm_agents_policies; components: defect taxonomy, real-defect benchmark
  - Audits four tool-calling benchmark families (BFCL v4, tau2-Bench, LiveMCPBench, MCP-Atlas) on 496 expert-reviewed tasks and finds 18.5% evaluator-human misalignment: deterministic checkers show brittle state matching, trajectory lock-in, incorrect ground truths and reward-basis misalignment, while LLM-judge benchmarks show rubric drift and large run-to-run variance (scores from 57.9% to 76.8% across 23 repeats). Releases a taxonomy, corrected evaluation components and the Tool-Veritas benchmark.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Documents real hidden-requirement and weak-check defects in tau2-bench's golden-state checks, the class SpecDx targets, via expert review; SpecDx aims to find the same issues automatically from executor populations. | Provides human-audited real defects in τ²-Bench that can serve as SpecDx ground truth and a taxonomy to map onto contradiction/hidden requirement/ambiguity/weak check; its audit is manual, SpecDx's is automatic.
- **[shah2026causal]** Jaineet Shah (2026). *Causal Agent Replay: Counterfactual Attribution for LLM-Agent Failures*. arXiv (preprint). [link](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.08275) · [code](https://github.com/jaineet17/causal-agent-replay) ★14 · **baseline candidate**
  - Relevance **high**; role: component prior art; components: refutation, identifiability
  - Causal Agent Replay models an agent run as a structural causal model, applies do-interventions to steps and re-executes forward under the same stochastic policy to measure shifts in the outcome distribution; it provides a contrastive single-step estimator and a budgeted Monte-Carlo Shapley estimator with confidence intervals, validated on synthetic SCMs, and runs on hosted or local models.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Distributional, executed counterfactuals with confidence intervals are close to SpecDx's sampling-based reasoning; but interventions target agent steps, not specification clauses, so a defective check would be misread as a pivotal agent step.
- **[ma2026dover]** Ming Ma, Jue Zhang, Fangkai Yang et al. (2026). *DoVer: Intervention-Driven Auto Debugging for LLM Multi-Agent Systems*. ICLR (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://proceedings.iclr.cc/paper_files/paper/2026/hash/2f5fdce256ff0d7fb774da76e5e63209-Abstract-Conference.html)
  - Relevance **high**; role: component prior art; components: refutation
  - DoVer argues that log-only failure attribution yields untested hypotheses and that single-step attribution is often ill-posed because several distinct interventions can each repair a failure. It validates attribution hypotheses by targeted interventions (editing messages, altering plans) and re-execution, flipping 18-28% of failed GAIA/AssistantBench trials and validating or refuting 30-60% of hypotheses.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Shares SpecDx's stance that a diagnosis must be tested by execution (validate or refute), but intervenes on agent messages, not on the specification; its finding that blame is not unique echoes SpecDx's set-valued truth.
- **[zanoli2026elt]** Christopher Zanoli, Andrea Giovannini, Tengjun Jin et al. (2026). *ELT-Bench-Verified: Benchmark Quality Issues Underestimate AI Agent Capabilities*. arXiv (preprint). [link](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.29399) · [code](https://github.com/uiuc-kang-lab/ELT-Bench) ★31 · **baseline candidate**
  - Relevance **high**; role: direct competitor; also in t2_irt_benchmark_audit; components: real-defect benchmark, defect taxonomy, baseline B1
  - Revisits ELT-Bench (data-pipeline construction agents) and shows capabilities were underestimated: an Auditor-Corrector method combining LLM-driven root-cause analysis of failures with human validation (Fleiss' kappa 0.85) finds that most failed transformation tasks contain benchmark-attributable errors (rigid evaluation scripts, ambiguous specifications, incorrect ground truth); ELT-Bench-Verified fixes them.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* A data-engineering, SQL-flavoured benchmark where failures are attributed to the benchmark rather than the agent, exactly SpecDx's dichotomy, with defect classes resembling SpecDx's (ambiguity, mis-keyed ground truth, over-strict checks). Its attribution is per-failure LLM RCA plus humans, not population statistics with logical witnesses. | Shows the three defect families SpecDx types (hidden requirement/incorrect ground truth, ambiguity, over-strict checks) on a SQL/data benchmark close to SpecDx's PostgreSQL setting, and attributes failures to the specification rather than the agent. Its audit is an LLM auditor plus human validation per failed task; SpecDx's diagnosis is statistical over an executor population with logic-based refutation and needs no human validation step.
- **[zhong2026impossiblebench]** Ziqian Zhong, Aditi Raghunathan, Nicholas Carlini (2026). *ImpossibleBench: Measuring LLMs' Propensity of Exploiting Test Cases*. ICLR (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). - **[liu2025empirical]** Simiao Liu, Fang Liu, Liehao Li et al. (2025). *An Empirical Study on Failures in Automated Issue Solving*. arXiv (preprint). [link](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.13941)
  - Relevance **medium**; role: context; components: defect taxonomy
  - Compares three state-of-the-art pipeline and agentic issue-solving tools on SWE-bench Verified and manually analyses 150 failed instances, deriving a failure taxonomy with 3 phases, 9 categories and 25 subcategories of failure modes.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* An agent-side failure taxonomy for SE agents; failures are attributed to tool behaviour rather than to task or test defects.
- **[gabor2025evilgenie]** Jonathan Gabor, Jayson Lynch, Jonathan Rosenfeld (2025). *EvilGenie: A Reward Hacking Benchmark*. arXiv (preprint). [link](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.21654) · [code](https://github.com/JonathanGabor/evilgenie_inspect) ★7
  - Relevance **medium**; role: evaluation/benchmark; components: defect taxonomy
  - EvilGenie is a reward-hacking benchmark built from LiveCodeBench problems in an environment where agents can easily hack (hardcode test cases, edit test files); hacking is measured with held-out tests, LLM judges and test-edit detection, and explicit reward hacking is observed in Codex and Claude Code.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Shows that agents exploit checks when the task is hard; in SpecDx such exploitation would make a weak or contradictory check look satisfied, so gaming behaviour must be separated from faithful readings.
- **[bouzenia2025understanding]** Islem Bouzenia, Michael Pradel (2025). *Understanding Software Engineering Agents: A Study of Thought-Action-Result Trajectories*. ASE (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.1109/ase63991.2025.00234)
  - Relevance **medium**; role: context
  - Large-scale empirical study of thought-action-result trajectories of RepairAgent, AutoCodeRover and OpenHands (120 trajectories, 2,822 LLM interactions) on program repair and issue resolution, combining quantitative analysis of action patterns and token use with qualitative assessment of reasoning coherence and feedback use, to characterise failure behaviour.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Characterises agent-side failure behaviour of SE agents; it does not consider whether the issue or its tests are defective.
- **[banerjee2025where]** Adi Banerjee, Anirudh Nair, Tarik Borogovac (2025). *Where Did It All Go Wrong? A Hierarchical Look into Multi-Agent Error Attribution*. arXiv (preprint). [link](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.04886)
  - Relevance **medium**; role: component prior art; components: baseline B1
  - ECHO improves agent/step error attribution in multi-agent traces over all-at-once, step-by-step and binary-search baselines by combining a hierarchical, position-based context representation, objective analysis by several specialised judge agents, and confidence-weighted consensus voting.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Uses a committee of LLM analysts with consensus, like SpecDx's population, but the committee judges one trace for agent blame; SpecDx's committee members are executors whose disagreement is the evidence about the specification.
- **[zhu2025where]** Kunlun Zhu, Zijia Liu, Bingxuan Li et al. (2025). *Where LLM Agents Fail and How They can Learn From Failures*. arXiv (preprint). [link](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.25370)
  - Relevance **medium**; role: component prior art; components: defect taxonomy
  - Introduces AgentErrorTaxonomy (memory, reflection, planning, action and system-level failure modes), AgentErrorBench with annotated failed trajectories from ALFWorld, GAIA and WebShop, and AgentDebug, which isolates the root-cause error and gives corrective feedback, improving all-correct accuracy by 24% and task success by up to 26% relative.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* A modular agent-error taxonomy for single-agent trajectories; the 'system-level' category is the nearest it comes to environment or task problems, and specification defects are out of scope.
- **[qi2026beyond]** Shihao Qi, Jie Ma, Rui Xing et al. (2026). *Beyond Individual Intelligence: Surveying Collaboration, Failure Attribution, and Self-Evolution in LLM-based Multi-Agent Systems*. arXiv (preprint). [link](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.14892)
  - Relevance **low**; role: survey
  - Survey of LLM multi-agent systems organised as the LIFE progression (capability foundation, collaboration, failure attribution, self-evolution), emphasising how errors propagate across agents and rounds and how attribution should feed self-improvement.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Covers the failure-attribution literature as a stage of MAS development; it frames failures as agent or coordination errors.
- **[shah2026characterizing]** Mehil B Shah, Mohammad Mehdi Morovati, Mohammad Masudur Rahman et al. (2026). *Characterizing Faults in Agentic AI: A Taxonomy of Types, Symptoms, and Root Causes*. arXiv (preprint). [link](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.06847)
  - Relevance **low**; role: context; components: defect taxonomy
  - Empirical study of 385 faults sampled from 13,602 issues and pull requests in 40 agentic-AI repositories; grounded theory yields taxonomies of fault types, symptoms and root causes (34 fault types), linked by association-rule mining and validated with 145 practitioners.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Software-engineering fault taxonomy of agent frameworks (implementation faults), distinct from specification defects in the tasks agents execute.
- **[zhao2026failure]** Xiangxin Zhao, Han Li, Shuaiting Li et al. (2026). *Failure as a Process: An Anatomy of CLI Coding Agent Trajectories*. arXiv (preprint). [link](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.09510)
  - Relevance **low**; role: context
  - Studies failure as a temporal process (onset, evolution, recovery) over 1,794 manually annotated Terminal-Bench trajectories from seven frontier models and three scaffolds, deriving 14 findings on how CLI coding-agent failures emerge and become unrecoverable.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Agent-side process view of failure; complementary context only.
- **[wang2026thinking]** Xinpeng Wang, Nitish Joshi, Barbara Plank et al. (2026). *Is it Thinking or Cheating? Detecting Implicit Reward Hacking by Measuring Reasoning Effort*. ICLR (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://proceedings.iclr.cc/paper_files/paper/2026/hash/9d0ee47d732bd526c4a03d4aecc39bc1-Abstract-Conference.html)
  - Relevance **low**; role: context
  - TRACE detects implicit reward hacking by measuring reasoning effort: it truncates a model's chain of thought at increasing lengths and measures how early the reward is already obtained, since exploiting a loophole needs less effort than solving the task.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* A detector of check exploitation on the agent side; relevant to SpecDx only as a way to discount gaming runs before inferring specification defects.
- **[sun2026scope]** Kai Sun, Wenqiang Li, Bo Dong et al. (2026). *Scope Delineation Before Localization: A Two-Stage Framework for Enhancing Failure Attribution in Multi-Agent Systems*. AAAI (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A* (as AAAI)). [link](https://doi.org/10.1609/aaai.v40i39.40594)
  - Relevance **low**; role: context
  - Proposes Scope Delineation Before Localization, a two-stage failure attribution framework that first identifies the failure scope (with stepwise or expertise-assisted strategies) and then pinpoints the failure step, improving Who&When step-level accuracy by up to 24.27%.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Peer-reviewed Who&When follow-up; agent-side attribution only.
- **[zhu2026stepfinder]** Taiyu Zhu, Yifan Wu, Weilin Jin et al. (2026). *StepFinder: A Temporal Semantic Framework for Failure Attribution in Multi-Agent Systems*. KDD (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.1145/3770855.3817991) · [code](https://github.com/taiyu-zhu/StepFinder) ★7
  - Relevance **low**; role: context
  - StepFinder is a lightweight failure-attribution framework that uses LLMs only to extract temporal semantic features of execution steps and then locates the root-cause step without full-trajectory LLM reasoning, reducing cost and noise from verbose logs.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Peer-reviewed representative of efficient agent-step attribution; not concerned with specification defects.
- **[li2026self]** Jiazheng Li, Emine Yilmaz, Bei Chen et al. (2026). *Towards Self-Improving Error Diagnosis in Multi-Agent Systems*. ACL Findings (peer-reviewed, CORE2023 A*). [link](https://doi.org/10.18653/v1/2026.findings-acl.98)
  - Relevance **low**; role: context; components: defect taxonomy
  - ErrorProbe is a self-improving semantic failure-attribution framework that operationalises the MAS failure taxonomy to detect local anomalies, traces symptoms backward to prune context, and identifies the responsible agent and originating error step.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Uses a failure taxonomy (MAST-like) operationally; agent-side only.
- **[majgaonkar2025understanding]** Oorja Majgaonkar, Zhiwei Fei, Xiang Li et al. (2025). *Understanding Code Agent Behaviour: An Empirical Study of Success and Failure Trajectories*. arXiv (preprint). [link](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.00197)
  - Relevance **low**; role: context
  - Analyses successful and failed SWE-bench trajectories of OpenHands, SWE-agent and Prometheus, finding that strategies such as defensive programming and context gathering drive success and that failed trajectories are longer and more variable, with failure patterns differing by agent.
  - *Relation to SpecDx:* Observes that failure patterns are agent-specific, which is the 'scattered, interpreter-specific' signature SpecDx uses to recognise agent errors; no task-defect analysis.
